# Changelog

All notable changes to the Starflight Unity port are documented in this file.

The format is based on [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.0.0/),
and this project adheres to [Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org/spec/v2.0.0.html).

## [Unreleased]

### Added

- **Known Fluxes on the Starmap**: the port remembered no continuum flux, and its starmap showed none, even one the ship had just been through. In the original a flux the ship goes through is known from then on and kept in the save (HYPER-OV `>FLUX-NODE`, `SET-FLUX-STATE`), and the starmap draws every known flux as a grey line between its two ends (MAP-OV `WEA70`, `WEA4C`). The port now does the same: `PD_General.m_knownFluxList` (both directions of a flux; older saves start with none), and the starmap draws the lines in a copy of its grid's material, in the colour scheme's secondary text colour. Roadmap 8.12. New probe scenario `fluxmap`, with a picture of the starmap.

### Changed

- **Music Made for the Port**: the game's music was fifteen ambiences by Tabletop Audio, under a licence that forbids commercial use and changes, eight of them played and seven never; the intro played a cut-down copy of one. All fifteen are gone, and eight tracks made for this port take their places: a modern science fiction score, dark and grounded, in low strings, deep synth bass, sparse woodwinds and metallic percussion. A 40 second title theme plays over the intro's credit pages, and the Starport, the docking bay, star systems, orbit, hyperspace, encounters and planet surfaces each have a loop of their own, from about 2 to 6 minutes, that starts after its opening swell, ends before its fade and is crossfaded into itself, so it repeats without a seam or a dip. The loops are at one loudness (the old ones were up to 14 dB apart, the Starport's the quietest), and every track now streams from disk in place of being unpacked into memory when the game loads, where each old 10 minute track held about 100 MB once unpacked; the files take 38 MB in place of 200. The README and the About screen say the music was made for this port. New probe scenario `music`; `aboutscreen` reads the new credit.
- **The Crystal Planet's Heat as the Original's**: in orbit around the Crystal Planet without the Crystal Orb the ship took 5 points of damage a second through its shields and armor (the port's own numbers), and could land and win without the orb. The original (HYPERMSG-OV `OSET`, `WED4B`, `HEAT`, `HCASE`) says "Captain, the ship is beginning to vibrate!" and the armor is gone; then on every tick a random system loses 3 points, the hull warns and is halved at the 70th, 140th and 210th tick ("The hull temperature is increasing!", "...is glowing red!", "...is melting!"), and at the 280th the ship melts and the game is over. Land answers "Computer not responding!" (LAND-OV `?LAND`) and a distress call is jammed (COMM-OV `WEFC3`). With the orb aboard, "The Crystal Orb is glowing." and none of this happens. Leaving orbit ends the heat, and the count starts again on the next visit. A tick is one pass of the original's main loop, taken as the port's measured pass of a quarter of a second (INFERRED: the original's speed depends on the machine), so the ship melts 70 seconds after it starts to vibrate. Roadmap 8.1. Probe scenario `crystalfield`, rewritten for the original's rules.
- **Disembark Lowers the Shields and Disarms**: the terrain vehicle went out with the ship's shields up and its weapons armed as they were. The original's Disembark lowers the shields and disarms the weapons first (SHIPBUTTONS `WF095` calls `WEDEC`), as docking does; the port now does the same, saying "Shields are down." and "Weapons are disarmed." before "Standby...scanning planet.", and the shields keep their charge. Roadmap 8.35. Probe scenario `disembarkshields`.
- **Arming the Black Egg Is the Crew's Choice**: a Black Egg dropped from the terrain vehicle was armed at once. The original asks "ARM THIS DEVICE? NO [  ] YES" on the drop (ITEMS-OV `WE9AA`) and says "DISARMING DEVICE" when an armed egg is picked up again (`WE9FA`). The port now does the same: the drop asks "Arm this device?" with No and Yes, an egg left unarmed lies there doing nothing until it is picked up, and picking up an armed one says "Disarming device.". An egg dropped in a save from before keeps counting as armed. Roadmap 8.31. Probe scenario `eggarming`; `blackegg`, `tvtakesegg` and the playthrough answer Yes.
- **Describe from the Ship's Cargo List**: the ship's Cargo button showed the hold as a list and nothing more. In the original the list has a cursor and its items can be described (CAPTAIN `1STOPTION`): an artifact by its Starport analysis, or "THIS ARTIFACT HAS NOT BEEN ANALYZED" (`WEA62`), a specimen by the system, planet, location and date of its capture (`WEC73`); an element has no description. The port's list now marks an item, with Next, Describe and Back on the console, and a captured specimen keeps where and when it was taken (a specimen from an older save shows its system and planet). Roadmap 8.33. Probe scenario `cargodescribe`; `shipcargo` reads the list without the marker.
- **The Flat Device Turns Away Nine Attacks in Ten**: with the Flat Device aboard, a lifeform hit half as often as without it, the port's reading of the Starport's "difficult for dangerous lifeforms to attack you". The original multiplies the lifeform's hit roll of 0 to 99 by 10 when the terrain vehicle has the device (BEHAV-OV `WE8C2`, `?FLAT`), so it hits about a tenth as often; the port now does the same. Roadmap 8.28. Probe scenario `flatdevice` now expects 5 to 40 of 200 attacks to hurt, since an attack strikes up to three times (32 in the run; about 100 before).
- **Sound Effects Made for the Port**: most of the game's sound effects came from the 2017 remake with no traceable source (two were cut from a video found online, one may come from a commercial sound library), two laser shots and an explosion were freesound.org downloads under their own licences, and the sounds made for 1.4.0 were in a different, early 1980s electronic style. All 43 are now made for this port in one grounded, industrial style, as if heard from inside a working spacecraft: metal hatches with servos and latches for the Starport's panels, magnetic boots on deck plates, hydraulic bay doors, air venting from the bay, a drive that rumbles through the hull at launch and landing, soft console chirps for clicks and confirmations, comm channels broken up by digital noise, and concussive explosions. Each is set to the loudness of the clip it replaces; the long ones follow their animations (the countdown's five ticks fall on its numbers, the air vents 3 seconds into a launch, the landing is joined where the view switches to the planet), and the loops repeat without a seam. Error and Launch are WAV files now, the freesound clips' places hold the terrain vehicle's laser and the surface explosion, and two old warp sounds nothing played are gone. The README and the About screen say the sound effects were made for this port, in place of the freesound credits. New probe scenario `soundeffects`; `aboutscreen` reads the new credit.
- **Jettison from the Ship's Hold**: nothing could be thrown out of the ship's hold. The original's cargo list offers JETTISON (CAPTAIN `WECDF`: the Trade Depot's sale with no depot, so nothing is paid): an artifact or a specimen goes at once, and for an element it asks "JETTISON HOW MANY CUBIC METERS? ENTER VOLUME (0-20.0)" (TDEPOT-OV `KEY-ELEM-AMT`). The ship's cargo list now has Jettison: the number keys type the volume as in the original, and the stick steps it, up and down a cubic meter and left and right a tenth (the port's addition for the console); fire or Enter jettisons it, and 0 jettisons nothing. Roadmap 8.17. Probe scenario `jettison`.
- **No Story Locations in the Roadmap**: `ROADMAP.md` still gave where the artifacts lie (planets, coordinates and sites in its Phase 4 table), the coordinates of the Crystal Planet and the Uhlek brain world, the place on the Crystal Planet that wins the game, and the names that tie the Rod Device and the Red Cylinder to their planets. They are gone, and the Phase 4 table says each artifact is found, bought at the Starport or traded; what each item does and how it was built is unchanged.
- **A Terrain Vehicle Out of Fuel Is Lost**: a terrain vehicle that ran out of fuel left its cargo on the ground, and the crew walked back to the ship, where the vehicle was waiting refuelled. In the original the vehicle is lost: it stays where it stopped as a wreck (MOVE-OV `WEF48`), and back at the Starport a new one is installed and the crew is fined 10,000 MU for its loss (OP-OV `WF045`). The port now does the same, as for a vehicle lost in lava: the wreck lies where the vehicle stopped, looks like it, is labelled by Scan and described by Look, and is far too big for the cargo hold; Disembark finds no terrain vehicle until the Starport installs a new one. Roadmap 8.14. Probe scenario `outoffuel` now checks the loss, the wreck and the fine.
- **Look Describes the Land and the Region's Temperature**: Look gave the planet's surface type ("Rock") and its whole temperature range. The original describes the land in its colours (PLSET-OV `(TERRAIN`, MAPS-OV `.SURFACE`): grey, yellowish, reddish, brownish or black rock and dirt, or a sand, under an atmosphere, cratered rock and dust without one, coloured oceans and their shores, cracked plains and hills of coloured snow and ice, volcanic rock and flowing lava; and it gives the temperature of the region the terrain vehicle is in, from the planet's coldest at the poles to its hottest at the equator, in the original's words (Subfreezing to Furnace, `WEDB2`). The port now does the same; a planet's colours are drawn from its id, so it looks the same on every visit (the original draws them when the ship lands). Not yet: the carpet lifeform the original adds to the description, which comes with the shipped game's lifeforms (roadmap 8.18). Roadmap 8.30. Probe scenario `lookland`.
- **A Deposit's Mineral by the Original's Shares**: a planet's deposits took its three elements in turn, a third each. The original gives a deposit the planet's first element half the time, its second a quarter, its third 15%, and any other element but Endurium the rest (SEED-OV `WF0F7`, `WF0C7`; Endurium itself when it is the planet's first). The port now does the same, with a generator of its own seeded from the planet and the deposit, so the deposits lie where they did and hold as much as before, and the survey from orbit names the same elements as the ground. A deposit partly mined in an older save may now hold another element. Roadmap 8.16. Probe scenario `depositminerals`, whose fingerprint of the deposits' places and volumes is the same before and after; `deposits` now checks the places alone.
- **The Red Cylinder Flashes the Planet's Sites**: the Red Cylinder flashed on the landing map while the crosshair was near one of the planet's ancient ruins, the port's own reading of the shipped game's "THE RED CYLINDER IS FLASHING". The shipped game's landing map, when it opens with the Red Cylinder aboard, flashes the planet's message and artifact sites on the map, up to ten, and says so (SITE-OV `.MERCATOR`, `WF389`, `WF290`). The port now does the same: the sites blink over the landing map for a few seconds (messages placed at random spots are left out, since the port draws those on the surface), and the ancient ruins stay marked as the survey's marks are. Roadmap 8.15. Probe scenario `redcylinder` now checks the sites.
- **The Veloxi Drone's Numbers as the Original's**: a Veloxi drone asked three numbers, about half of them multiples of 6, 2 to 4 seconds apart, waited for each answer for as long as it took, and put each answer through the comm's equipment check. The original asks six numbers (COMMSPEC-OV `VPROBE#`), each drawn from 1 to 99 with all equally likely, so only 16 in 99 are multiples of 6; the first comes half a second after the comm link is up and each next a moment after an answer; the player has 15 seconds to answer, and no answer is a wrong one; and the answers, given on the drone's own yes and no buttons, need no working comm. The port does the same. New probe scenario `dronenumbers`; `drones` answers six numbers (roadmap 8.36).
- **A Planet's Minerals, Lifeforms and Curios Come Back After the Ship Leaves**: what the terrain vehicle took from a planet stayed taken for good, and a lifeform killed or captured was back on the vehicle's next run. The original keeps both as they are while the ship stays in orbit, and when the ship leaves the orbit of a planet it has landed on it deletes the planet's elements, lifeforms and curios, seeding them again the next time (HYPER-OV `WDB32` runs DPART-OV `DEPART`, `WF166`, `WF0D6`; MOVE-OV `?POPULA`). The port now does the same: the Disembark button marks the planet visited (`PD_PlanetSurfaces.VisitPlanet`), a lifeform killed, captured or eaten stays so on later runs in the orbit (`LoseLifeform`, by its place in the seeded order), and leaving the orbit (`SpaceflightController.SwitchLocation`) gives the visited planets back their deposits, lifeforms and the curios of their random ruins, and deletes the minerals and curios dropped there (`RefillVisitedPlanets`). Special artifacts, the artifact sites, the Endurium of STRINFO's ancient ruin sites, the wreck of a terrain vehicle and a Black Egg (until the game is won) stay as they are. Roadmap 8.19, replacing item 3.0. Probe scenario `planetrefill`.
- **The Officer's Translation as the Original's**: how much of what aliens said came through compared each word's difficulty with the communications officer's skill and the comm system's health, and a crew member of the aliens' race made every word understood. The original works out a translation strength (COMM-OV `INIT-MCOMM`): the square root of the officer's skill, plus 50 for a crew member of the aliens' race aboard and 50 more if the officer is one (only the Elowan, Thrynn and Veloxi can serve), plus 250 for the Spemin with the Whining Orb aboard, times 167 / 10, at most 250; a word is garbled when its own value, 0 to 249, is over the strength (`?SCRAMBLE`). An officer of skill 100 now understands about two words in three and one of 225 or more every word, and a damaged comm system garbles no more (its equipment check fails it instead). The port does the same (`Encounter.GetTranslationStrength`). New probe scenario `translation` (roadmap 8.9).
- **Aliens Move and Fire by Their Posture**: every alien ship flew toward the player, aliens fired only when their stance was hostile, from one ship at a time on a timer of 2 to 5 seconds and only within 5 cells, a hostile mood was talk with no attack, and an encounter ended when the player was 4096 units from its centre. In the original each pass of the main loop is one icon's turn (COMBAT-OV `COMBAT`), and an alien ship's turn goes through eleven combat rules (`<COMBAT>`, tried in an order that the rules that fire move up, kept from one encounter to the next): aliens that fight open fire at once, hostile ones with a chance of 2 in 99 a turn, and once they have opened fire each ship fires by its vessel's fire delay, a missile at any range or the laser within 5 cells; hostile aliens close in to 11 cells (3 for those with only a laser); once the comm link is over the others move away, grovelling aliens of a race that does not talk flee, and with no shot for 2 minutes they leave; otherwise a ship wanders, a cell a turn (a diagonal step is a cell each way, in the same turn). They arrive about 21 cells ahead, later ships 15 cells round the player's, and the encounter ends when every ship, wrecks included, is more than 22 cells away (HYPER-OV `?UNNEST`), not while the Combat menu is up. The port now does all this (`Encounter.UpdateCombatTurns`, `AlienShipTurn`, `CombatRuleHolds`; `CombatController.AlienLaunchesMissile` and `AlienFiresLaser`; the turn, the fire latch, the time since the aliens fired and the end of the comm link saved with the encounter, the rules' order with the game). Roadmap 8.3 and 8.4. New probe scenario `alienpostures`; `encounterspeeds` now reads the aliens' turns, and measures the player's speeds in the Combat menu.
- **The Starport Asks Before Repairing**: Ship Configuration's Repair repaired the ship at once and said "Repairs complete". The original asks first (CONFIG-OV `WF346`, `WF2C5`): it shows the cost to repair the entire ship and "Shall we repair the ship? [N Y]", answered with the stick, right for yes and left for no, and the repair is all or nothing; a whole ship gets "No repairs are needed", and without the money it shows the cost and "Insufficient funds" and asks nothing. The port now does the same (`ShipConfigurationPanel.RepairClicked`, `AnswerRepairQuestion`); Y and N answer too. Roadmap 8.34. New probe scenario `starport-repairquestion`; `starport-repair` and `starport-armorequivalency` answer yes.
- **A Flux Shows as Dots by the Navigator's Skill**: a navigator above skill 150, or the Ring Device, showed every flux as the same swirl of particles. The original draws a flux as grey dots (HYPER-OV `.FLUX-ICON`): (skill - 150) * 6 / 100 + 1 of them, 1 at 151 to 7 at 250, and 16 with the Ring Device whatever the skill, each a random point of a small ellipse round the flux (`RNDCPT`: up to 120 of the port's units across and 117 along), placed afresh at every step of the ship so that they twinkle. The port now does the same (`Hyperspace.GetFluxDotCount`, `PlaceFluxDots`), with numbers of its own so that the game's random numbers are not touched. Roadmap 8.20. New probe scenario `fluxdots`.
- **A Star System's Aliens Are Placed Afresh on Each Visit**: a star system's alien groups were placed once, at a new game, and then stayed where they had been, or where the ship last met them. The original places them afresh each time the ship comes into the system from hyperspace (HYPER-OV `SYSSETUP`: `SETPLNXY` gives each alien group of the system `SETRNDXY`, each axis a random 395 of the system's 400 units either way), and not when the ship comes back from orbit, an encounter or the Starport. The port now does the same (`SpaceflightController.PlaceStarSystemEncounters`, `PD_Encounter.PlaceAtRandomInStarSystem`); a place in the original's square system that is outside the port's round one is brought in to its edge. Roadmap 8.22. New probe scenario `systemgroups`.
- **The Old Empire and the Ancients Lock Out**: aliens answered every question about the Old Empire and the Ancients with their next line on it, and once out of lines said they knew no more and started over. The original locks such a subject out: each question about either has a chance of 20 in 101 to shut it off (COMM-OV `?LOCKED-OUT`, RRND 0 to 101 under 20), and from then on the aliens answer it with a line of having no more to say, until the next encounter clears the lock (`INIT_GCOMM`). The port does the same (`Encounter.IsLockedOut`, saved in `PD_Encounter.m_lockedOutSubjects`). New probe scenario `lockout` (roadmap 8.25).

### Fixed

- **The Crystal Orb Goes Back to Its Site**: a Crystal Orb handed to the Veloxi was gone for good. In the original the Veloxi's demand (COMMSPEC-OV `CRYSTAL-ORB`, `-ARTIFACT`, `WEB3F`) puts the orb back into its region of its planet, so it can be found again, unless that planet has been destroyed; anything else they take is gone. The port now does the same (`Encounter.ReturnCrystalOrbToItsSite`, `PD_PlanetSurfaces.ReturnArtifactSite`). Roadmap 8.2. New probe scenario `orbreturns`.
- **The Crystal Pearl Draws the Aliens, Not the Dodecahedron**: with the Dodecahedron aboard the aliens came four fifths of the way toward the ship at each move instead of half (six sevenths in a star system instead of three quarters) and were not held near their home ground. That rule is the shipped game's, but it belongs to the Crystal Pearl: its flag `?12` is set when artifact 13, the Crystal Pearl, is in the hold (HYPERMSG-OV `(!'EXT)`, COMMSPEC-OV `WEA50`), and no word of the shipped game looks for the Dodecahedron. The pursuit now follows the Crystal Pearl (`SpaceflightController.UpdateEncounters`). Roadmap 8.11. The probe scenario `dodecahedron` is now `pearlchase`: with the Dodecahedron the aliens stay put, with the Crystal Pearl they come 4 coordinates nearer.
- **The Edge of Hyperspace Holds the Ship**: the ship could fly off the map in hyperspace, past coordinates 0 and 249 across and 0 and 219 up, where nothing is. The original keeps each axis within the map on every step (HYPER-OV `FLY`, `WEE53`), with no message and the heading kept, so a ship stops at the edge and one flying into it at a slant slides along it; its engines still burn fuel there. The port now does the same (`PlayerShip.Update`). A place off the map (a ship leaving a star system or a flux at the edge) is not moved, as in the original: the next step brings the ship back. Roadmap 8.21. New probe scenario `hyperspaceedge`.
- **Each Race Remembers How It Was Treated**: every encounter with a race started from the race's fixed mood, however the last one had ended. In the original the end of every encounter moves the race's base mood toward the mood the encounter ended in, a third of the way and at least 5, and 5 toward calm when the two are the same, kept within 0 to 100 (HYPER-OV `ENCCLEANUP`, `BASE-EDL`), and the next encounter starts from it: a race the crew fought stays angry, one it treated well grows friendlier. The port now does the same (`PD_General.m_raceMoodList`, saved; older saves start every race at its old mood). Roadmap 8.5. New probe scenario `racememory`.

## [1.4.0] - 2026-10-18

New looks, new sounds and more of the original's rules. The last seven ships with simple models get textured ones, so every vessel of the game has one (#480), and so do the terrain vehicle, the ancient ruins, the rocks and the mineral deposits (#470 to #472); the special artifacts hover beside their ruins and turn in a window on the Trade Depot's lists and the terrain vehicle's cargo list (#473, #474, #477). The terrain vehicle's tools have sounds of their own, and the combat and flight sounds are new ones made for this port in place of clips from Star Trek: The Next Generation (#489, #490). A damaged system can malfunction when it is used, as the original's equipment check has it (#486), the shields regain their charge on the ship's turns in a fight (#481), the science officer can be unsure of a star's state (#483), and the ship's Cargo list shows everything in the hold (#478). The intro credits this port, and the Escape panel has an About screen with the full credits (#492).

### Added

- **Artifacts on the Ground**: the 15 special artifacts had no models: the game knew them only by name, as the original did, and a ruin looked the same with its artifact as without it. Each now has a textured model of its own, drawn from its name and the science officer's analysis (the cracked Black Egg, the Tesseract's cube in its frame, the Hypercube's chain of cubes, the jewelled Dodecahedron, the rest), and it shows beside the ruin that holds it, hovering over the ground, rising and sinking a little and turning slowly under a soft light, until the terrain vehicle takes it. The curios have no models and show nothing, as before. Probe scenario `artifactmodels`.
- **Artifacts in the Trade Depot**: on the Trade Depot's Buy, Sell and Analyze lists a selected special artifact turns slowly in a window beside the list, in the item column's free room right of the names, so you can see what you are buying, selling or having analysed. The window is a small 3D view, dark in every colour scheme and mode, and shows nothing for an element, a curio or a specimen. Probe scenario `starport-artifactview`.
- **Artifacts in the Terrain Vehicle's Cargo List**: with the terrain vehicle's cargo list open, a special artifact marked with Next turns in a small window under the list on the cargo display, the same window as the Trade Depot's. It is sized to the room the list leaves and is not shown when there is too little; an element or a curio shows nothing. Probe scenario `artifactcargo`.
- **Sounds for the Ground Tools**: the terrain vehicle's tools have sounds of their own. The mining laser hums and crackles as it cuts (it played the scanner's sound), a deposit crumbles when the laser has cut the last of it, a capture plays the stasis beam's pulsing hum for as long as the beam holds the lifeform (there was only a click), and a floating artifact hums softly beside its ruin, louder the nearer the vehicle comes and silent beyond about 40 units. The three hums loop seamlessly and pause with the game. Probe scenarios `groundsounds` and `artifacthum`.
- **Flight Check in Windows Sandbox**: `DevTools/WindowsSandbox/flight-check.ps1` checks a build's flight with nobody at the keyboard. For each engine class it starts the build in Windows Sandbox from a copy of a save with a crew, put in Arth's star system with those engines, presses Navigation, Maneuver and the right arrow as a player would, and times the ship to the system's edge from the game's own log, against what the speed rules give; a last run takes screenshots of a turn from east to north. The 1.3.0 build gave 14.51, 6.89 and 4.50 s for engine classes 1, 3 and 5, against 14.49, 6.86 and 4.45 s.
- **Damaged Equipment Malfunctions**: a damaged system always worked, only as well as its health, and the sensors refused below a quarter of theirs (a rule of the port's). In the original every use of a system goes through an equipment check (MISC-OV `(?.EQ`): a damaged system malfunctions with a chance of its damage in percent (never when whole, always at 0), flashes MALFUNCTION, and is then inoperative until it is repaired. The port now does the same, with the original's checks: the sensors before a scan, the comm before a hail, a reply and each of the player's phrases in a comm link (a failure there ends the link), the engines before Maneuver, Land, Descend and a launch from a planet (not from the Starport), the shield generator before the shields go up, and each weapon before it is armed (one that fails is not armed, the other still is). Engines shot down to nothing break down at once (DAMAGE-OV). A breakdown is saved; the engineer's repair ends it when the system is whole, and past 73% each repaired point has a chance of 1 in 5 to end it sooner, with "Item operational." (REPAIR-OV); the Starport's repairs end every one. The damage report's "Current functionality" line now reads the breakdown, as the original's does. Probe scenario `malfunctions`: the old code passes only its control (1 of 12 checks), the new code all 12; `systemeffects` and `repairchoice` read the new rule.
- **Phase 8 of the Roadmap**: the shipped game's own code (the decompiled overlays) was read against the port in four parts, flight and fluxes, encounters and comms, planets and the surface, and the Starport, crew and ship, and 39 differences turned up that the research sources had not shown. 37 are to be built as the shipped game does them, in the order of `ROADMAP.md`'s new Phase 8: first the Crystal Planet without the Crystal Orb and the Orb going back to its site, then how aliens move, fire and remember, then the rest. Decision D9 records the rulings, including the earlier calls they replace (D3's lifeforms, item 3.0's taken deposits, item 3.5's random ruins, the translation rule, the Veloxi drone, the Shimmering Ball, the Crystal Pearl and the Crystal Planet's damage rate). The README's completion figure counts the new items.
- **Probe README**: the probe's README had no row for the `malfunctions` scenario and still counted 271 scenarios; it now has the row and counts 272.

### Changed

- **The Port's Own Credits, and an About Screen**: the intro's third page credited the 2017 Starflight Remake Project, its team and BraveArmy.com. It now credits this port: its name, its programmer and project lead (@cptjamestiberiouskirk-pixel) and the GitHub account, and says where the full credits are. The Escape panel has a new About screen with them: who made the game; "Built on the Starflight Remake Project by Marvin Herbold, released into the public domain."; thanks to the remake's Jeff Graw, Sebastian Macke, Michael Gams, Daniel Blakeley, Stephen A Rogers, Aaron Diggens and the Planet Map Project, whose planet maps the game still uses; Starflight's makers, Binary Systems and Electronic Arts; and the credits the licences of the music, the sound effects and the fonts ask for. The original game's credit pages stay as they were. The README's closing line says the same. New probe scenario `aboutscreen`.
- **Textured Model for the Terrain Vehicle**: the terrain vehicle is a new textured model in the look of the Arth ship that carries it: light grey and dull white plating with navy trim, the gold front cowl with its headlights, the twin laser on the nose, a rear door with a blue window, and six chunky tires on their own suspension arms. It keeps the old model's layout and every part the game moves: the six wheels still turn and steer, and the radar on the roof still turns on its mast. The wheels meet the ground where the old ones did, and the body now clears it. The vehicle is shorter than before (about 13 units long against 20) and a little wider, so its collision capsule was resized to match. It has 11,900 triangles and its wheels 4,200 each, where each old wheel had 10,700. Probe scenario `tvmodel`.
- **No Story Locations in the Docs**: ten rows of the headless probe's scenario table (`artifactmodels`, `ruins`, `artifactsites`, `formations`, `blackegg`, `crystalcone`, `ancientsites`, `homeworlds`, `colonyrating` and `transmissions`) and one of its notes, fifteen entries of this changelog and two decisions in `ROADMAP.md` gave the places of story sites: coordinates, star numbers, and which planet holds an artifact. They now say what was done and why without saying where; the game and the scenarios are unchanged.
- **Textured Models for the Ancient Ruins**: the five ancient ruins, plain grey pyramids of about 60 triangles drawn in Unity's default material, are new textured models: the crumbling remains of pyramids of huge dark stone blocks, with fallen blocks at their feet and carved glyph lines along their faces that glow a faint cyan. Each keeps its old ruin's square of 24 units, its broken shape and its place on the ground. They have about 8,000 triangles each. Probe scenario `ruinmodels`.
- **Textured Models for the Last Seven Ships**: the Spemin transport, scout and warship, the Mechan scout and the Mysterion were simple hand-built models, and the Nomad probe and the Minstrel flew as their comm screen models. All seven are now textured models built from the original's sensor pictures, so every vessel of the game has one, sized by STRINFO as the others: the Spemin ships are lumpy pods of grey-green jelly joined by strands, lit blue and cyan with a red core (the warship a ring of seven pods round its core); the Mechan scout a gunmetal machine with two long rails lined with red lights; the Mysterion a huge cage of dark bronze ribs round a core of countless coloured lights; the Nomad probe an old, pitted machine with a crescent antenna at its bow; and the Minstrel a pearly living creature with a glowing heart and two arms curling round it like a spiral galaxy. Each leaves a wreck of its own. The comm screens keep their portraits. Probe scenario `shipmodels` checks their sizes now.
- **Textured Models for the Rocks and the Mineral Deposits**: the nine rocks are new textured models of weathered stone, each in its old rock's shape, size and place, with about 2,600 triangles. Twenty of the 22 kinds of mineral deposit are made in the rocks' shapes, in their element's colour: they keep that colour, now over a grey copy of the new rock's texture, as it lay over the old rock's before. The two deposits with shapes of their own are new too: Endurium is a cluster of dark violet crystals that glow from within, in a bed of dark rubble, and mercury is a mirror-bright pool in a rim of rock. Probe scenario `rockmodels`.
- **New Combat and Flight Sounds**: seven sound effects were clips from Star Trek: The Next Generation (the red alert, the transporter, the comm chirp, the phaser, the warp in and out and the torpedo), the space missile clip was under a licence that forbids commercial use, and the aliens' energy weapon had no source to trace. All are new sounds made for this port in an early 1980s electronic style: a hull alarm of four pulsing bursts when the armor runs low (2.5 s, where the old one ran 7 s and overlapped itself on every hit), a rising shimmer as cargo is beamed aboard, a short chirp as the ship hails, a laser cannon zap for the ship's laser and the terrain vehicle's stunner, a rising whoosh into a flux and a falling one out of it (2 and 3 s, as the trip and its fade take 1.5 and 2 s, where the old ones ran 6.5 and 7 s), a launch for every missile, the player's and the aliens', and a crackling discharge for the aliens' laser. Each is as loud as the clip it replaces, the two shots a little quieter than the old ones, which were driven into clipping. Probe scenario `soundclips`: the old clips pass 3 of its 32 checks, the new ones all 32.

### Fixed

- **Probe Runs and a Stuck Licensing Client**: every headless Unity of a Windows user shares one licensing client. A probe run that launched Unity a second after the previous scenario's Unity had quit started a client while the old one was still shutting down; the new client took the client's lock and never served its pipe, and from then on every headless Unity on the PC, of every session, failed to license after 75 seconds, retried and left another client behind. `probe.ps1`, `compile-check.ps1` and the four `wire.ps1` scripts (ship models, lifeform models, system map, system view) now launch Unity through `DevTools/HeadlessProbe/unity-batch.ps1`, the wiring scripts with Burst off as the probe has it: one launch at a time across sessions until Unity's licensing is initialized, no launch while a licensing client runs without serving its pipe (it is waited for, and after 45 seconds reported with its process ids as stuck), and a Unity that fails to license is stopped at once. A stuck client makes the scripts exit with code 3 (the wiring scripts too), and `run-all-scenarios.ps1` and `run-playthrough.ps1` stop there.
- **The Elowan Portrait's Blink Check**: the probe scenario `commelowan` watched the Elowan's eyes for 8 seconds of real time, a frame at a time. A blink keeps an eye under half its size for about 54 ms, so one slow frame of about a quarter of a second could step over it, and a full run once failed the check (the smallest eye 0.72). The watch now runs 8 seconds of game time at a steady 30 frames a second, which no blink can slip between, and 8 seconds always hold one.
- **Return Order Notes**: the 1.2.0 notes and the Return order's code said a cliff could stop the order. The ground never stops the terrain vehicle: it drives up any slope at the same speed, since the original reads only the ground the vehicle is on (only the fuel it uses rises with the height); what stops it is a rock, a tree, a ruin or the ship in the way. The notes and the comment now say so.
- **The Ship's Cargo List Shows Everything in the Hold**: the Captain's Cargo list showed only the elements in the ship's hold, so artifacts and specimens never appeared on it although they fill the hold. The original lists everything in the hold, each kind of item under a heading of its own (CAPTAIN `(/ITEMS)`, IT-OV `BOX>LIST`), and now the port does too (`ShipCargoButton`): ELEMENTS, ARTIFACTS and SPECIMENS, each kind's items with the one put in last first, an element at what the Trade Depot pays for a cubic meter of it, an artifact at what the Trade Depot pays for it or ??? while its kind has not been analysed, and a specimen at its sale value. An empty hold says "Starship hold is empty." instead of showing an empty table. Verified: the `shipcargo` probe scenario fails on the old code and passes on the new, and `cargo` still passes.
- **Shields Recharge as the Original's**: the shields regained 1% of their full charge every 5 seconds wherever the ship was, a pace of the port's own (the last known difference from the original in its rules). The original gives each icon in an encounter a turn, one a pass of its main loop, and on the ship's turn its shields regain 20 points while below what the shielding holds (COMBAT-OV `COMBAT`, `WEF20`, `WEEC4`); it refills them when an encounter ends (HYPER-OV `ENCCLEANUP`, `CHARGE-D`) and nowhere else. The port now does the same, a turn every (ships + 1) passes of 0.25 s, the pace measured in the GOG release: with one alien ship 40 points a second, with three 20. Probe scenario `shieldturns`.
- **A Star's State Is the Science Officer's Call**: the star system scan always gave the star's stability. The original's analysis of a star prints its state only when the science officer can tell (ANALYZE-OV `WF31E`, `?.CERTAIN`: the skill against a roll of 0 to 200), else "NOT CERTAIN", as for an alien ship's lines and a planet's. The scan now does the same. Probe scenario `starstate`.
- **Mining Laser Check Reads the Beam's Width**: the probe's `mininglaser` scenario read the beam's width from the line in one frame, and the beam fades with each pulse, so on a busy PC a frame late in a pulse read 0.10 and the check that the beam is drawn at the ground's scale failed (the end-of-series run of 2026-10-18; it passed alone). It now reads the width the beam is set to, 1.35.
- **Credits for the Music and Two Laser Shots**: the music, ambiences by Tabletop Audio, is under Creative Commons Attribution-NonCommercial-NoDerivatives 4.0, and two of the laser shots, by bubaproducer on freesound.org, under Creative Commons Attribution 4.0. Both licences ask for a credit, which the game never gave. The README now credits them, and says that the Unlicense covers the code and what was made for this port, not the third-party sounds and music, which keep their own licences.

## [1.3.0] - 2026-10-17

Easier flight. The ship steers the moment you press a direction and stops when you let go, as the original's does; before, its heading swung round over about a second and carried it wide past planets (#467). Its top speed in hyperspace and in a star system now follows its engines' class, a third of the original's with class 1 engines and all of it with class 5, and its acceleration sets how quickly it gets there (#468, `ROADMAP.md` D8, a departure from the original, whose ships all fly at one speed).

### Changed

- **Ship Speed by the Engines**: the ship flew at the original's speed whatever its engines, 2998 units a second in a star system and 371 in hyperspace, which made a new ship too fast to stop at a planet. Its top speed in hyperspace and in a star system now follows its engines' class: a third of those speeds with class 1 engines, each class a sixth more, and all of them with class 5. The ship gets there from a twelfth of its top speed in 5 seconds over its acceleration in G (engines against mass): a second for a new ship of 5 G, as the original's 12 steps, five for a heavily loaded one of 1 G, a quarter of a second for the lightest and fastest. Fuel still goes by the distance flown, and an encounter keeps the original's rules. A ruling of 2026-10-17 (`ROADMAP.md` D8), a departure from the original, whose speed is the same for every ship. Probe scenario `enginespeeds`; `shipspeeds` now flies class 5 engines.

### Fixed

- **Steering as the Original's**: in a star system and in hyperspace, Maneuver eased the ship's heading round toward the direction held, over about a second, so at the original's speed the ship swung far past a planet before it pointed at it, and landing took luck. The original's ship has no turn: each step goes the way of the key held (HYPER-OV `(XYSCAN)`). The ship now flies the way the stick points at once; only its model turns smoothly, in about a fifth of a second, without changing its path. Probe scenario `steering`.

## [1.2.0] - 2026-10-17

The ground game, natural planets and the original's flight. The terrain vehicle is easier to bring home and has more to do: a mining laser cuts a deposit into the hold (#456), Tools has Capture for a stunned lifeform (#459) and a Return order that drives the vehicle back to the ship (#457), Send beams its hold to the ship from within 20 km (#458), and a vehicle coming back can go aboard anywhere within 3 km of the ship (#452, #455). The ground up close has grain, depth and shadows, grass and pebbles, under a new ground detail setting (#461 to #464). The planets can be drawn in natural colors in space, on the console and on the ground (#443 to #448), and the science officer's analysis surveys a planet's mineral fields and lifeforms from orbit (#451, #453, #454). Flight, fuel, flux jumps and the alien groups' pursuit follow the original's code, and hyperspace and star system travel run at the pace measured in the GOG release (#442, #445). Alien ships call for help, and many smaller rules of trade, the crew, the sensors and Operations now match the original's.

### Added

- **Grass and Pebbles on the Ground**: at the Medium and High ground detail, small tufts of grass and pebbles stand on the ground around the terrain vehicle, out to 70 units at Medium and 110 at High, shrinking away toward that edge. They take the colour of the ground under them from the planet's own map, in either look, the pebbles greyer and darker than the soil. Grass grows in patches and only on a planet with life, thicker the more life it has; pebbles lie on every planet, more on one rich in minerals; nothing stands in water or lava. Each 8 unit cell of the ground is filled from a hash of the cell and the planet, so everything is in the same place every time and no random numbers are drawn (the rest of a planet is placed from them). They are drawn with GPU instancing, the grass swaying in the wind and the pebbles casting shadows at High, and nothing collides with them. Probe scenario `groundclutter`.
- **Depth on the Ground**: at the Medium and High ground detail, the ground around the terrain vehicle is shaded by its own shape: darkened where it curves in (valleys, craters' floors, creases) and lightened a little where it curves out (ridges, crowns), measured on the graphics card from the planet's heights 8, 16 and 32 units around each point, so the land reads in three dimensions from the vehicle's camera. The shadows follow the detail too while the vehicle is out: hard, at medium resolution, in 2 cascades to 300 units at Low, for the graphics cards Auto puts there; the project's own at Medium; at very high resolution at High; and the project's back once the vehicle is in the ship. Probe scenario `grounddetail`.
- **The Grain of the Ground**: at the Medium and High ground detail, the ground around the terrain vehicle has grain laid over the planet's albedo map, which is four units to a pixel and a blur up close: fine grit, and larger pebbles with dark cracks between them, at scales the vehicle's camera can see, fading out with distance and kept off water, its average colour and brightness unchanged. The texture is made once a session from an integer hash, so it draws no random numbers and moves nothing placed on a planet (`GroundGrain`, `SF_GROUND_DETAIL` in the terrain shader). Probe scenario `grounddetail`.
- **Ground Detail**: the save panel's Colors screen has a new Ground row: Auto, Low, Medium and High. Auto picks a level by the graphics card's memory (High from 4 GB, Medium from 2 GB, Low below that). The terrain grid around the terrain vehicle follows it: a vertex every 2 units at Low as before, every unit at Medium, and a fourth ring that carries it farther out at High. At every level the ground's heights are now worked out on the graphics card with the same smooth Catmull-Rom curve the game uses to place the vehicle and everything on the ground; one bilinear sample made the hills of flat facets between the map's points, a little off the heights the vehicle stood at. Probe scenario `grounddetail`.
- **Send Cargo to the Ship**: the terrain vehicle's cargo list has a new Send button. Within 20 km of the ship, the ship's transporter takes what the vehicle carries (minerals, artifacts and specimens) into the ship's hold, as far as the ship has room, and a crate of the cargo shimmers away on the vehicle, so the crew can go on exploring with an empty hold. What does not fit stays in the vehicle's list. Beyond 20 km, or with the ship's hold full, nothing goes and the crew says why. Probe scenario `sendcargo`.
- **A Return Order**: the terrain vehicle's Tools have a new Return button that drives the vehicle back to the ship by itself, on the fuel the trip takes, and takes it aboard once it is beside the ship (within 1.5 km: the ship's hull keeps the vehicle about 0.9 km from its middle on some sides). The fire button stops it on the way, as it stops Move. It stops short of lava between the vehicle and the ship, and when the vehicle makes no headway for 4 seconds (a rock, a tree, a ruin or the ship in the way), and says why. Probe scenario `returnorder`.
- **A Mining Laser**: the terrain vehicle took a mineral deposit into its hold in one press of Cargo. Its Weapon button is now Tools, with a new Mine button before the weapons: the mining laser aims a beam at the nearest deposit within reach and cuts it into the hold a tenth of a cubic meter at a time, 15 a second, on the vehicle's energy (10 of its tank a tenth, so filling the hold takes about a sixth of the tank), while the deposit shrinks. It stops when the deposit is gone, when the hold is full or the energy runs out, or when the fire button is pressed again, and what is left of a deposit is remembered as before. Cargo beside a deposit now names it and points to Mine, and still records ruins, takes artifacts and dropped cargo, offers lifeforms and lists the hold. The deposits, the hold and the values stay the original's. Probe scenario `mininglaser`; `m10`, `cargo`, `terrain`, `deposits`, `pickups`, `leaks` and the playthrough mine with it now.
- **Survey Marks on the Sensor Picture**: once the science officer has analysed a planet from orbit, the planet turning in the sensor picture shows the survey's marks too, pulsing gently: the landing map's crosses for the mineral fields and dots for the lifeforms, and its boxes for the ancient ruins with the Red Cylinder aboard. Both pictures share one set of marks, which now has mipmaps so that it stays smooth where it is shown smaller than it is drawn. Probe scenario `surveysensor`.
- **Survey Marks on the Landing Map**: once the science officer has analysed a planet from orbit, the landing map marks what the survey found, where the officer placed it: each mineral field as a cross (the larger the field, the larger the cross) and each lifeform as a dot. With the Red Cylinder aboard, the planet's ancient ruins are marked as boxes, with or without an analysis. The marks have dark edges so they read on any ground, and they take the colors of the scheme in use (in the classic scheme, gold crosses, green dots and red boxes), changing with it at once. Probe scenario `surveymap`.
- **A Survey from Orbit**: the science officer's analysis of a planet in orbit now surveys it, with two more lines: its mineral fields, the concentrations of its deposits, with the element most of them are of ("Mineral Fields: 12 fields, mostly Zinc"), and its lifeforms ("Lifeforms: 1 species, 7 found"). The places are worked out from the planet's own seeds exactly as the surface places its deposits and lifeforms (on planet 92 all 729 deposits and 7 lifeforms, each where the terrain vehicle finds it), and found as well as the officer's skill and the sensors' health allow. A science officer of full skill finds every field and lifeform and places them within a third of a degree; one of skill 20 finds about a fifth of the fields and places them up to 6 degrees off; damaged sensors find fewer. Emptied deposits no longer count. Each new line can read "Not certain", as the other lines of an analysis do. Decided 2026-10-17 (roadmap D6). Probe scenario `orbitsurvey`; `analysisskill` counts the two new lines.
- **Natural Planets on the Ground**: in the natural look, the ground the ship lands on and the terrain vehicle drives over is drawn in the planet's natural colors too, from its maps as detailed as in orbit, so the surface matches what the ship saw from orbit. The terrain keeps its own heights, water and relief. A molten world's lava still reads as lava there: dark red plates with orange seams. Probe scenario `naturalplanets`.
- **Natural Planets on the Console**: in the natural look, the System Map and the sensor picture of a scanned planet now show each planet in its natural colors too, and they follow the Colors screen's switch at once. The landing map keeps the planet's own colors, since they are the heights its legend explains. Probe scenario `naturalplanets`.
- **Natural Planets Close Up**: a launch from a planet's surface starts with the camera on top of the planet in orbit, looking out over the ground before it rises. In the natural look the camera sat inside the planet's smooth sphere there, so the view showed sky above the horizon and space through the planet below it. The smooth sphere is now 1% smaller, which does not show in orbit or in the star system, so the ground fills the view below the horizon. Wherever a texel of the planet's maps covers more than a pixel, as it does there, the ground also gets finer detail worked out on the spot (a little light and shade, and bumps), so it stays sharp close up. Probe scenario `naturalplanets`.
- **Natural Gas Giants and Molten Worlds**: the natural look now covers every kind of planet but the Crystal Planet. A gas giant's bands follow the bands of its own maps from the original's data, in the colors of its gases: cream and brown with a red storm for hydrogen and helium, as on Jupiter; cyan and blue with a dark storm for methane, as on Neptune; pale gold for ammonia, pale blue-gray for nitrogen and oxygen, yellow-green for chlorine and fluorine. The bands tear into eddies where they slide past each other, carry a great oval storm and a few small white ones, and the planet's deep air glows along its edge. A molten world has a dark crust with the deposits its seas leave on it (broad yellow fields of sulfur, as on Io). Its seas of lava, where the data has them, are crusted over in dark plates that drift apart along glowing seams, and thin glowing cracks run through the land. The lava shines by itself, also on the night side: white-hot for metal, yellow-orange for sodium compounds, deep red-orange for sulfur. Its clouds are ash. The air's glow and haze are now sampled in two halves, before and after the point where the line of sight passes closest to the planet, so a planet that fills the view shows no rings where the line of sight grazes the air. Probe scenario `naturalplanets`.
- **Natural Planets**: the planets in the star system and in orbit can now be drawn in natural colors. The save panel's Colors screen has two new buttons, Planets: Original (the look the port's planets have always had) and Natural (the default), and the choice is kept on this computer. Each planet's natural maps are made on the graphics card from its own maps and its climate in the game data, so its coastlines stay where the terrain has them (at least 99.7% of the land and sea in the same place on the planets checked). Seas take the color of what they are made of: blue water with turquoise shallows, dark brown methane as on Titan, slate nitrogen under pink ice, yellow-green chlorine compounds. Land takes its color from temperature, moisture and life, with deserts, salt flats, forests, tundra, snow and sea ice. Worlds without air show bare regolith with dark plains and frozen seas, and frozen worlds show cracked ice. The air is worked out by scattering sunlight in the planet's own gases (blue for nitrogen and oxygen, pale gold for carbon dioxide, cyan for methane, yellow-green for chlorine), as a glow along the planet's edge and a haze that thickens toward it, and sunlight reddens near the line between day and night and glints on open seas. A planet with weather has a layer of clouds, with cyclones and bands as rough as its weather, that turns a little faster than the ground and casts shadows on it. Natural planets are drawn on a smooth sphere, so their edge is round. Gas giants, molten worlds and the Crystal Planet keep the original look for now, and so does every planet on a computer whose graphics card cannot make the maps. Probe scenario `naturalplanets`; `atmospherepictures` checks the original look, and `orbitplanet` the planet under an encounter in orbit in either look.
- **Alien Distress Calls**: one or two ships of the Elowan, Gazurtoid, Mechans, Spemin, Thrynn, Velox, Uhlek or Interstel police, neither friendly nor neutral and not surrendering, now call for help as the original's do (COMM-OV `<AUX>`, `CALL-FOR-SHIPS`): a chance of 1 in 1500 each pass of the original's main loop, a quarter of a second, so about once in six minutes, and at once at their homeworld. "Captain, I'm picking up an alien distress call." More of their ships then warp in with a chance of 1 in 50 / (2 x their ships + 1) a pass, a few seconds later (`SHIPS-ARRIVE`): a new wave of the group, as the first one. The timing of a pass was measured in the original running at GOG's speed. Probe scenario `distresscalls`.
- **Build Test in Windows Sandbox**: `DevTools/WindowsSandbox/test-build.ps1` tries a Windows build on a clean copy of Windows. It maps the build folder read-only into Windows Sandbox, runs the game there in a window and collects the game's log (written straight to the host, so it survives a crash of the Sandbox), a record every 10 seconds of whether the game was running and responding, and, once it is quit, its saves and the Code Integrity events. Nothing touches the real saves. It also runs a build on a PC where Smart App Control blocks unsigned programs, since Smart App Control is in evaluation mode in the Sandbox. The graphics card is not shared by default, because sharing it crashed the Sandbox as the game started, and in full screen the picture stopped updating, so the game always runs in a window. Tried with the 1.1.0 build: from the intro through the Starport to orbit, with the saves and their backups written and no file blocked.

### Changed

- **Capture Needs a Stunned Lifeform**: Cargo beside a living lifeform offered Capture, which put it in stasis whatever its state. Capture is now in the terrain vehicle's Tools, and only a stunned lifeform beside the vehicle can be captured, as the 1984 design notes have it that a stunned lifeform will not run away or attack; a capture beam holds it during the 6 seconds the capture takes. Cargo offers Record and Ignore, and a dead lifeform can only be recorded. Probe scenario `capture`.
- **Back Into the Ship Within 3 km**: the terrain vehicle went back into the ship only when Move was stopped within half a kilometer of it, the original's rule that the vehicle must be on the ship's own map cell. The original moves the vehicle a cell at a time, so that cell was easy to stop on, but the port drives smoothly, and the spot was a few meters across. A stop within half a kilometer still takes the vehicle in at once. A vehicle coming back that stops anywhere else within 3 km of the ship is now asked "Return to the ship?", and Yes takes it in. It is asked rather than taken in, since a stop near the ship to mine or look would otherwise end the trip, and only once it has been farther away than 3 km, since the vehicle comes out 1.8 km behind the ship. Probe scenario `reboard`.

### Fixed

- **Grass and Pebbles in a Build**: a Windows build had nothing to draw the ground's grass and pebbles with: it kept none of their shader's instanced variants, which a build keeps only for a material in it that has instancing on, and the clutter made its materials from the shader alone, loaded from Resources. The Editor never strips variants, so every test there passed. The materials are now made from `Resources/Materials/Ground Clutter.mat`, which has instancing on, and the probe's `groundclutter` scenario checks it.
- **Encounter Speeds Measured Right Under Load**: the probe scenario `encounterspeeds` read the player's ship at 755 for the 640 of two cells in a full run with another Unity running, and 641 alone. It summed the path of every frame that ended in its half-second window and divided by half a second, so a long frame that began before the window added its whole path. It now divides the path by the time those frames took, and runs in game time of a sixtieth of a second a frame, so the speeds read 320, 640 and 960 exactly whatever the load.
- **Star System Travel at GOG's Pace**: in a star system the ship's top speed was 983 units a second, the original's 12 units a step at a pace of the port's own, 4 steps a second, and the step took 3 seconds to grow from 1 to 12. Measured in GOG's DOSBox at its 1500 cycles, the ship flies from over the sun to the system's edge, 400 units, in 2.72 s (2.74 s east and 2.70 s west through Arth's system): 12.2 steps a second, about the pace of hyperspace's 11.6. The top speed is now 2998 units a second and the step grows to it in about a second. Probe scenario `shipspeeds`.
- **Hyperspace Travel at GOG's Pace**: the ship crossed hyperspace at 128 units a second, 4 of the original's steps a second, a speed of the port's own. The original takes a step each pass of its main loop, as fast as the computer runs it. Measured in GOG's DOSBox at its 1500 cycles, the ship covers 1.45 coordinates a second in open space, 11.6 steps of 32 units, so hyperspace travel now runs at 371 units a second, about three times as fast. Fuel still goes by the distance flown. The speed in a star system is unchanged. Probe scenario `shipspeeds`.
- **Encounter Speeds as the Original's**: in an encounter the player's ship and every alien ship moved at the same 128 units a second, and the ship eased up to speed and coasted to a stop. The original moves one icon a pass of its main loop (COMBAT-OV `COMBAT`), the player's ship among them, so an alien ship moves a cell every (ships + 1) passes: with one ship two cells a second. Both were measured in the original at GOG's speed: one Spemin ship moved every 0.508 s (two passes of a quarter of a second), and each of three Veloxi ships every four passes (1.40 s, some ship every 0.355 s). The player's ship moves its step each pass while a direction is held (HYPER-OV `ACCELERATE`, `#VES-SPEED`): a cell at first, a cell more every 96 / ESPEED + 1 passes (ESPEED the larger of 2 and the ship's G times the engines' health), up to 6 cells with one alien ship, 3 with two and 2 from three, and it stops at once when the direction is let go. Aliens are now 8 to 14 times slower than the ship at its top speed. Probe scenario `encounterspeeds`.
- **The Win's Evaluation Does Not Keep the Ship Docked**: after the win, the docking bay refused to launch with "Report to Operations for evaluation." until Interstel's evaluation on the completion of the mission had been read. The released game's preflight check looks at an unread colony evaluation, a lost terrain vehicle and a tow (CHKFLIGHT-OV `WF40A`: `?EVAL`, `?TV`, `?RECALL`), not at the win's message (`WMSG`), so the crew may leave without reading it; Operations still shows it once. The docking bay now does the same. Probe scenario `starport-preflight`.
- **Debris Reads and Analyzes as the Original's**: the sensors read a wreck's Bio as 0 and a "Min" figure the port made up from the vessel's mass, and its analysis said only that it was debris. The released game reads debris with Bio and Energy, both 0 (SCIENCE `WF239`), and its analysis lists what the wreck is made of (ANALYZE-OV `.CONSTIT`: "CONSTITUENTS:" and the Endurium and elements of the vessel's debris). The readout now shows Bio 0 and Energy 0, and the analysis gives the constituents. Probe scenarios `sensorpictures` and `salvage`.
- **The Analysis Words a Planet's Surface as the Original's**: a planet's analysis gave the game data's surface names, "Gas Giant" and, for the Crystal Planet, "Crystal". The released game's analysis says "GAS" for a gas giant, and has no word for the Crystal Planet's surface, which falls to its "UKNOWN" (ANALYZE-OV `(.SURF)`). The analysis now says "Gas" and "Unknown"; the planet labels and the Look report keep the full names. Probe scenario `analysissurface`.
- **The Status Display's Energy as the Original's**: the energy (the Endurium in the hold) had three decimals below 10 cubic meters ("5.000") and none from 1,000 ("1235"). The released game prints two decimals below 100 cubic meters and one from 100 on (SCIENCE `WE8CE`), so 5 cubic meters read "5.00" and 1,234.5 read "1234.5"; the display now does the same. Probe scenario `statusdisplay`.
- **Crew Deceased, Game Over**: when the last of the crew died, the game over screen said "The entire crew has died." and nothing about the game being over. The released game says "!!CREW DECEASED!!" and "**GAME OVER**" (HEAL-OV), and the screen now says "Crew deceased!" and "GAME OVER", as it says "Hull destroyed!" and "GAME OVER" for a destroyed hull. Probe scenario `crewdeath`.
- **Probe's Forced Vessels Get Their Own Hit Points**: `sensorpictures` failed now and then on master since alien readings fall with damage (#425): a living Spemin scout read Bio 40 and Energy 30 instead of 100 and 100. The probe's `ScanShip` and `ForceVessel` reset an encounter, which draws each ship's vessel at random and gives it that vessel's hit points, and then changed the vessel without its hit points, so the reading was a share of the wrong maximum. They now give each ship the hit points of the vessel they force.
- **Probe's Operations Scenarios Let It Show Its Evaluations**: since Operations shows a waiting supplemental evaluation by itself a second after it opens (#421), `starport-win` and `starport-supplementalfine` failed on master: they opened Operations, waited, then showed the evaluations themselves, by which time Operations had shown them and booked the fine, so they read only what was left. They now wait for Operations to show them, and show them only if it does not; `starport-supplementalfine` also checks that nothing is booked as Operations opens. Both pass on master's code: `starport-win` 5/5 (3/5 before), `starport-supplementalfine` 2/2 (1/2 before).
- **Probe Runs That Write No Log Fail**: when Unity quit at once without writing a log, `probe.ps1` read the log an earlier run of the same name had left and showed its checks and its RESULT as this run's: a playthrough stage whose Unity quit within seconds showed "pass 5 fail 0" from an earlier run. The probe now deletes the log before Unity starts and, when there is none afterwards, prints `CHECK FAIL no log written: unity exit=<code>`, gives no RESULT line and exits with 1, so `run-all-scenarios.ps1` counts a failed check and `run-playthrough.ps1` shows the stage as `FAILED: no RESULT line`. `run-playthrough.ps1` also deletes a stage's save from an earlier run before the stage runs, so `-From` no longer carries the chain on past a stage that saved nothing, and `compile-check.ps1` deletes its log and says when Unity wrote none.
- **Operations Does Not Bring Up the Evaluations Twice**: Operations brings up a waiting supplemental evaluation by itself a second after it opens. A player who pressed Evaluation within that second saw the evaluation, and then, when the second was up, the screen started over without it, since a supplemental evaluation is shown once: the towing charges or the lost vehicle's report vanished while being read. Opening the evaluations now stops Operations from bringing them up again. Probe scenario `starport-evaluationonce`.
- **A Balance Below 0 Is Charged Interest**: the bank paid its 12% a year only on a balance above 0, so a crew whose fines (a tow, an unsuitable colony, a lost terrain vehicle) took the balance below 0 owed nothing more. The released game works the interest out signed (BANK-OV `INT%`: the balance times 12 over 100, times the days over 300), so a balance below 0 is charged it and the ledger shows the charge. The bank now does the same: -10,000 M.U. over 150 days is charged 600, with an "Interest 600-" row. Probe scenario `starport-interest`.
- **Personnel Keeps the Original's Roster**: Personnel held 20 files, took names of up to 24 letters, and took a name already on file. The released game has 25 files, takes a name of 14 letters at most, and refuses a name already on the roster, dead or alive (PERSONNEL-OV: its loops over 25 files, `OV#IN$` with 14, `WEFFF` and "THIS NAME IS ALREADY ON FILE"). Personnel now does the same: Create is offered until 25 files, the name box takes 14 letters, and a name already on file, in any letters, is refused with "This name is already on file" and the box asks again. Probe scenario `starport-personnelrules`.
- **A Star's Stability as the Original's**: the star system scan called a star "slightly unstable" only more than 1000 days before its flare, as the 1984 notes have it, and no star of the game is that far, so every star yet to flare read "Unstable" with its days to the flare, up to 792. The released game reads it otherwise (ANALYZE-OV `WF31E`): stable from 399 days before the flare, slightly unstable from 398 to 61 days, and "UNSTABLE - ESTIMATED TIME TO FLARE:" with the days only within 60; after the win every star is stable, and only a flare that came before the win is called post-flare (`WEC04`). The scan now says the same, and the time of the win is saved for it. Probe scenario `starstability`.
- **Interstel Pays the Win's Bonus at Once**: the 500,000 MU bonus for destroying the Crystal Planet was paid when the ship docked at the Starport again, as the chairman's message says ("WHEN YOU RETURN TO STARPORT"). The released game pays it as the planet goes: DPART-OV's `WF09C` shows the award, then adds 500,000 MU to the balance and books it as a BONUS (transaction type 3), so it is in the bank on the day of the win whether the ship comes home or not. The bonus is now paid right after the message, and docking pays nothing more. A game saved between the win and the return under the old rule is still paid on docking. Probe scenario `win`; `towbonus` keeps the older save's case, and the playthrough's stages 19 and 20 check the new timing.
- **The Dead Can Be Neither Assigned nor Trained**: in Crew Assignment, cycling through the crew gave the post to every file shown, a dead crew member's too, and an empty post started on the first file even when that crew member had died; in Personnel, Train offered a dead crew member's skills for training at 300 M.U. a session. The original takes only a living crew member for a post (ASSCREW-OV `WF395`, otherwise "YOU CAN'T ASSIGN" the dead crewmember) and trains only the living (PERSONNEL-OV `WF3A3`, otherwise "YOU CAN'T TRAIN"). A dead crew member's file is now shown without taking the post, an empty post starts on the first living crew member, and Train on a dead crew member says "You can't train a dead crewmember" and charges nothing. Probe scenario `starport-deadcrew`.
- **Missiles Scenario Fires Its Far Shot at a Living Ship**: the probe's `missiles` scenario fired a missile at a ship 300 away, then fired at the same ship 1200 away, and failed in many runs: when the first ship was a Spemin transport the close shot destroyed it (200 armor points), and a missile fired at a dead ship is not launched. The far shot now goes to a living ship. Three runs in which the close shot destroyed its target pass 4/4.
- **Alien Ships Move at One Speed**: every alien ship moved at a speed taken from its vessel's move delay in the game data, so Spemin ships ran at 1.47 times the speed of most others and the Mysterion barely moved. The released game reads that delay nowhere: COMBAT-OV moves every alien ship one unit in each of its turns, and only if its vessel record's byte 6 is not 0 (`WE06B`, copied by `+A-VESS`), which keeps the Mechan Scout, the Veloxi Drone, the Nomad Probe and the Noah 9 derelict where they are. Every vessel now moves at the encounter's one alien speed, and those four stay still. How fast the aliens move against the player's ship is unchanged for now: the original's ratio depends on how often its main loop ran, which still needs a measurement in the original. Probe scenario `vesselstats`.
- **Flight Speeds as the Original's**: the ship gathered speed slowly in hyperspace (a 1 G ship took about a minute to reach 90% of its top speed), coasted to a stop, flew a star system at 4 times its hyperspace speed, and lost top speed with its engines' health. The original moves its ship a step at a time (HYPER-OV `CSPEED`, `!MAXSPEED`, `ACCELERATE`): in hyperspace at its top speed from the first step, in a star system with a step growing from 1 to 12 of the system's units, whose top speed is 7.68 times hyperspace's (12 units of 20.48 against 1 of 32), and only while a direction is held, so the ship now stops at once. The engines' health counts only in an encounter's acceleration (`!S/A`); destroyed engines still move nothing (DAMAGE-OV `WF2AC`). How many steps a second the original made is not known, so the hyperspace speed stays the port's 128. Probe scenario `shipspeeds`; `systemeffects` now checks that half-health engines keep the top speed in hyperspace.
- **Skilled Navigators See Continuum Fluxes**: fluxes were shown on the screen only with the Ring Device aboard (a call of 2026-10-10, made before the original's code was read). The released game also shows them to a navigator of skill above 150 (HYPER-OV `.FLUX-ICON`: NAV-SKILL above 150, or the Ring Device), so a well trained navigator now spots them without it. Probe scenario `ringdevice`.
- **Encounters Begin with the Original's First Wave**: an encounter always brought in as many ships as its "ships at a time" allows. The original draws the first wave's size (HYPERMSG-OV `+A-VESS`): from 2 fewer than that number up to it, at random, and at least one, so an encounter of 3 at a time comes with 1, 2 or 3 ships, and one of 2 at a time with a single ship two times in three. Later waves, which the original brings in only after an alien distress call, are not in the port yet. Probe scenario `encounterwaves`.
- **Terrain Vehicle Weapons Use the Original's Fuel**: a laser shot of the terrain vehicle took 8 of a full tank's 999 and a stunner shot 4, figures from the 1984 design notes, and the weapons refused to fire with less than that left. The released game takes 300 of the tank's units for a stunner shot and 600 for a laser shot, about twice as much (TV-OV `DO.WEAPON`), and fires whatever is left; the port now refuses only once the tank's reserve is gone too, when the crew would be walking back. Probe scenario `tvweapons`.
- **The Walk Back to the Ship Can Injure the Crew**: when the terrain vehicle ran out of fuel the crew were back in the ship at once, unhurt however far they had to walk. The original walks them back a map cell at a time (MOVE-OV `WF16E`), and at each cell a chance in five injures one of the six posts' crew members (`WF122`, `INJURE-PL`): the larger of 1 and a roll of up to 29 over the race's durability, times the larger of 1 and three times the cell's E/M over the weather's efficiency, so a long walk over high ground or a liquid can be deadly. Each crew member hurt on the way is named once. Probe scenario `outoffuel`.
- **Surface Weather as the Original's**: the weather on a planet's surface came from shares measured in play on two planets of Arth: storms in 12% to 50% of the hours by the planet's weather class, even where the planet data had none, heavy snow on frozen planets, injuries in half of the storm hours, and the bearing to the ship lost in every storm. The released game's own rules are now followed (BEHAV-OV `?STORM`, STORM-OV `(DO.STORM`). A planet with an atmosphere keeps a spell of weather for 1 to (5 - activity) x 3 - 1 more star hours, then rolls a storm against 0, 20, 40, 60 or 85 in 99 by its weather activity, picked by its surface from the original's table, or else a hazy, clear or overcast sky. A storm halves the vehicle's efficiency and heavy snow, fog and rain take a quarter of it. A storm hour injures the crew 29 times in 99 while the vehicle stands and one time in three while it moves, with one more roll as the storm begins. The bearing to the ship is lost in storms and heavy snow unless the navigator's skill is above 199. The planet data had no weather activity for planets 90 and 453 and too little for 503; they now have the original's (1, 1 and 4).
- **Hyperspace Fuel by the Original's Steps**: the engines burned their fuel by the straight distance flown, so a diagonal course cost 1.41 times as much as the original's. The original burns a step's fuel at every step of the ship, an eighth of a coordinate, and a step on the diagonal moves the ship along both axes at once (HYPER-OV `FLY` and `-ENDURIUM`), so the fuel now goes by the longer of the two distances flown along the axes: 0.48 m³ a coordinate for class 1 engines, straight or diagonal. A step's fuel is a whole number of hundredths, 7 less the engines' class, and the Tesseract halves it in whole hundredths (PORTMENU-OV `WEAF0`), so class 2 and class 4 engines with it use 0.16 and 0.08 m³ a coordinate, not 0.20 and 0.12; the starmap's estimate and the low fuel warnings use the same step. Probe scenario `hyperspacefuel`.
- **Flux Jumps as the Original's**: going through a continuum flux slid the ship across hyperspace for two seconds or more while the clock ran, set it down along its own heading, and one jump in four flashed an invented "Continuum flux detected! Navigational sensors offline!". In the original the jump is instant: the ship comes out a step off the far end in one of the eight directions at random (HYPER-OV `>FLUX-NODE`, `JMPSHP`), and the navigator loses the ship's bearings for 250 less the navigator's skill, in half seconds. Until they are back the coordinates above the main view are random numbers and the starmap answers "I can't get our bearings, captain." (MAP-OV `(/STARMAP)`, `WEC57`), so a skilled navigator matters after a jump. Probe scenario `fluxjump`.
- **Terrain Vehicle Fuel as the Original's**: the vehicle burned its fuel by the time its engines ran, slowed down on rising ground and in storms (a rule from the 1984 design notes), and in a storm burned four times as much for the distance. The released game burns a step's fuel for every map cell driven (MOVE-OV `SET-SPEED` and `WF29C`): 170 of the tank's 30767 on a liquid, one and a half times the contour of the ground on land (24 at the shore up to 190 on the highest ground), divided by the weather's efficiency, so a storm doubles the cost of each kilometer and the speed stays the same; it reads only the cell the vehicle is in, so slopes no longer slow it. A step on the diagonal costs one step, the efficiency on the display is the original's (99% at the shore, 14% on a liquid), and the tank is empty at 6.5% below 0%, the original's reserve of 2000, not at 3%. Probe scenario `tvfuel`; `uphill` now checks that the vehicle goes up a slope no slower than down it.
- **Alien Groups Come After the Ship as the Original's**: alien groups within the port's radar distance glided toward the ship at a steady speed and went back home when it was out of reach, and the Dodecahedron doubled that distance. The original moves them in jumps every 2 star hours (HYPERMSG-OV `TRAK-E`, run by `TRAK-ENCS`): along each axis a group comes half the way in hyperspace and three quarters in a star system, in whole map coordinates, and the whole way when that rounds to none, so its last jump lands on the ship; in hyperspace only the groups of the region around the ship move, and only while within 63 of the original's units (about 8 coordinates) of home; groups never go back. The Dodecahedron makes the share four fifths and six sevenths and lifts the leash. Probe scenario `alienpursuit`; `dodecahedron` now checks the leash and the Dodecahedron's longer jump.
- **Starport Door Names Readable in the Light Modes**: the name of the door the astronaut stands at, shown at the top of the screen over the Starport's hall, took the light theme's text colour in every light mode, nearly black, while the hall and the space behind it keep the scheme's dark colours, so the name could not be seen (a contrast of 1.0 to 1.2 against the backdrop, 15 of the 30 scheme and mode pairs). It is drawn over a 3D view, so it now takes the views' colours as the overlays in the main viewport do: a new marker, `UIKitOverView`, on the door name tells `UIKitTheme.IsOverView` so, and the name is drawn in the scheme's dark text colour in both modes, with a contrast of 17.7:1 or better at all seven doors and in all 15 schemes. The probe scenario `starport-doorlabels` walks the astronaut to each door and measures the name against a picture of the hall behind it.
- **Playthrough Stage 9 Meets the Velox After Another Encounter**: on the way out of Sphexi's star system the ship can meet a Velox group (encounter 214), and leaving it starts the minute without encounters (#367). Stage 9 then flew toward the Velox of encounter 229 for 20 seconds, within that minute, so the meeting never began and the stage failed by chance. The probe now skips what is left of the minute before that flight, as it skips the travel, and counts the meeting only if the encounter that begins is 229: in one trial a wandering Minstrel group came first and passed for the Velox. Checked by forcing the minute before the flight: stage 9 failed on the old probe and passes on the new one.
- **Probe Runs Leave Burst Off**: on a computer where Smart App Control enforces its policy, every headless probe run and compile check had Unity's Burst compiler load an unsigned DLL it wrote to the project's `Library/BurstCache/JIT/`, and Windows blocked it (Code Integrity event 3033, "error code 4551" in the Unity log) with a notice that was taken for the game itself being blocked; 22 of the 26 blocks of one day came from probe runs. `probe.ps1` and `compile-check.ps1`, and with them `run-all-scenarios.ps1` and `run-playthrough.ps1`, now start Unity with `--burst-disable-compilation`, and so does the documented headless compile check command. Burst only speeds up the experimental `TerrainJob` of `Test.unity`, which no build scene uses, so the game and the scenarios are unchanged. The argument is used rather than the `UNITY_BURST_DISABLE_COMPILATION` environment variable, which also switches off Burst's IL post-processing and would make the next Editor with Burst on rebuild every script assembly. Checked in a new worktree: with the argument Burst writes no DLL at all and Code Integrity logs no block, the compile check passes, and the scenarios `latent` (the one that runs `TerrainJob`), `h6`, `starport`, `m18`, `deposits` and `terrain` give the results of the last runs on master.
- **Starport Scene Has Its Lighting Data**: the Starport scene pointed at a lighting settings asset that was never committed (Unity made it when the scene was first saved in Unity 6) and had no lighting data of its own, while it was set to bake its lighting on load. In play mode Unity cannot bake, so every load of the Starport printed "Computing lightmaps is not available in play mode", and a build, which can bake, left untracked lighting files in `Assets/Scenes/Starport/`.
