- **Winning the Game Is Saved and Rewarded**: destroying the Crystal Planet at its control nexus brings Interstel's message with the Interstel Medal of Sublime Achievement and "a special bonus of 500,000 MU when you return to Starport" (STRINFO 2.12); the original kernel records the win (`?WIN`), looks for flares only before it (`?FLARE`), and keeps a "play the win evaluation message" flag (`WMSG`). The port showed the message and nothing followed. The win is now saved (`PD_General.m_gameWon`): stars stop flaring, docking pays the 500,000 MU once with a "Mission completed" ledger entry and a message (`PlayerData.PayWinBonus`, `PD_General.m_winBonusPending`), and the Evaluation screen shows Interstel's supplemental evaluation on completing the mission (STRINFO 1.2) instead of the scene's colony recommendation text. The ledger wording and docking message are the port's; the medal appears only in Interstel's message, since the sources show it nowhere else. Verified with the `win` and `starport-win` probe scenarios.
- **Arth's Sun Flares**: Arth's sun has its flare day in the game data (day 300, 01-01-4621 in the original calendar, "the final week of your Ten-month" to the Elowan). The original kernel checks the current system's star for a flare as each day ends, unless the game is won (`?FLARE`, `?WIN`); a ship caught in one is "incinerated" (STRINFO 2.14), and the Starport is gone afterwards: Interstel's win message "will be received even after Starport has been destroyed" (2.12), and a distress call gets "There's no response!" (2.14). The port had none of this. On the flare day of an unwon game the Starport is now destroyed (`PD_General.m_starportDestroyed`, saved; `ArthFlare`). A ship in Arth's system is incinerated with STRINFO's text filled in and the game ends, by the same destruction as in combat; a ship elsewhere carries on. Afterwards Arth's Starport model is gone, "There's no response from Starport." replaces docking in orbital range of Arth, and Distress gets STRINFO's "no response". The flare is checked only in space, where the game clock runs. The orbital range wording and Arth being unorbitable afterwards are the port's choices; the sources do not describe Arth after the flare. Verified with the `arthflare` probe scenario.
- **Every Way to Lose Leads to the Same Game Over**: the original kernel checks the current system's star for a flare as each day ends (`?FLARE`), and a ship caught in one is "incinerated" (STRINFO 2.14). The port's stars instead dealt up to 50 points of damage to a ship entering their system in the last day before the flare, which no source describes, and the game over screen cleared the messages and always said "Ship destroyed!", hiding the cause once the ship had exploded. Every star now flares on its day at a ship in its system (`StellarFlares`, which absorbs Arth's flare): a ship arriving that day, or in hyperspace or an encounter, is spared, and no star flares after a win. Every loss of the ship takes one path (`CombatController.DestroyPlayerShip` for causes other than damage), and the game over screen states the cause: STRINFO's flare text with star and stardate, or "Ship destroyed!" after combat. A star still brightens as its flare nears. Losing the whole crew is not covered: nothing in the port can hurt the crew yet, and the sources for crew death are the 1984 drafts (roadmap Phase 6). Verified with the `gameover` probe scenario; the `win` and `arthflare` scenarios were updated to match.
- **Consequences of Destroying Elan or the Uhlek Mind-Ganglion**: STRINFO (5.1) says the Uhlek "will be rendered harmless" once a Black Egg destroys their mind-ganglion's planet, and the Spemin that they "would be helpless until another one developed"; for Elan the game data has the Elowan line "Thou hast done a deed most unthinkably foul... We are now and forever mortal enemies." (STRINFO 2.4). The port gave only the crew's reports (#117). Uhlek ships now hold their fire for good once that planet is gone, and after Elan's destruction the Elowan start every encounter hostile, say that line instead of a hail, never answer a hail with a comm link again, and attack (`Encounter.UpdateElowanAfterElan`, `BlackEgg.IsElanDestroyed`, `BlackEgg.IsUhlekMindGanglionDestroyed`). `Encounter.AddComm` can now take a specific comm. Treating "harmless" as not firing, and the Elowan's single line before fighting, are interpretations. Verified with the `racelosses` probe scenario.
- **Tesseract Doubles Engine Efficiency**: the Starport's analysis says the Tesseract "projects a field which doubles the energy efficiency of a ship's engines", but it did nothing. In the hold it now halves engine fuel use in hyperspace, the only place the engines burn fuel (`PD_PlayerShip.GetEngineFuelFactor`; `PD_PlayerShip.HasArtifact` checks for an artifact aboard). Weapons and hourly shield fuel are unaffected. Verified with the `tesseract` probe scenario.
- **Whining Orb Translates Spemin**: the Thrynn say an "orb device which makes a queer whining sound" translates the Spemin language (STRINFO 2.5), and the Starport sells it for 6,000 MU, but it did nothing. In the hold it now stops all garbling of Spemin speech, whatever the communications officer's skill; other races are garbled as before. Verified with the `whiningorb` probe scenario.
- **Rod Device Shields Against Lasers**: the Starport's analysis says the Rod Device "projects an enhanced laser shield around a spaceship", and the original combat code tracks it (`?ROD`), but it did nothing. In the hold it now halves alien laser damage; plasma bolts and missiles are unchanged. The sources give no figure, so half is a design choice. Verified with the `roddevice` probe scenario.
- **Crystal Pearl Warps a Crippled Ship Out**: the Thrynn call the Crystal Pearl "an automatic warp-out device for when a ship is heavily damaged" and the Spemin say it "automatically warps a critically wounded ship out of danger" (STRINFO 2.5 and 2.7), but it did nothing. In the hold it now warps the ship out of an encounter, as if it had flown out (`Encounter.WarpOut`), when a hit would leave it below a tenth of its armor or destroy it; the ship keeps its remaining armor (at least 1 point) and missiles in the air are cleared. The tenth is a design choice, as the sources do not define "heavily damaged". The ship is not yet destroyed when the Pearl acts, so the rule that nothing restores a destroyed ship's armor above 0 still holds. Verified with the `crystalpearl` probe scenario.
- **Dodecahedron Draws Alien Ships**: the Thrynn call the Dodecahedron "an ancient distress beacon which attracts any ships in the area" (STRINFO 2.5), and the Starport's analysis agrees, but it did nothing. In the hold it now doubles the radar distance at which alien ships notice and approach the ship, in hyperspace and star systems. The sources give no distance, so the factor is a design choice. Verified with the `dodecahedron` probe scenario.
- **Shimmering Ball Cloaks the Ship in Combat**: the Starport's analysis says the Shimmering Ball "seems to be a cloaking device" that "should operate automatically during combat", and the Thrynn would pay well for it, but it did nothing. In the hold it now cloaks the ship in combat so the aliens cannot fire at it (`CombatController.PlayerIsCloaked`); each player laser shot or missile drops the cloak for 3 seconds. Hiding the ship until it fires was decided on 2026-10-10; the 3 seconds are a design choice. Verified with the `shimmeringball` probe scenario.
- **Flat Device Shields the Terrain Vehicle**: the Starport's analysis says the Flat Device projects a shield around the terrain vehicle "which makes it difficult for dangerous lifeforms to attack you", and the 1986 kernel has a flag for it (`?FLAT`, "TRUE if TV has flat device"), but it did nothing while there were no lifeforms. In the ship's hold or the terrain vehicle's, it now halves how often lifeform attacks hit (`TerrainLifeform.TerrainVehicleIsShielded`), and a blocked one reads "..., but the shield turns it away." The sources say only "difficult", so halving is a design choice. Verified with the `flatdevice` probe scenario: of 200 attacks at a 96% hit chance, 195 hurt the crew without the device and 104 with it; the old code made no difference and fails the check.
- **Red Cylinder Locates Ancient Ruins**: STRINFO calls it "the Red Cylinder ancient-ruin locater device" and the Starport's analysis "an orbital scanner of some sort", but it did nothing. The shipped game's landing map code contains "THE RED CYLINDER IS FLASHING" (`STARB.COM`, beside the site and region words), so in the hold it now reports "The Red Cylinder is flashing." while the landing map crosshair is within 8 degrees of an ancient ruin, random or a formation (`RedCylinder`). Random ruins are worked out from orbit exactly as the surface places them: `TerrainRuins` now makes all of a random ruin's draws in one place (`MakeRandomRuins`), used by the surface and read by `GetRandomRuinPlaces` with the same seed (`TerrainGrid.GetRuinSeed`), so no ruin moves. The radius is a design choice. Verified with the `redcylinder` probe scenario on planet 94 of Arth: the ruin computed from the maps is where the surface placed it (100.6, -75.3), and the cylinder flashes beside it, not 90 degrees away, nor when it is not aboard; both checks fail on the old code.
- **Ring Device Reveals Continuum Fluxes**: the Starport's analysis says the Ring Device "will identify any nearby continuum flux", the Thrynn say it makes fluxes appear on a ship's screens (STRINFO 2.5), and Noah 1 failed "because a Ring Device malfunction resulted in an inability to identify continuum fluxes"; yet every flux was always visible and the device did nothing. Fluxes now show in hyperspace only with the Ring Device in the hold (decided on 2026-10-10). A hidden flux is still there, and a ship flying into one goes through. The device lies at the north pole of Mars, in Earth's system next to Arth. Verified with the `ringdevice` probe scenario.
- **Endurium Price Follows the Notices**: the notices of 20-02-4620 and 15-05-4620 raise Endurium to 1500 and then 2000 M.U. per cubic meter, and STRINFO's price table (1.4) values it "equally ... whether being bought or sold" at "1000, 1500, 2000 (price rises over time)". The port sold it for 1000 and always bought it back for the game data's 500. Endurium now trades at 1000 M.U. both ways, 1500 from 20-02-4620 and 2000 from 15-05-4620 (`GD_Element.GetStarportPrice` and `GetActualValue`, now behind every price in the trade depot and cargo list); other minerals keep the game data's prices. Following STRINFO over the game data was decided on 2026-10-10. The sell list also showed each mineral's selling price, twice what a sale brings; it now shows what the Starport pays. Verified with the `starport-endurium` probe scenario.
- **Nebulae Keep the Shields Down**: the design notes say nebulae affect the shields (priority.txt) without saying how, and the port only drew them ("TODO: affect shields"). Inside a nebula the shields now cannot be raised, and raised shields collapse on entry (decided on 2026-10-10). The ship is inside a nebula in hyperspace per the game data, anywhere in the system of a star inside one, and in an encounter per where it began (`SpaceflightController.ShipIsInsideANebula`). Nebulae do no damage. Verified with the `nebula` probe scenario.
- **Starport Repairs Restore the Ship**: the original ship configuration has a Repair button (SS Ship Configuration); the port's only played a sound. Repair now makes the ship whole: missing armor points cost the armor class price (class 1 is 1,500 M.U. for 500 points, 3 M.U. a point; a bare hull pays the class 1 rate), rounded up, and shields recharge free (`PD_PlayerShip.GetStarportRepairCost`). An undamaged ship needs nothing, and an unaffordable repair is refused with its cost. The payment joins the other ship configuration entries in the bank ledger. No source gives a price; charging the armor's worth was decided on 2026-10-10. Verified with the `starport-repair` probe scenario.
- **Distress Gets the Ship Towed Home, for a Fine**: STRINFO (1.2) has Interstel's supplemental evaluation of towing charges after an emergency distress call, with fines that "vary directly with the distance from Arth and range from 15000 to 80000 MU"; the Distress button only printed text. A distress call now tows the ship into Arth's docking bay with a fine of 15000 M.U. plus a share of 65000 proportional to its distance from Arth against the farthest star (`PlayerData.GetTowingFine`; the scale is a design choice), a "Towing charges" ledger entry, and Interstel's evaluation with the amount on the Operations evaluation screen. That screen now lists every supplemental evaluation (`PD_General.m_supplementalEvaluationList`, oldest first), then the mission completion evaluation after the win. Once the Starport is destroyed there is still no response (#121). Verified with the `distress` probe scenario.
- **Alien Vessels With Their Own Armor, Shields and Speed**: the game data gives every alien vessel armor and shield points and a move delay (xls Vessels), but alien ships had 100 points per armor and shield class, half the data's 200, and all moved at one speed. Each alien ship now has its vessel's points (`PD_AlienShip.ResetHitPoints`), so it takes twice the hits, and a speed that follows its move delay (`GD_Vessel.GetSpeedFactor`): at 2200, the most common delay, it moves at the encounter's alien ship speed as before; a Spemin ship (1500) is about one and a half times as fast and an Uhlek one (3200) about two thirds as fast. The data gives relative speeds only, so the reference delay is a design choice. Ships saved mid-encounter keep their points. Verified with the `vesselstats` probe scenario.
- **Starmap Fuel Estimate**: the designers' 1985 list has "STARMAP - The fuel estimate will be working" (alpha.txt), but the starmap showed only the cursor's coordinates. It now also shows the Endurium a hyperspace flight from the ship to the cursor would use: the distance in starmap coordinates times the engine class's fuel per coordinate, as in flight, halved with the Tesseract (`PD_PlayerShip.GetFuelForDistance`). The label reads "60   40   Fuel 9.6"; the layout is a design choice. Verified with the `starmapfuel` probe scenario.
- **Colony Recommendations**: the original Log Planet asks "Recommend this planet for colonization?" (SS Log Planet), and STRINFO (1.2) has Interstel's evaluations: a bonus for each planet on its list of 51 (optimal or suitable, 30000 to 55000 MU), and for any other a notice that "due to [reasons], this planet proved unsuitable for colonization", a 100 MU fine that is "only a warning", and later fines that "vary with how unsuitable the planet is". The port logged the planet and nothing followed. Log Planet now asks, with No and Yes on the console; Yes recommends the planet (`PD_General.m_colonyRecommendationList`, saved). Back in the Starport's docking bay, Interstel evaluates every planet recommended since the last visit (`PlayerData.EvaluateColonyRecommendations`): a listed planet earns its bonus; any other is fined, the first 100 MU with the warning and each later one 5000 MU per reason, with a ledger entry and the evaluation on the Operations evaluation screen. The reasons follow what the game data's planet tables mark habitable (temperature, weather, hydrosphere, atmosphere and its density; `GD_Planet.GetUnsuitableReasons`), plus crushing gravity above 2 G. The 5000 MU and 2 G are design choices; the draft's 1 to 10 rating is not in the original screens and is left out. Verified with the `colonies` probe scenario.
- **Ship Systems Take Damage**: the original damage screen has bars for the hull, engines, sensors, comm, shield, missile and laser (SS Damage), which its engineer repairs one at a time with minerals from the hold; the port tracked only armor points. Each other system now has a saved health from 0 to 100 (`PD_PlayerShip.m_systemHealth`, full in older saves), and every hit through the shields damages the hull (the armor points, as before) and one random system the ship has, by the same share (`PD_PlayerShip.DamageRandomSystem`). The engineer's Damage report lists each system's health, and Starport repair restores the systems free with the armor. How damage spreads was decided on 2026-10-11 (no shipped source gives it); the share is a design choice. System effects and mineral repairs follow in separate changes. Verified with the `systemdamage` probe scenario.
- **Damaged Systems Work in Proportion**: each damaged system now works in proportion to its health (decided on 2026-10-11): engines lose top speed and acceleration, the shield generator holds less charge (`PD_PlayerShip.GetMaximumShieldPoints`), the laser cannon and missile launcher do less damage and cannot fire when destroyed, sensors give no readings below a quarter health, and comm equipment garbles more (the officer's training counts in proportion to its health). The quarter is a design choice. The `racelosses` and `m19` probe scenarios now restore the systems after their hits, so later measurements do not depend on which system a hit damaged. Verified with the `systemeffects` probe scenario.
- **Engineer's Repairs Take Minerals**: the original engineer asks "Captain, we need 2 cubic meters of Molybdenum for repairs. Shall I take this from cargo?" and, without it, says "Ceasing repair" (SS Repair); the port's engineer repaired armor for free. Repair now starts on the most damaged item, hull or system, and asks for its mineral, a cubic meter per tenth missing: Aluminum (hull), Molybdenum (engines), Copper (sensors), Silver (comm), Titanium (shield generator), Iron (missile launcher) and Platinum (laser cannon). With No and Yes on the console, Yes takes the mineral from the hold and starts the repair; No, or too little in the hold, gives "Ceasing repair". Systems repair at the hull's pace, and when an item is whole the engineer moves to the next worst and asks again (`RepairButton.AskForRepair`, `PD_PlayerShip.m_repairItem`, saved; the hull in older saves). Worst first and one mineral per system were decided on 2026-10-11; the pairings are a design choice. Verified with the `systemrepairs` probe scenario; `m20` puts aluminum in the hold and answers yes.
- **Star Stability in the Star System Scan**: the original Analysis of a star gives its stability, with readings in the 1984 design notes (`starship.txt`): "STABLE [POSTFLARE]", "SLIGHTLY UNSTABLE" with more than 1000 Arth days to the next flare, and "UNSTABLE - ESTIMATED TIME TO FLARE XXX DAYS" with fewer. The port has no sensor reading of stars, so the line goes into the star system scan, its only star reading: "Stability: Unstable - estimated time to flare 284 days", or "Stable (post-flare)" after a flare (`GD_Star.GetStabilityText`). The text exists only in the 1984 notes; using it was decided on 2026-10-11. No star in the game data is more than 792 days from its flare, so "slightly unstable" never appears. Verified with the `starstability` probe scenario: its two checks fail on the old code and pass on the new.
- **Science Officer's Skill Decides the Analysis**: the crew's science skill did nothing. The 1984 design notes (`starship.txt`) give each header a chance of "the Science Officer's skill / 2" percent, with "unknown" after the header on failure; the object's class comes "regardless of the Sci Off skill"; an analyzed reading gets "I have already attempted analysis of this data, Captain"; and "alien ships may be read and analyzed as often as desired". The Analysis button now follows this: the object's class always, and every other line (a planet's orbit number, surface, gravity, atmosphere, temperature and weather; a ship's type and size) at that chance, else "Unknown", so skill 200 or more gives every line. A planet's reading is analyzed once (`SensorsDisplay.m_readingCount` counts readings); a new reading gives a new chance. With no science officer, or an incapacitated one, there is no analysis. The rule exists only in the 1984 notes; adopting it was decided on 2026-10-11. Not done: the notes' learning by use (a skill point per analysis; roadmap Phase 7). The `m7` and `cargo` probe scenarios now give their science officer full skill, since they check analysis lines. Verified with the `analysisskill` probe scenario: its three skill checks fail on the old code, and all five pass on the new.
- **Salvage From Debris**: the original vessel records (`STARB.COM`, in the extract under `Research/Data`) carry "Endurium Debris" and "Debris 1" to "Debris 3", the Endurium and up to three elements a destroyed ship leaves, held in the game data as `GD_Vessel.m_enduriumVolume` and `m_elementIdA` to `C` with their volumes, and the Thrynn say the Elowan "use plutonium to build their ships", salvageable from wrecks. The port had only placeholders: a "salvage potential" from the vessel's mass, and an analysis reading "Salvage collection not yet implemented". The ship now takes a wreck's debris aboard by flying within 64 units of it, once per wreck (`PD_AlienShip.m_salvaged`, saved), as much as the hold can take: "Salvaged from the wreck: 1.0 cubic meters of Endurium, 6.0 cubic meters of Lead, 4.0 cubic meters of Iron." for a Spemin scout, or "There is no room in the cargo hold for the debris". A wreck's sensor reading lists its makeup like a living vessel's; its analysis gives only the object, as in the 1984 notes ("DEBRIS - Nothing"). The sources say what a wreck leaves, not how it is collected, so the method is a design choice of 2026-10-11. Verified with the `salvage` probe scenario: its three salvage checks fail on the old code, and all five pass on the new.
- **Random Ruins**: the original kernel has a class of ruins, and its SEED-OV overlay seeds a planet's "mineral, lifeform & ruin" objects (`dir.txt`), but that code is not in the sources, and the port placed only the ruins of STRINFO's messages and artifacts. So 36 of the game data's 51 artifacts, those found nowhere else (Adrynna's Gold, the Silver Gadget, the Ticking Sphere...), could never be found. The 1984 design notes (`planet.txt`) describe random ruins: ancient crystal ruins holding "XXX lumps of Endurium" or one to four "truly amazing" artifacts (60%, 25%, 10% and 5%) worth 500 to 5000 MU, and Old Empire ruins with one to four "amazing" artifacts worth 50 to 1000 MU, the crew's estimate off by up to half. A quarter of planets now have one or two random ruins (from a hash of the planet's id, so the count is known without placing them: `TerrainRuins.GetRandomRuinCount`), placed with the planet's random numbers after everything else, so no deposit, rock, tree or ruin moves (planet 90's deposit checksum is unchanged). Half are ancient, and half of those hold 1 to 5 cubic meters of Endurium; the artifacts are those found nowhere else, at their own values within the ruin's range, which excludes A Mulligan, the Red Herring and the Terrain Vehicle Debris. The Cargo button beside a random ruin loads its contents into the terrain vehicle once, with the crew's words from the notes (`PD_PlanetSurfaces.m_takenRandomRuinItemList`, saved), and Scan labels a ruin that still holds something. Planets of STRINFO's messages and artifacts have none, as STRINFO describes them. The notes' Old Empire writings are not placed, as the notes give no texts. Ruins per planet, Endurium per ruin and the artifact choice are design choices; the notes give the rest. Deferred on 2026-10-09, the item was decided on 2026-10-11. Verified with the `randomruins` probe scenario: three of its four checks fail on the old code, and all four pass on the new.
- **Lava Destroys the Terrain Vehicle, and the Starport Replaces It**: STRINFO 1.2 has Interstel's supplemental evaluation "LOSS OF TERRAIN VEHICLE", which installs a new vehicle and fines 10,000 MU "DUE TO YOUR NEGLIGENCE", and the original kernel keeps "?TV is TVEHICLE available from ship?", but nothing in the port could lose the vehicle. The 1984 notes (`planet.txt`) give the cause: "If the TV moves into a lava terrain a warning message is given ('The heat from the lava is too much - we're burning up'). If the TV still does not move off it is destroyed." A molten planet's liquid is its lava, drawn red; a terrain vehicle in it now gets that warning, and if still there 3 seconds later (a design choice; the notes give no time) it is destroyed with its cargo (`TerrainVehicle.IsInLava`, `LoseToLava`), the crew returns to the ship, and the ship has no vehicle (`PD_TerrainVehicle.m_isLost`, saved): Disembark says "We have no terrain vehicle, Captain". At the Starport a new one is installed, the 10,000 MU fine goes into the bank ledger, and the evaluation appears on the Operations screen. The crew's unharmed return is a design choice of 2026-10-11. The notes' other hazards (storms, slow uphill driving) wait for roadmap 6.6. This covers roadmap 1.6 and the lava part of 6.6. Verified with the `lava` probe scenario: its loss checks fail on the old code (run before the warning step was added, which the old code, having no warning, cannot pass either), and all five pass on the new.
- **Uphill Driving Is Slow**: the terrain vehicle climbed as fast as it descended. The designers' 1985 list (`alpha.txt`), the 1986 kernel (an efficiency "for %eff in move") and the 1984 notes ("going uphill is very slow and inefficient", `planet.txt`) all have the slope slow it. The vehicle now measures the ground's rise 8 units ahead and slows with the steepness, down to a quarter speed (`TerrainVehicle.GetSlopeEfficiency`); downhill and level driving are unchanged. Its engines burn fuel by running time, so a slow climb costs more fuel per distance. The look-ahead, the slowdown and the minimum are design choices. Verified with the `uphill` probe scenario on planet 92: on the steepest slope near the landing site (a rise of 0.42), the vehicle covers 61% of its downhill distance in one second; on the old code it went as far or farther uphill, and the check fails.
- **Storms on the Surface**: the 1986 kernel has a STORM task and its data the original sky phrases, but the port's planets had no surface weather. The original was observed in a GOG copy of the game (planets 90 and 91 of Arth's system, about 50 game hours each) and is now followed (`PlanetWeather`): while the terrain vehicle is out, the sky is rolled every game hour and reported when it changes, in the original's words ("You are in a windstorm.", "It is hailing.", "You are in an electrical storm.", "It is hazy." and ten more), with no message when a storm starts or ends. A storm halves the vehicle's efficiency (EFF 14% to 7%, as in the original; half the speed and half the fuel's worth), the bearing to the ship reads LOST!, and in about half of storm hours a crew member "is injured" by 10 to 50 vitality (for a Human, scaled by durability), which can kill. Heavy snow costs some efficiency (EFF 11%) and the bearing but injures no one; aboard the ship nothing happens. Storms fill about half the hours on a Very Violent planet and one in eight where the weather is None, as observed; the classes between, and the sky phrases of unobserved surfaces (rock, liquid, molten), are design choices, since the original's probability table (STARB.COM, STORMS) could not be decoded. Look shows the sky too, like the original's planet description ("SKY: IT IS HAZY."). Verified with the `storms` probe scenario: of 300 hours on planet 91, 159 were storms (85 injured the crew, no other hour did) and 63 heavy snow; of 300 on planet 90, 42 were storms; a storm reads EFF 7% and LOST!, and the ship has no weather. Its storm checks fail on the old code.
- **Probe Captures Every Screen**: the probe rendered only the 3D camera, so no capture showed the console, displays or Starport panels. `screenpictures` and `starport-screenpictures` (run with `probe.ps1 -Graphics`) now render the full screen as the player sees it, every camera in depth order, for the star system, orbit and its sensors, the landing map, the terrain vehicle, hyperspace, an encounter, the Starport and its seven rooms, for comparison with the original's screens in `Research/Screenshots/` (roadmap Phase 7). Test tooling only.
- **Project Status, Counted**: the roadmap's "Where the port stands" table still described the port before Phases 1 to 6 (Repair a stub, no lifeforms, no story), and the README claimed "Completion: ~95%" with nothing behind it. The table now lists each area's state and the PRs behind it, and the README's figure counts roadmap items: 55 of 59 done, leaving storms, a play test of the early visual changes, and two fidelity pass items (every screen against the original, the planet drawn in encounters that begin in orbit). The Black Box, Hypercube and Ellipsoid row now records decision D5: no effect beyond their roles. Documentation only.
- **Aliens Name Their Captains and Ships**: 15 game data lines use `%` and `+` for the aliens' captain and ship names ("Me is naming captain % of most elegant and dangerous Veloxi ship naming +"), and the port showed them verbatim. STRINFO prints names there, with alternatives in brackets ("CAPTAIN FINOCTZI [XIKSIVE, POXTITSE] ... SHIP NAMING TOCHUPU [VOPUNOC, XXSH TZ]", the Thrynn's "CAPTAIN YRHEY [SS] OF THE THRYNN SHIP IART L"), so the original generated them from the race's syllables, which the game data holds as garble words: Fi-noc-tzi, To-chu-pu, Ksi-tox-phe, Yrh-ey, I-ar-t'l. The aliens now name captain and ship with two or three of their syllables (`Encounter.GetAlienName`), the same at every meeting of an encounter (from its own random numbers, so the game's do not shift): the Veloxi captain Ksipoxphe of the ship Fitse, for example. The syllable count is a design choice of 2026-10-11. Verified with the `aliennames` probe scenario: its two name checks fail on the old code, and all three pass on the new.
- **Planets With Life Now Have Lifeforms**: no planet had lifeforms. The 1986 kernel generated them in code missing from the sources (VITA-OV, HP-OV and LP-OV, `dir.txt`), so roadmap decision D3 (2026-10-11) rebuilds them from the 1984 design notes. The 35 planets with a bio density above 0 now have one to six species each, depending on how much life they hold (`GD_Species.ForPlanet`), each with the notes' species record: a body plan from the notes' icons (snakelike, bilateral with no legs to many, radial, amorphous, leaved plants small to large), a niche (producer, herbivore, carnivore, omnivore), size 1 to 9, up to 256 hit points, aggression and intelligence of 1 to 100, stun points and the stunner's effect (nothing, stuns, kills), a movement rate, flying or floating, a composition with carbon's place in it, and a colour. Species use their own random numbers seeded from the planet's id, leaving the game's untouched. Three to eight of each stand on dry ground near the equator, at the lowest of a few spots, as the manual (page 21) says they "tend to clump in the lower elevations near the equator or other temperate areas". They are placed after every other populator with the planet's seed, so no deposit, rock, tree or ruin moves (planet 90's deposit checksum is unchanged). Scan counts them and reads the nearest as in the notes: body plan and status, volume, "aggression analog" (Mouse to Tiger), "intelligence analog" (Insect to Human), niche and composition. For now lifeforms are inert plain shapes in their species' colour (procedural stand-ins until models land in a later PR); behaviour, the stunner and laser, capture and sale, and crew injuries follow (roadmap 6.3 to 6.5). Ranges and kinds the notes leave open are the port's. This is roadmap 6.1 plus the scan from 6.2. Verified with the `lifeforms` probe scenario: its three lifeform checks fail on the old code, and all four pass on the new.
- **Terrain Vehicle Laser and Stunner**: the Weapon button only reported "No hostile lifeforms detected". The manual (page 21) says "Your Terrain Vehicle is equipped with a laser and a stunner. You can choose which one to fire through the Weapons option"; the 1984 notes (`planet.txt`, `lf-words.txt`) add that the laser "uses 8 units of energy each time it is fired. The stunner uses 4", that it "does from 20 to 100 points of damage", that the stunner reaches "about 1/2 as far as the laser", and that "the effect of the stunner on any given species is drawn from that specie's Species record. The lifeform may either be 1) unaffected 2) stunned for some period of time or 3) killed", a stunner hit taking 10 to 50 stuns, with stuns below zero coming off hit points. Weapon now opens Fire, Switch (laser or stunner, `PD_TerrainVehicle.m_stunnerMounted`, saved) and Back (`ButtonSet.TerrainVehicleWeapon`). Fire hits the nearest living lifeform in the mounted weapon's range, costing 8 or 4 of the vehicle's 999 units of fuel. A lifeform out of hit points dies and lies on its side; a stunned one stays darker until its stuns recover. The original aims with the joystick; firing at the nearest lifeform, the ranges of 60 and 30 units and recovery of two stuns a second are the port's choices. This is the weapons part of roadmap 6.4. Verified with the `tvweapons` probe scenario: its five checks fail on the old code and pass on the new.
- **Realistic System Map**: the console's System Map drew the star, planets and ship as flat coloured dots on black, which looked "incredibly plain". Each planet is now drawn with its generated albedo map on a sphere lit by the star (day and night sides, a darker limb, atmosphere on the lit rim), sized by mass (18 to 34 map units, on the curve of `GD_Planet.GetScale`). The star gets a white-hot core, a corona and short rays in the colour of real stars of its class (decided on 2026-10-11, replacing the original game's class colours). Thin orbit rings brighten in a trail behind each planet, a faint band marks the class's ecosphere, a star field and faint nebula fill the background, and the ship is a chevron pointing along its heading. Orbits, positions and game data are unchanged. Four shaders (`Assets/Shaders/System Map *.shader`) draw it; the two textures come from the Blender 5.2 script `DevTools/SystemMap/build_system_map_art.py`, and `DevTools/SystemMap/wire.ps1` sets up the scene (see its README). The `systemmap` probe scenario fails 10 of its 12 checks on the old map and passes all of them on the new.
- **Planets Lit by Their Star**: the 3D star system view lit every planet with one white light high above the star, so each was lit from above wherever it orbited, and its atmosphere drew a full ring. Following look B (Realistic), chosen from mock-ups, each planet is now lit by its star, in the orbital plane and in the colour of real stars of its class (matching the system map and the sun), with day and night sides and an atmosphere glowing only on the sunlit edge. `StarSystem` creates a directional light, "Starlight", that lights only the star system's layer and points from the star toward the ship every frame; the scene's "Sunlight" still lights the ship from above so it stays easy to see. Verified with the `systemviewpictures` probe scenario: on the old code the light is white and 50 to 55 degrees off the star's direction (2 of 6 checks pass, the two controls); the new code passes 6 of 6. With `-Graphics` it renders the view beside the sun and each planet of Arth's system and star 156 into `Logs/screen-pictures/system-view/`. Mock-ups and before and after pictures are in `DevTools/SystemView/`.
- **A Milky Way Across the Sky**: the spaceflight sky held only the territory's nebula and the starfield. Following look B (Realistic) from the mock-ups, a faint Milky Way now crosses it beneath the nebula, with star clouds, dark dust lanes and a warmer bulge. It is a seamless cubemap made in Blender (`DevTools/SystemView/build_sky_art.py`, a latitude and longitude image computed on directions) that the skybox shader adds by pixel direction, so it turns with the sky in flight. Its strength, `StarflightSkybox.m_milkyWayStrength`, is 0.035 in the Spaceflight scene and zero in the Starport, which shares the skybox material. The look's multicoloured stars and a main view free of the "Regen Starfield" debug button already existed (#232, #240). Verified with the `systemviewpictures` probe scenario: the old code has no Milky Way (8 of 9 checks pass, the starfield colours and debug button being controls); the new code passes 9 of 9.
- **Orbit Paths and Engine Glow**: these complete look B (Realistic) for the 3D star system view. Each planet now traces a faint orbit path in its star's colour, brightest in a trail just behind it with a short glow ahead, so the planets' motion shows as on the system map. `Planet` builds a ring mesh in `Awake` and, when the planet is shown, places it round the star at the height of the planet's centre so the sphere hides the part behind it (`Assets/Resources/Shaders/Orbit Path.shader`, one material for all planets, angle and colour from a property block). The ship's engines glow soft blue at the exhaust, faint at rest and brighter with speed, with a slight pulse that stops while paused (`PlayerShip.UpdateEngineGlow`, `Assets/Resources/Shaders/Engine Glow.shader`); exhaust particles and contrails are unchanged. Ring meshes and the glow material are destroyed along with the objects that own them. Verified with the `systemviewpictures` probe scenario: on the old code no planet has an orbit path and the ship has no engine glow (9 of 12 checks pass); the new code passes 12 of 12.
- **Sun Gets a Living Surface**: the star system view's sun now comes alive, as requested on 2026-10-11. Its shine (`Shine.shader`, `Shine.cs`) has slowly churning cells on the outer disc (the centre stays white under the lens flare), flickering corona streamers round the rim, and every 6 to 14 seconds a small flare that bulges from a random point on the rim and falls back over 2.5 seconds. The flares are purely cosmetic: the shine's size, which grows as the game's flare nears, is still set by `StarSystem.Show`, and the sun uses its own `System.Random`, taking nothing from the game's `Random`. The `sunlife` probe scenario's flare check fails on the old sun and passes on the new; its two controls (the shine's size; nothing taken from `Random` and no per-frame allocation) pass on both.
- **Star System Sun in the Colours of Real Stars**: the 3D star system view's sun kept the original game's class colours (pure green for class A, pure yellow for class G), so it no longer matched the system map. Per the decision of 2026-10-11, `StarSystem.Show` now takes the map's colour (`SystemDisplay.GetStarColor`), and the shine shader (`Assets/Shaders/MaybeConvert/Shine.shader`) no longer gives every sun its gradient's orange rim: the gradient keeps the shape, and the colour runs from nearly white at the centre to a deeper shade of the star's own colour at the rim (orange for a G star, as before, deep blue for an A star). The `systemmap` probe scenario's two colour checks fail on the old code and pass on the new.
- **System Map Comes Alive**: the system map gains animations, as requested on 2026-10-11. The ship's chevron now has an engine glow at the back that pulses and brightens with speed, a wake of fading segments along its recent positions (gone once the ship stops, restarting after a jump), and every 4 seconds a sensor ping, a thin ring expanding from where the ship was and fading. The orbit trails shimmer gently toward their planets. Everything stops while paused and is hidden in orbit, and a map frame still allocates no memory. The map is now clipped to its display (`RectMask2D`) so a ping at the system's edge, where the ship arrives, does not spill over the console. Two sprites come from `DevTools/SystemMap/build_system_map_art.py`; `wire.ps1` sets up the objects. The `maplife` probe scenario fails 7 of its 8 checks on the old map and passes all of them on the new.
- **Lifeforms Respond to the Terrain Vehicle**: lifeforms stood still. The 1984 notes' expert system (`lf-behav.txt`, `lf-words.txt`) now runs every half second (`TerrainLifeform.Behave`) for each one in the encounter area, "twice the visible area". Level one reacts to weapons: a smart lifeform (intelligence 70 or more, aggression under 80) near a laser shot may lose 1 to 50 aggression, and a dumb one (intelligence under 35) hit by laser or stunner may gain 1 to 20, each at a 10% chance; plants are never angered. Level two responds to the player once a lifeform notices the vehicle (a small chance each step): a dangerous one (aggression over 65) approaches and attacks when adjacent, a wimpy one (under 35) flees, and any other mover wanders in its species' direction with some variation. Ground lifeforms keep to dry ground; flyers skim over anything. Attacks follow the notes: one to three (70%, 25%, 5%), each on a random crew member, hitting if a 1 to 100 roll is under the average of aggression and intelligence, for up to (aggression + 2 x hit points) / 3 / the crew member's durability (`GD_CrewRace.m_durability`) off their vitality. Messages read "Captain, we're being attacked!" and "The [lifeform] attacks [crew name]", then "and misses" or the vitality left (the original game's crew display shows vitality as a percentage). Not yet done: the notes' third level, a lifeform's mate (pairing, approaching, fleeing, attacking and eating it), crew deaths (roadmap 6.5) and capture. Distances, speeds and chances the notes omit are the port's. Verified with the `lifebehaviour` probe scenario: its three behaviour checks fail on the old code, and all four pass on the new.
- **Lifeforms That Float**: the notes' second air-ground transition was missing: "FLOAT <-- ?MOVING TRUE ?ON-GROUND TRUE ?CAN-FLOAT TRUE ?PRODUCER TRUE", and for an animal "?WANTS-TO-FLOAT TRUE" (lf-behav.txt; planet.txt has a "Floating (plant or animal)" icon). Floating plants now drift (movement rate 1, the port's choice; they had the draw but never moved, so never floated) and rise as they move, and a tenth of non-flying animals can float, drawn from separate random numbers so every planet's other species are unchanged (`GD_Species.m_canFloat`). A moving floater rises 3 units (flyers fly at 8), crosses anything including liquids, and lands by a flyer's LAND rules: when wandering and inclined to, when attacking, and at once when stunned or dead (`TerrainLifeform.IsAirborne`). As planet.txt says, "Airborne creatures may not be dealt with by the player": weapons and the Cargo button now skip flyers and floaters (`TVFireButton.FindNearestLifeform`, `TerrainLifeforms.FindLifeformBeside`), where before they hit and took flyers in the air. Verified with the `floaters` probe scenario: its three checks fail on the old code and pass on the new, its control (a stunned floater comes down and can be fired on) passes on both, and the ten other lifeform scenarios, `flyers` among them, pass.
- **Crew Members Can Die**: lifeform attacks lowered vitality, but nobody died at 0. Every injury now goes through `PD_CrewAssignment.InjureCrewMember`, and at 0 vitality the crew member dies, as in the 1984 notes (`starship.txt`): "X is dead!", the personnel file is saved as dead (`PD_PersonnelFile.m_isDead`, false in older saves) and shows DEAD beside the name ("his file has a DEAD stamp"). A dead captain's command passes down the notes' chain of command (science officer, navigator, engineer, communications officer, doctor) to the first survivor, with "Y takes command." When the last crew member dies, the game ends with the same game over as the ship's destruction (`CombatController.EndGame`, "The entire crew has died."), armor at 0 so the lost game is never saved. The doctor's treatment already stopped at 0 vitality, and the Docking Bay already refused to launch with a dead crew member. Verified with the `crewdeath` probe scenario: both checks fail on the old code and pass on the new.
- **Lifeforms Hunt, Flee and Eat One Another**: lifeforms ignored each other. The third level of the 1984 notes' expert system (`lf-behav.txt`, `lf-words.txt`) now runs whenever a lifeform has nothing to do about the player: each living lifeform in the encounter area pairs with the nearest unpaired one of any species (the notes do not say which) and acts on that mate by the notes' rules (`TerrainLifeform.RespondToMate`). It approaches a mate it can eat (herbivore or omnivore to a plant, carnivore or omnivore to a herbivore at most 2 sizes larger, ultra-aggressive meat eater to another), bites it when adjacent for 1 to 40 hit points (ATTACK-ITS-MATE with the mate's REDUCE-HITS-BY-1-TO-40), and at 10% a step eats a bitten plant or a dead mate, which disappears (`TerrainLifeforms.RemoveEaten`). A lifeform approached by its mate flees; one that is bitten fights back with aggression 40 or more, or breaks away with a 10% chance. Pairs break when the mate leaves the area or is eaten, and at random (2% a step, the port's choice). The notes set no precedence between rules: eating is tried before biting, so a grazer can eat a plant it has bitten. Pairs, like lifeforms, are rebuilt on every visit, so anything eaten is back. Verified with the `mates` probe scenario: its four checks fail on the old code and pass on the new.
- **Recording Lifeform Data**: the manual (page 21) has Cargo "make holographic recordings of lifeforms", and the 1986 kernel keeps BIO-DATA objects, but Cargo beside a lifeform only listed the hold. It now offers the lifeform as the 1984 notes do (`planet.txt`): "A lifeform.", or for a dead one "A dead lifeform. Shall we record it in our data banks?", with Record and Ignore (`ButtonSet.TerrainVehicleLifeform`). Recording takes about 3 seconds and 2 units of vehicle energy, shows "Recording..." and ends with "Data collection completed, sir."; a lifeform that wanders off first "got away", and each attack delays it by a second (`TerrainLifeforms.StartRecording`). The data earns the notes' value (`PD_General.LearnLifeform`, `GD_Species.GetDataValue`): 50 for a class's first, 20 for a new order, 5 for a new species, nothing for one already recorded, plus bonuses for intelligence, 25% or 50% for herbivores or meat eaters, volume, and 50%, 30% or 10% for carbon missing, third or second in the composition; dead ones earn half. The notes give no taxonomy, so body plan families serve as classes and body plans as orders. Interstel pays on return to the Starport ("Lifeform data" in the bank). Capture into stasis and specimen sales come next. Verified with the `lifeformdata` probe scenario: its five checks fail on the old code and pass on the new.
- **Capturing Lifeforms**: the manual (page 21) has Cargo pick up "any item (including lifeforms)" beside the terrain vehicle, and the 1986 kernel keeps specimen objects, but lifeforms could only be recorded. Cargo beside a living lifeform now offers Capture, Record and Ignore, as in the 1984 notes (`planet.txt`: "<-- IGNORE CAPTURE RECORD -->"), and beside a dead one only Record and Ignore (`ButtonSet.TerrainVehicleDeadLifeform`). Capture takes about 6 seconds and no energy, angers the lifeform ("Capturing raises the aliens EDL": by 1 to 20, the port's choice), and fails or waits like a recording if it escapes or attacks. Then comes "The alien lifeform has been captured and put in stasis. Now loading into T.V." and, with room, "The lifeform is stored as item # N": it moves from the surface to the vehicle's hold as a specimen (`PD_Specimen`, `PD_SpecimenStorage`, saved, empty in older saves) worth five times its data value. Without room it stays put. Back aboard, specimens join the ship's hold with the rest of the cargo; the Trade Depot lists them under Specimens and buys them at that value ("Captured specimens may be sold in the trade depot along with minerals"), and the vehicle's cargo display lists them too. Verified with the `capture` and `starport-specimens` probe scenarios: their seven checks fail on the old code and pass on the new.
- **Look Describes Nearby Lifeforms**: the terrain vehicle's Look described the planet's surface, weather, atmosphere and gravity, but nothing near the vehicle. As in the 1984 notes (`planet.txt`, LOOK: "a description of the icon the eyeball is over is given"), it now describes the nearest lifeform within 45 units (the notes' "within 5 squares"): body plan, size and colour in words (`GD_Species.GetSizeText`, `GetColourText`), "any current Movement behavior" (wandering, approaching, fleeing or attacking the vehicle, or stalking, fleeing or attacking another lifeform: `TerrainLifeform.GetMovementText`) and whether it is alive, stunned or dead. Minerals, ruins and dropped cargo within reach get the notes' lines: "An outcropping of minerals. Possibly incredibly valuable and useful.", "A very ancient looking crystalline structure, beautiful and mysterious.", "Ruins from the period of the Old Empire." (the notes' eight phrases for recent ruins are not in the sources) and "Ex-Cargo (good with garlic and butter sauce)." The original aims Look with a cursor; the port picks the nearest. Verified with the `look` probe scenario: its three checks fail on the old code and pass on the new.
- **Lifeforms Get Their Models**: lifeforms were plain shapes (pillar, ball, box or capsule). Each is now its body plan's model (#178); a plant takes one of the two models for its size, chosen by its planet and its place among the planet's species so no random number is drawn (`TerrainLifeforms.GetModelName`). `DevTools/LifeformModels/wire.ps1` builds each model's two SF - Standard materials from its baked maps (via the material Inspector's own keyword logic, `SFShaderGUI.MaterialChanged`), sets up its import and saves a prefab under `Assets/Resources/Lifeforms` for the populator to load. The skin takes the species colour through a material property block while details (eyes, claws, horns, wood, pods) keep theirs; dead lifeforms darken all over. A model stands on its base, is rotated by its map position and turns to face where it goes; a dead one lies on its side, settled on the ground. Without a model the plain shape stands in, and its pillars and capsules no longer sink halfway into the ground (they are two units tall, not one). Verified with the `lifeformmodels` probe scenario: its four checks fail on the old code and pass on the new. `lifeformpictures`, run with `probe.ps1 -Graphics`, renders planet 92's game view with every model, plus a close-up of each, into `Logs/lifeform-pictures/`.
- **Two Probe Scenarios Updated for the Engineer's Minerals**: the full run after #147 found `m11` still expecting a free repair of a bare hull; it now puts aluminum in the hold, answers the engineer's question as `m20` does, and accepts the new "No repairs are required". The `nebula` scenario read messages only once after entering the nebula's star system, so a radar message could push the nebula's message out of the list first; it now checks every frame's messages. Test tooling only.
- **Probe Scenario m12 Restores the Laser Cannon First**: since hits damage ship systems (#145), the preceding Uhlek attack could destroy the laser cannon, so the shot at the Enterprise never fired and the check that it shoots back failed (seen in a run on 2026-10-11; the next run's log showed the cannon at 0 health). The scenario now logs the cannon's health and restores the systems before that shot. Verified: `m12` passes 7 of 7.
- **Home Fleets Warn Ships Away From Their Homeworlds**: the game data has 22 homeworld lines for the Spemin, Thrynn and Veloxi, which STRINFO (2.2, 2.5, 2.7) places "while in orbit of" the homeworld, but every comm skipped them, so the home fleets in orbit (encounters 139, 165 and 233) hailed with ordinary lines and never attacked. A home fleet now says only homeworld lines: hails and statements from its homeworld hails, replies to the player's hail from its homeworld responses, and answers to questions from its refusals ("Us are not answer the questions"). Each list runs in game data order, and each warning steps one further than the last, whichever list it comes from: the Thrynn go from "You are within the limits of our homeworld territory" to "We now give additional warning" to "You must pay the consequences". A home fleet asks no questions, holds fire while warning, and after a list's last line ends the comm link and attacks, as the lines say. That the attack follows the final warning is inferred from the lines; the sources give no timing, and warnings come at the aliens' usual pace of one every 10 to 30 seconds. The Gazurtoid fleet has no homeworld lines and is unchanged. These races' surrender lines are not used yet (roadmap 2.2). Verified with the `homeworlds` probe scenario: its three home fleet checks fail on the old code, and all four pass on the new.
- **Spemin, Thrynn and Veloxi Surrender After Heavy Losses**: STRINFO 2.7 says "after some of their ships are destroyed, the Spemin will act cowardly" and lists their lines: the game data's obsequious Spemin lines, with the surrender of 296 ("We surrender! We surrender! Please do not hurt us"). The game data also has Thrynn and Veloxi surrender lines that STRINFO does not place; on 2026-10-11 it was decided they surrender after losses the same way. None of these lines were ever said: the three races fought to the last ship. Once they have lost half the ships that entered the encounter (two of three, one of two) with one still alive, they now act cowardly (`PD_Encounter.m_aliensAreCowardly`, saved; `Encounter.UpdateCowardice`), staying obsequious and holding fire even if the player fires again. Their surrender line for what they are saying comes first (the Thrynn's is a neutral reply to a hail: "You have the advantage of us"), then their obsequious lines: the Spemin's "Oh! Flash of inspiration! We now see that you are right", the Veloxi's "Veloxi are actually most reasonable", and neutral lines for a race with none (the Thrynn). Questioned, they answer with what they know, as STRINFO says the Spemin "offer information"; with no obsequious answers in the game data, the neutral ones are given, as before. How many ships make "some" is the port's choice. The Spemin's "we lay down our powerful defensive screens" stays a line, as Spemin ships have no shields in the game data. A home fleet never turns cowardly; it only warns. Not yet done: alien moods before the losses (roadmap 2.1). Verified with the `cowardice` probe scenario: its six surrender checks fail on the old code, and all seven pass on the new.
- **Elowan Give Energy to a Crew in Need**: STRINFO 2.4 has the Elowan ask "Is thine need for energy dire?" with two answers to a yes: "answering yes when carrying less than 20 units of Endurium" earns "We give unto thee, friends of the Elowan, 15 Arth units of fuel", and with 20 or more "Thy need does not seem dire. Thou would'st speak falsely and take advantage on us". The three lines exist in the game data (comms 4 to 6, subject Custom) but were never said. It is now the Elowan's first question in an encounter, asked once (`Encounter.FindSpecialQuestion`, `PD_Encounter.m_specialQuestionAsked`, saved). A yes with under 20 cubic meters of Endurium in the hold earns 15 cubic meters, as much as the hold can take; a yes with 20 or more earns their reproach and nothing; a no earns nothing. An Arth unit is read as a cubic meter, as in the Starport's prices. The timing is the port's choice: STRINFO is silent, and the first question is the one moment every Elowan conversation has. Not yet done: the Elowan refusing to deal with a ship that has a Thrynn aboard (roadmap 2.4). Verified with the `elowangift` probe scenario: its three gift checks fail on the old code, and all four pass on the new.
- **Veloxi Demand Tribute, the Crystal Orb and the Ellipsoid**: STRINFO 2.2 has the Veloxi demand "a tribute of 3 Arth energy crystals" and, when the hold has them, the "most precious Small Egg of Veloxi" (the Crystal Orb) and the "focusing stone of Veloxi" (the Ellipsoid), where "answering no or terminating communications with no answer will result in a Veloxi attack"; the designers' 1985 list says "the veloxi always ask for tribute" (`alpha.txt`). The demands exist in the game data (comms 397 to 399, the tribute's amount written as X) but were never made. The demand is now the Veloxi's first question in an encounter, most precious first: the Crystal Orb, else the Ellipsoid, else the tribute with its 3 filled in. A yes hands over the artifact (`PD_PlayerShip.RemoveArtifact`) or 3 cubic meters of Endurium, the tribute drawing "You is cooperating with Veloxi jim dandy" (comm 415). A no, or closing the comm link unanswered, brings an attack: they end the link and turn hostile. By decision of 2026-10-11, the same applies to the tribute, and a yes with under 3 cubic meters of Endurium also brings an attack. The line saying the Veloxi take an artifact aboard is the port's own, as the sources have none. Verified with the `veloxdemands` probe scenario: its five checks fail on the old code and pass on the new.
- **Thrynn Now Trade for Endurium**: STRINFO 2.5 says "the Thrynn will offer to purchase artifacts for Endurium" at their own prices (table 1.3.1, `GD_Artifact.m_thrynnPrice`, previously unused), "will offer to purchase plutonium for Endurium", and sell the Black Box, "a device that will make your ship invulnerable", for "only 30 of your energy crystals". These four lines and the Thrynn's two answers ("You do not have enough. Do not try to deceive us." and "Very well.") are comms 299 to 303 in the game data, but the port never used them. The Thrynn's first question in an encounter is now an offer: for the artifact in the hold they value most, named and priced in their line; otherwise for the hold's plutonium, in whole cubic meters; otherwise the Black Box, if the ship lacks one. A yes completes the trade, paid in as much Endurium as the hold can take, and gets "Very well."; a yes to the Black Box with under 30 cubic meters of Endurium gets "You do not have enough"; a no gets "Very well." and no trade. They pay one cubic meter of Endurium per cubic meter of plutonium, a design decision of 2026-10-11 based on STRINFO's "the amount of Endurium offered varies directly with the amount of plutonium available". Which artifact they pick among several, and "Very well." after a trade as well as a refusal, are the port's own choices. Verified: all five `thrynntrades` probe checks fail on the old code and pass on the new.
- **Aliens Have Moods**: the original kernel tracks an "alien's emotional disposition level" (EDL, `disys.txt`) alongside posture, and the manual says "statements are more likely to affect the aliens' attitudes than are questions" (page 17), yet the port's aliens had no mood. Only special rules changed their stance, so most hostile and obsequious lines went unheard, and a Spemin met in peace, having no neutral statements, showed the word ERROR. On 2026-10-11 it was decided to rebuild the model of the 1984 design notes (`compart.txt`, which gives the rules but no numbers), with numbers drawn from each race's character in STRINFO. The Elowan, Thrynn, Spemin and Veloxi, the races with lines in more than one stance, now have a personality (`AlienPersonality`): a starting mood from 0 (calm) to 100 (agitated) (Elowan, Thrynn and Veloxi 45, Spemin 55); three thresholds for friendly, diplomatic, hostile or obsequious, and too agitated to talk; the mood effect of each player posture; and an aggression level. A statement shifts the mood by the effect of the player's posture, a question by half that, and each further phrase in the same posture by less ("DELTA-EDL = PPE * [1/(#-THIS-POSTURE + 1)]"). Between the hostile threshold and "too agitated", the notes' strength differential picks hostile or obsequious: the player's strength minus the aliens', weighted by aggression / 5, with strength taken as the ships' armor and shield points (the port's choice). The mood sets the posture of their lines (`Encounter.GetAlienTalkStance`) and holds their fire: a hostile posture means abusive talk, not an attack. Too agitated to talk, they end the comm link and fight, or take flight from a stronger ship without firing (though they hold their position, also the port's choice). As the notes describe, they also tire of talking after a number of phrases picked for their posture when the link opens (obsequious 10 to 20, friendly 5 to 15, diplomatic 3 to 8, hostile 2 to 5) rather than after 2 to 5 minutes. The earlier stance rules (fired on, cowardly, a home fleet, Elan destroyed, a refused Veloxi demand) still apply. Not yet done: the notes' mood effect of each answer to an alien's question (the game data gives the phrases none), phrase intervals and talkativeness. Aliens with no line for a posture now say nothing instead of showing ERROR. The `m12` probe scenario now checks that an unprovoked Spemin is not hostile, rather than neutral by name, since they grovel before a strong ship. Verified: the six mood checks of the `mood` probe scenario fail on the old code and pass on the new, and its control (aliens with no farewell line still ending a conversation when their time is up) caught a regression in this change before commit.
- **Elowan and Thrynn Hold a Crew's Friendships Against It**: STRINFO 2.4 and 2.5 give each rival race's lines for a crew friendly with the other. The Elowan say "We dost detect the presence of accursed Thrynn aboard thy vessel. We wouldst have no dealings with thee" when a Thrynn is in the crew, and "thou art friends of the evil Thrynn. Therefore art thou enemies of the Elowan" when the crew contacted the Thrynn first. The Thrynn say "So you are friends with the Elowan... Most regrettable" when an Elowan is aboard, and "Unless you change your mind we must consider you our enemy" when the crew met the Elowan first. All four lines were in the game data (comms 1, 2, 297 and 298) but never used. Which of the two races the crew first opens a comm link with is now saved for the whole game (`PD_General.m_firstContactRace`). A crew friendly with the other race, through a crew member aboard or that first contact, hears the race's line for it once per encounter in place of a greeting, and the race starts in its hostile band (the moods of #157): its abusive lines come out and a few hostile phrases make it fight, though friendly talk can still calm it. With a Thrynn aboard, the Elowan say their line and end the comm link for the encounter without attacking. What follows each line is a design decision of 2026-10-11, as STRINFO gives only the lines. Under the same decision, a crew member of the aliens' own race, anywhere in the crew, makes all their words understood: "all phrases will be un-scrambled if any member of the player's crew is the same race as the race of the alien" (the 1984 notes, `compart.txt`), whereas the designers' 1985 list has the communications officer's skill and race affect translation (`alpha.txt`). Verified: all five `rivals` probe checks fail on the old code and pass on the new.
- **Minstrels, Nomad Probes and the Noah 9 Derelict Transmit**: STRINFO 2.8 to 2.11 describe what these races send once answered: the Minstrels' seven verses ("...through the windless night we sail"), the Nomad probe's planetary data after "Commencing transmission of planetary data", the Noah 9 derelict's recorded message "upon acknowledging the distress signal", and the Mysterions' binary signal. The game data stores these as the race's lines for having nothing more to say (subject 5), which the port used only as a random reply to a question it had no answer for. Once the comm link is up, these races now send their lines in game data order, one each time they speak, and end the link after the last (`Encounter.IsTransmitter`, `FindNextTransmission`). The derelict never talked: it fell through to the race switch's default case, which only shoots back. With its own case and conversation timers it now sends its beacon ("Captain, we're picking up a distress beacon signal", "SOS... SOS") and, once answered, both parts of Captain Smelenuf's message. Its first comm link exposed a fault no other race could reach: `Encounter.Connect` and `Disconnect` looked up the comm screen's race model by race number without a bounds check, and the scene's list has no slot for races after the Minstrels, so the link threw an IndexOutOfRangeException. Both now use `GetAlienRaceModel`, which returns null for a race without a slot. Verified: all three `transmissions` probe checks fail on the old code and pass on the new.
- **Optional World-Event Prototype**: an experimental, editor-only prototype for generating world events with Microsoft Semantic Kernel (`Microsoft.SemanticKernel.Core` 1.80.1, installed by NuGetForUnity 4.5.0 from `Assets/packages.config`). Its chat service (`ClaudeCliChatService`) drives a language model through a command line client instead of an API key. The prompt goes to the process on standard input and every argument is fixed text, so nothing a player or the model writes is parsed by `cmd.exe`. Because the command line offers no function calling that Semantic Kernel can see, the service implements it: the offered kernel functions go into the system prompt, `--json-schema` makes the model answer with either a final text or tool calls, and the service invokes the calls and loops, for at most five rounds. `FunctionChoiceBehavior.Auto()` replaces the deprecated, connector-specific `ToolCallBehavior.AutoInvokeKernelFunctions`. `MetagameOrchestrator.TriggerWorldSimulation()` asks for a border dispute; the model calls `WorldState-generate_faction_event`, which decides the outcome and logs it on the main thread through `MainThreadDispatcher`, and then narrates the result. The code lives in two assembly definitions under `Assets/Scripts/AI/` that compile only with the `STARFLIGHT_SEMANTIC_KERNEL` define, which `SemanticKernelDefineSync` sets once the packages are in `Assets/Packages/`, so the project keeps compiling before they are restored. Editor menus under **Starflight Remake > AI** add the orchestrator to the active scene and import the crate that `DevTools/Blender/make_scifi_crate.py` exports (as a prefab placed at the origin), and `Assets/UI/EventLogBg.svg` is a background for an event log panel. Verified: in CI, NuGetForUnity restored the packages, `SemanticKernelDefineSync` added the define, and Unity compiled `Starflight.AI` and `Starflight.AI.Editor` with no errors and no warnings from the new files; the tool-call protocol was exercised end to end against the command line client. In the Unity Editor on Windows the same restore, define and compile went through, and in play mode in `Assets/Scenes/AI Sandbox.unity` (a test scene for this prototype, not in Build Settings) the model called `WorldState-generate_faction_event` and narrated its outcome in about 36 seconds, with no errors or warnings. The restored packages, the define, the crate exported by Blender 5.0.1 and its prefab are committed. The first restore logs that two source generators in `analyzers/` folders (`System.Text.Json.SourceGeneration`, `Microsoft.Extensions.Logging.Generators`) will not be loaded; NuGetForUnity configures those as analyzers, not assemblies, and the message does not return on the next compile. The Blender script no longer reads `Material.use_nodes`, which logs a deprecation warning in Blender 5.
- **Planet Scans Show the Planet**: the sensor window filled a scanned planet's disc with fine colour noise that looked like static (the original draws random EGA pixels there). As the scan runs, the noise now clears into the planet's own surface: the albedo, water and normal maps the planet generator made for it, on a lit sphere tilted slightly toward the viewer and turning once every two minutes, with a rim in its atmosphere's colour and a glint on its water (option A of four previews, chosen on 2026-10-11). A new UI shader (`Assets/Shaders/Sensor Planet.shader`, material `Sensor Planet`, `SensorsDisplay.m_planetMaterial`) draws it inside the planet mask's disc, which keeps the size the original gives it by mass. A planet whose maps are still being made or could not be made, or whose maps are destroyed while shown (the ship has left the star system), keeps or returns to the noise. The `sensorplanet` probe scenario covers it (8 of its 13 checks fail on the old code).
- **UI Kit Foundations**: the foundations of the modern sci-fi UI kit designed in Figma (chosen on 2026-10-11) are now in the project, with nothing on screen changed yet. Its fonts sit in `Assets/Fonts` with their SIL Open Font License files: Orbitron Medium and Bold (theleagueof/orbitron; the kit's SemiBold heading uses Bold, as Orbitron has no static SemiBold), Rajdhani Medium and SemiBold, and Share Tech Mono (google/fonts). `Starflight Remake > UI Kit > Rebuild` (`UIKitBuilder`) builds their TextMesh Pro font assets: 1024 x 1024 atlases filled with ASCII, Latin-1 and the symbols the game shows, then made static so play never rewrites them. `UIKitTheme` (`Resources/UI Kit Theme`, `UIKitTheme.Get()`) holds the kit's colour roles, eight text styles (`TextStyle.Apply`) and six white sliced sprites (`Assets/UI Kit/Sprites`: panel, outline, glow, bar, diamonds) drawn at double size. Later PRs move the screens to the kit.

### Changed

- **Compile Check Pins the game-ci CLI**: the GameCI test runner queried GitHub's API for the latest game-ci CLI release on every run, and a 403 from that lookup failed master's run after #350 before Unity was reached (no login, no compile). The workflow now pins v0.1.72, the release every passing run used, which downloads without the API (`cliVersion` in `.github/workflows/compile-check.yml`).
- **Compile Check Moves to Newer Checkout and Cache Actions**: Dependabot's first update moves `actions/checkout` from v4.4.0 to v7.0.1 (`3d3c42e`) and `actions/cache` from v4.3.0 to v6.1.0 (`55cc834`), both still pinned to release commits; `game-ci/unity-test-runner` stays on v4.4.0, as its v5 is a beta. No breaking change reaches this workflow: both now run on Node 24, which the GitHub-hosted `ubuntu-latest` runner has; checkout v6 keeps the git credentials in their own file, and the only later git step reads the local tree; checkout v7 refuses fork pull requests under `pull_request_target` and `workflow_run`, and this workflow runs on `pull_request`; cache v6 keeps its inputs.
- **Dependabot Keeps the Pinned Actions Current**: actions pinned to commits no longer follow their releases, so `.github/dependabot.yml` now has Dependabot check the workflows' actions weekly and open one pull request (commit prefix `ci`) that moves the pins and their `# vX.Y.Z` comments. Unity packages stay out of it and are still updated by hand, one per branch.
- **Compile Check Actions Pinned to Commits**: `.github/workflows/compile-check.yml` ran `actions/checkout@v4`, `actions/cache@v4` and `game-ci/unity-test-runner@v4`, tags that can be moved to other code, in a job that holds the `UNITY_*` secrets. Each now names the commit its `v4` tag points to today, with the release in a comment (checkout v4.4.0 `11d5960`, cache v4.3.0 `0057852`, unity-test-runner v4.4.0 `32e5771`), so CI runs unchanged code. Hardening raised by the security review of 2026-10-13, not an exploitable finding.
- **Combat Wounds the Crew and Reports Hits as in the Original**: combat never hurt the crew (only lifeforms and weather called `InjureCrewMember`), and the text window showed the port's own numbers ("Shields absorb 10 damage!", "Hull takes 20 damage!") and warnings ("Incoming torpedo!", "Enemy laser fire!", "Plasma bolt incoming!"). As in the original's DAMAGE-OV, a hit through the shields now lands on one of the ship's fields: its systems or its crew (the seventh of eight). A crew hit wounds each living crew member with a 70% chance, by max(5, a part of the hit / the race's durability / 10), reported with Examine's bands: "Data is moderately wounded." (deaths as before; with nobody left the game is lost). Hits are named in the original wording, "Missile class 2" for a missile, "Plasma bolt" for a plasma bolt, nothing for a laser, and the damage numbers are gone (the hull breach warning stays). A system's share of a hit is unchanged (decided on 2026-10-11), but systems are picked slightly less often now that the crew is a field (`CombatController.WoundCrew`, `PD_PlayerShip.GetInstalledSystemCount`). Verified: the `combatwounds` probe scenario's three checks fail on the old code and pass on the new; `colournames` now checks its colour tag on the hull breach warning; the combat, damage and crew scenarios pass.
- **Unity Editor 6000.3.13f1**: `ProjectSettings/ProjectVersion.txt` moves from 6000.3.2f1 to 6000.3.13f1 (8c4f11e4fb20). The project compiles on 6000.3.13f1 in CI with no errors.
- **Textured Models for the Thrynn Ships**: the Thrynn transport, scout and warship (vessels 8, 9 and 10), procedural low-poly stand-ins in flat colours, are now textured models, the first race in the plan of 2026-10-13 to remake the game's models. Each began as an approved concept picture from the original's sensor picture and silhouette, then became a textured 3D model with 4K maps, remeshed to about 10,000 triangles. `DevTools/ShipModels/import_meshy.py` rotates and scales them like the build scripts (still the STRINFO size rule's length, longest along the nose), gives the rear backward-facing faces a glowing engine material, converts metallic/roughness maps to SF - Standard's albedo and specular/smoothness maps plus an emissive map for the blue crystal, and writes 1024 pixel JPEG maps (about 1.4 MB per ship) and the manifest; `meshy-thrynn.json` records each ship's sources, and the debris script rebuilt the wrecks. `ShipModelWiring.cs` applies a manifest's maps to its materials, and the review sheet can render with Cycles (`ship_kit.preview_sheet`). `build_thrynn.py` is removed, since running it would restore the stand-ins.
- **Textured Model for the Player's Arth Ship**: the hand-made Arth ship rendered plain white (its hull materials had a texture but not the keyword enabling it). It is now a textured model like the Thrynn ships: a bare hull from a concept picture based on the original's Ship Configuration picture and the old model's layout, plus a sheet of parts in its style (a cargo pod, the laser turret, a missile launcher), each approved. The game shows equipment through the old model's nodes, so `DevTools/ShipModels/import_meshy_arth.py` assembles both models under the old tree (same node names and units, verified by round-tripping the old model through the same export): the hull at the old hull's place and length, the turret on the fork's hub, a launcher on each engine arm, and the 16 cargo pods in four rings of four around the new spine, thicker and shorter than the old. The emissive map lights the windows, nozzles and canopy, the ship's only lights in hyperspace. `ShipModelWiring.cs` now handles the player ship: it removes the 227 material overrides on the five scene copies (Spaceflight, Starport and Test), moves the two contrails to the new nozzles and imports the rebuilt wreck at the ship's size. The old model's nine unused materials and four textures are removed (`Arth Ship Barrel.mat` stays for the terrain vehicle). The `shippictures` probe scenario checks every mesh, pods and weapons included, for its maps (failing on the old model) and photographs the ship under `-Graphics`.
- **Textured Models for the Elowan Ships**: the Elowan transport, scout and warship (vessels 5, 6 and 7), flat-coloured procedural stand-ins, are now textured models made like the Thrynn ones: approved concept pictures from the original's sensor pictures and silhouettes in one style for the plant race (grown green hulls of overlapping leaves, cyan bioluminescent strips along the booms, engines like red seed pods), turned into textured 3D models remeshed to about 10,000 triangles and imported by `DevTools/ShipModels/import_meshy.py` (`meshy-elowan.json`). Each has the STRINFO size rule's length (scout and warship both at the 0.3 floor), is longest along its nose, and has its engines at the back and its blue and cyan strips lit. The debris script rebuilt the wrecks, `build_elowan.py` and the four old flat Elowan materials are removed, and `shippictures` now checks the Elowan ships' maps too.
- **Textured Models for the Veloxi Ships**: the Veloxi transport, scout, warship and drone (vessels 11, 12, 13 and 18), flat-coloured procedural stand-ins, are now textured models made like the Thrynn and Elowan ones. The concept pictures, from the original's sensor pictures and silhouettes, share one style for the insect-like race (glossy dark bronze chitin plates, long red and pink engine pods, a cockpit and sensor ball of faceted blue crystal at the tip of a forward boom); the warship's was edited once because its wing came out as a closed hoop. Approved in advance on 2026-10-14, they became textured 3D models remeshed to about 10,000 triangles (6,000 for the small drone), imported by `DevTools/ShipModels/import_meshy.py` (`meshy-veloxi.json`) at the STRINFO size rule's length, longest along the nose, engines at the back. A race file's glow can now list several hue ranges, so the Veloxi light both their blue crystal and their red and pink pods (red wraps around hue 0). The debris script rebuilt the wrecks, `build_veloxi.py` and the five old flat Veloxi materials are removed, and `shippictures` now checks the Veloxi ships' maps too (those four checks fail on the old models; all 11 pass now).
- **Textured Models for the Gazurtoid Ships**: the Gazurtoid scout and warship (vessels 14 and 15), flat-coloured procedural stand-ins, are now textured models made like the other races' ships: concept pictures from the original's sensor pictures and silhouettes in one style for the huge water-dwelling race (a ragged mass of wet dark stone and coral-like growths bristling with spines, blue and cyan lights in recesses, a glowing red and pink core), approved in advance on 2026-10-14, then textured 3D models remeshed to about 10,000 triangles and imported by `DevTools/ShipModels/import_meshy.py` (`meshy-gazurtoid.json`) at the STRINFO size rule's length (60 and 90 times an Interstel ship). The warship model's core faced sideways, so a race file can now roll a model about its length (`roll`). The debris script rebuilt the wrecks, `build_gazurtoid.py` and the four old flat Gazurtoid materials are removed, and `shippictures` now checks the Gazurtoid ships' maps too.
- **Textured Models for the Uhlek Ships**: the Uhlek scout and warship (vessels 16 and 17), flat-coloured procedural stand-ins, are now textured models made like the other races' ships: concept pictures from the original's sensor pictures and silhouettes in one style for the ruthless hive race (a sinister hull of dark violet and black chitin, the scout a ring of pincers open at the bow and the warship an outer and inner pair, around a core with blue and cyan lights and a glowing red heart at the back), approved in advance on 2026-10-14, then textured 3D models remeshed to about 10,000 triangles and imported by `DevTools/ShipModels/import_meshy.py` (`meshy-uhlek.json`) at the STRINFO sizes. The scout model's ring was round, where the original's is an oval longer than wide, so a race file can now narrow a model across its nose (`widthScale`, 0.85 for the scout), keeping every ship longest along its nose as the `shipmodels` scenario checks. The debris script rebuilt the wrecks, `build_uhlek.py` and the four old flat Uhlek materials are removed, and `shippictures` now checks the Uhlek ships' maps too.
- **Textured Models for the Noah 9 Derelict and the Enterprise**: the last two procedural stand-ins (vessels 23 and 21) are now textured models, so every alien vessel that had a stand-in has one.
