The Noah 9 derelict is an ancient human colony transport, dead for centuries (battered, dented and scorched cargo and habitat modules behind a small nose module); STRINFO gives it no energy, so nothing on it glows: a race file can now declare a ship without engines (`"engines": false`), and the import then lists no engine material. The Enterprise, the original's easter egg, is an original rugged Earth-built design with the sensor picture's layout (a disc, a neck, a rear hull and two nacelles with red bands) and no lettering, numbers or insignia, agreed on 2026-10-14 in place of the recognizable model chosen on 2026-10-09. Both were approved in advance on 2026-10-14. The debris script rebuilt the wrecks, `build_noah.py`, `build_enterprise.py` and the eight old flat materials are removed, the ShipModels README now describes only the imports, and `shippictures` checks both ships' maps too.
- **Shield and Weapon Messages in the Original Wording**: the text window showed the port's "Shields raised.", "Shields dropped.", "Weapons armed.", "The shields cannot be raised inside a nebula.", "Insufficient fuel to raise shields." and "The nebula has collapsed the shields.", and nothing for Disarm (it set the flag without calling `DisarmWeapons`). It now uses the original's messages (NAVIGATR): "Shields are now up.", "Shields are down.", "Arming missiles." and "Arming lasers." for each working weapon (none if all are destroyed), "Weapons are disarmed.", "Shields can't be raised." with a beep in a nebula or without fuel, and in a nebula "Magnetic disturbances in nebula causing energy fluctuations." before the shields drop. Verified: the `shieldmessages` probe scenario's four checks fail on the old code and pass on the new; `nebula`, `combat` and the shield scenarios pass.
- **Comm Portrait Clears as in the Original**: with a low-skilled communications officer, the alien's portrait stayed under interference for the whole comm link (strong coloured rays, low resolution, bursts of waves and static every few seconds) and was hard to see, while the original shows the portrait clean and garbles only the words. The interference now tunes in as the link comes up: as strong as before at first (still set by the officer's skill), it clears over the same 5 s and does not return; the words are garbled by skill as before (`CommScreen`). A design decision of 2026-10-12. Verified: the `commpicture` probe scenario's two checks of the settled picture fail on the old code and pass on the new; the comm scenarios pass.
- **Fallback Font Asset in the Current Format**: `Assets/TextMesh Pro/Resources/Fonts & Materials/LiberationSans SDF - Fallback.asset` used an older serialization format, so any play session showing the "Starport clear" message made Unity rewrite it, leaving 146 changed lines in git. It is now committed once in Unity 6000.3's format. Field by field, all 157 fields of the font asset keep their values (its glyph and character tables are empty before and after: it is a dynamic font); the rest is format: new fields at their defaults, a new field order, and the material and texture versions. One value is more than format: the empty atlas texture is stored as 1 by 1 pixel with one zero byte instead of 0 by 0 with none, as this TextMesh Pro version stores a cleared atlas. A new `.gitattributes` checks this file out with LF line endings, as Unity writes it; otherwise a CRLF checkout still listed it as modified (with an empty diff) after such a play session.
- **Starmap Bar Matches the Original**: the starmap showed only the cursor coordinates and fuel estimate. The original's bar has POSITION, DESTINATION, DISTANCE and FUEL (MAP-OV WE72B); the label above the main view now shows all four, "Pos 120 96   Dest 140 110   Dist 24   Fuel 11.7" (`Starmap.UpdateLabel`), abbreviated to fit one line (confirmed in a `-Graphics` picture), with the fuel's tenths the port's own. Verified: the `starmapfuel` probe scenario now checks all four, failing on the old code and passing on the new; tesseract and ringdevice pass.
- **Planet Scan Text Laid Out as in the Original**: the scan put each value under its heading and the lithosphere on one line ("Tin, Lead, Gold"); the original puts each value on its heading's line and lists the elements one under another (starflt_144). Values now start in one column after the headings (a TextMesh Pro indent, `SensorsDisplay.c_sensorValueIndent`), one element per line. The `sensorlines` probe scenario checks both (both fail on the old code).
- **Bank Ledger Shows Whole Rows**: a ledger longer than its window scrolled its newest row above the balance by an arbitrary amount, cutting the top row in half under the headings; the original's ledger shows whole rows (starflt_046, starflt_053). `BankPanel` now scrolls by whole rows. The `starport-ledgerrows` probe scenario checks it (the rows check fails on the old code).
- **Trade Depot Texts Match the Original**: the price column read UNIT VALUE in every list, whereas the original's buy list says UNIT COST (what the Starport asks) and its cargo list UNIT VALUE (what it pays); the heading now follows the list (`TradeDepotPanel.m_unitValueHeadingText`). The welcome drops an exclamation mark absent from the original's WELCOME TO THE TRADE DEPOT, "Insufficent funds" is now spelt correctly, and the Trade Depot and Ship Configuration show the balance with thousands separators, like the Bank and Personnel. The `starport-tradetexts` probe scenario checks all four (all fail on the old code).
- **Save Slots in a Star System Show the System's Coordinates**: the save panel converted the ship's position inside a star system as if it were in hyperspace ("Coordinates: 159, 128" in Arth's system, which is at 125, 100). It now shows the system's own position, like the coordinates above the main view (`SaveGamePanel.UpdateDescriptions`), and "Missle Launcher" in the same description becomes "Missile Launcher". Verified: the `savecoords` probe scenario's check fails on the old code (144, 138) and passes on the new; savepanel and savedata pass.
- **Log Planet Works as in the Original**: Log Planet wrote "Planet logged:" and the location above the question and judged "already logged" by a ship's log entry made before asking: a planet answered No once could never be recommended, one recommended once could be recommended again after Interstel's evaluation for a second bonus, and in orbit of Arth it offered to recommend Arth. The original (CAPTAIN (/LOG-PLAN), WF3D3) clears the window and always asks only RECOMMEND THIS PLANET FOR COLONIZATION?, answers Yes with THIS PLANET HAS ALREADY BEEN LOGGED for a planet recommended earlier in the game, and says THAT'S ARTH YOU FOOL! in orbit of Arth. The port now matches (`LogPlanetButton`, `RecommendYesButton`, `PD_General.m_loggedPlanetList`, saved, and `IsPlanetLogged`); the ship's log still records the planet's data. Verified: the `logplanet` probe scenario's four checks fail on the old code and pass on the new; colonies and starport-preflight pass.
- **Terrain Vehicle Cargo Lists Each Item's Value**: the cargo display listed the vehicle's hold by volume only. The original shows each item's value beside its volume (starflt_190, 191: MOLYBDENUM 9.6 160), and now the port does too (`TerrainVehicleCargoDisplay`): elements at the Starport's price per cubic meter, artifacts and specimens at their value. Picking an item from a PLANET SURFACE list keeps the port's approach, with Cargo taking what is beside the vehicle (by design decision). Verified: the `cargodisplay` probe scenario now checks the values, failing on the old code and passing on the new; dropcargo, capture and pickups pass, and a `-Graphics` picture shows the column fits.
- **Landed Ship Keeps the Terrain Map, as in the Original**: landing switched the auxiliary view from the terrain map to Status, while the original keeps the map with the landing site's LAT and LONG on the ground (starflt_178); `Planetside.Show` now leaves it there. Verified: the `landinglines` probe scenario now checks the auxiliary view after a real landing, failing on the old code (Status) and passing on the new; terrain, disembarklines, unmapped, coordinatebar and savedata pass.
- **Encounters Shown at the Original Scale**: the camera climbed until every alien ship was in view, 7,000 to 11,000 units above the ship for ships arriving from about 3,700 units away, shrinking them to specks (a 90-unit Spemin scout under 2% of the view). The original uses a fixed scale, with ship icons about a sixteenth of the view's height, and distant ships come into view as they close in. The camera now frames alien ships out to their fire range and no further (`Encounter.c_cameraViewRange`, the 1,000 units of `c_alienFireRange`, which the aliens' fire check now uses too), staying below about 4,450 units with every ship that can fire on the player in view. Ships are also drawn twice as large in encounters (`Encounter.c_shipViewScale`: player and alien models, wrecks fitted to them as before; positions and ranges unchanged), about a twentieth of the view's height. A design decision of 2026-10-12. Verified: the `encounterviewscale` probe scenario's camera check fails on the old code (10,495 units) and passes on the new (3,580), and its scale checks pass; `shipmodels` now measures models at the encounter scale; the combat, encounter, salvage, debris and picture scenarios pass.
- **Orbit Approach Messages Scroll On**: the port cleared the text window at each step into orbit (standing by to orbit, the orbital maneuver, orbit established), so only the last line was ever seen. The original lets them scroll, from "...COMMENCING SYSTEM TRAVEL" to "...ORBIT ESTABLISHED" (starflt_141, starflt_143), and so does the port now, with the original's "...Orbit established." replacing "Orbit established.". Verified: the `orbitlines` probe scenario fails on the old code and passes on the new; `unmapped` now clears the window before its control, since the last orbit's lines remain.
- **Docking Bay Preflight Check Matches the Original**: the check used the port's own lines ("At least one of your crew members is dead.", the name and engines on two lines), let the ship leave with evaluations unread, and kept colony evaluations forever. The original (CHKFLIGHT-OV ?CAN-LEAVE) says REPORT TO OPERATIONS FOR EVALUATION while an evaluation is unread (a colony evaluation, the lost terrain vehicle, the towing charges, the completion of the mission), REPORT TO CREW ASSIGNMENT for a missing or dead crew member, and REPORT TO SHIP-CONFIGURATION TO CHRISTEN SHIP AND PURCHASE ENGINES as one line, and deletes read colony evaluations as the ship leaves (WF40A); the evaluation screen then says "You have not filed any new colony world recommendations" (OP-OV WEBC1). The port now does all of this (`DockingBayPanel.Open`, `PD_General.HasUnreadEvaluation` and `m_colonyEvaluationUnread`, saved; `OperationsPanel.MakeEvaluationPages`). Verified: the `starport-preflight` probe scenario's four checks fail on the old code and pass on the new; every Starport scenario, colonies and win pass.
- **Trade Depot Analysis Lists Only the Ship's Artifacts**: Analyze listed the Starport's artifacts for sale at their price under one Artifacts heading and the ship's under another. The original's ANALYSIS screen lists only the artifacts in the hold, at their value (starflt_080, starflt_099), so the Starport's now appear only on the Buy list (`TradeDepotPanel.UpdateScreen`). Verified: the `starport-analyzelist` probe scenario's check fails on the old code and passes on the new; starport and starport-specimens pass.
- **Notices Typed Out as in the Original**: the Operations notices screen showed one paragraph per press of More, and Quit always left it. The original types a notice out by itself, a paragraph every 300 ms; the spacebar shows the next at once, and the abort key ends the current notice with "***NOTICE ABORTED***" (then "***END OF NOTICES***" after the newest) (OP-OV WE912). The port now matches: a paragraph every 0.3 s, More shows the next at once, Quit during typing aborts the notice and stays on the screen, and a second Quit leaves; the UI kit's More and Quit buttons stand in for the original's two prompts (`OperationsPanel.Tick`, `AbortNotice`). Verified: the `starport-noticetyping` probe scenario's three checks of typing and abort fail on the old code and pass on the new; `starport-notices` passes.
- **Project Settings Checked Out With LF**: Unity saves the files under `ProjectSettings/` with LF line endings, even when nothing changes. A `core.autocrlf=true` checkout has them in CRLF, so after such a save git status listed them as modified with an empty diff (`ProjectSettings.asset` in the main checkout: 24,521 bytes checked out, 23,706 as Unity wrote it, same content). `.gitattributes` now checks `ProjectSettings/**` out with LF, as it already did for the fallback font asset; the files in git already had LF, so none change. `UnityConnectSettings.asset` takes the value the Editor kept writing: the Unity Services switch is off (`m_Enabled: 0`), as every service under it and `cloudEnabled` already were. Verified in a worktree: a settings file rewritten with LF shows as modified before the change and not after.
- **Roadmap Status Brought Up to Date**: `ROADMAP.md` still counted 57 of 59 items done with play testing and the screen comparison left, kept decisions D3 to D5 under "Still open" though all are ruled, and had Phase 7 waiting on the launch question and a hand test of Examine and Treat. It now records what #298 and #326 noted elsewhere: all 59 items are done or decided, leaving the UI kit work and a manual playthrough to the win.
- **Screen Comparison Closed for Gameplay**: `Research/Screen comparison.md` is recounted at a6e7dddb (87 gameplay items: 62 fixed, 25 not a defect or decided; 52 UI kit items, 49 left to the UI kit work; the probe's pictures cover every screen). The last three gameplay rows are decided (the save model keeps the port's five slots; the Black Egg's countdown matches the original in its window and timing), and the remaining work is listed (the UI kit items, and two rendering seams in the probe's pictures to check in the Editor). `ROADMAP.md` marks Phase 7's screens done, and the README's completion figure is 59 of 59. Docs only.
- **Screen Comparison UI Kit Items Decided**: all 52 UI kit items in `Research/Screen comparison.md` are closed: 11 fixed (#328 to #331 and the sensor lines PR) and 41 decided on 2026-10-12/13 with a reason each, mostly the kit's own wording and layout, kept by the decision of 2026-10-12. The summary, the remaining work (the probe's two rendering seams and a manual playthrough to the win), the README and the roadmap reflect this.
- **Black Egg Explosion as in the Original**: after BOOM! the planet vanished at once, with no flash and no final message. The original flashes the main view through black, orange, red, yellow and white for 3 s (DPART-OV EXPLOSION), then deletes the planet and says PLANET OBLITERATED (?DELETEPLANET). The port now plays that 3 s flash (`Viewport.StartFlash`), destroys the planet when it ends, and says "Planet obliterated." (`BlackEgg`). Verified: the `blackegg` probe scenario's two new checks fail on the old code and pass on the new; win, racelosses, tvtakesegg and crystalfield pass.
- **Console Menus After a Hail and a Launch Match the Original**: a hail the aliens did not answer at once put the bridge menu back on the console, as did the launch from the Starport; the original keeps the communications officer's HAIL, DISTRESS, BRIDGE after a hail (starflt_044, starflt_047) and shows the captain's menu after launch (starflt_139). The posture buttons now return to the communications officer's menu (`ButtonController.SetCommunicationsButtons`, which runs the Communications button so it keeps toggling between Hail and Respond), and the launch ends on the captain's (`SetCaptainButtons`). The `consolemenus` probe scenario checks both (both fail on the old code).
- **Screen Comparison: Final Open Rows Decided**: in `Research/Screen comparison.md`, the Look, Scan and Fire cursor rows are NOT A DEFECT (the port's 3D surface has no map for a cursor, and the nearest object is the one the cursor would pick), the launch's security code is left out with D4, and the Ship's Log stays a menu (it holds what the original's notepad left the player to write down), all decided on 2026-10-13. Docs only.
- **Distress Call Asks for Confirmation**: Distress asked "Transmit emergency distress call?". The original asks TRANSMIT EMERGENCY DISTRESS CALL, then CONFIRM: [N Y] on the next line (COMM-OV TOW-US, starflt_128); the port now shows "Transmit emergency distress call" and then "Confirm?", like its Launch question. The `distressconfirm` probe scenario now checks for the confirmation, failing before the fix and passing after; distress passes.
- **Probe Pictures for the Screens the Comparison Missed**: the headless probe now photographs the screens marked PROBE in `Research/Screen comparison.md`. `screenpictures` covers orbit with the system display left on by Maneuver, the landing map with the crosshair at 15N 30W, and the encounter at Arth's coordinates. `starport-screenpictures` goes on into the rooms (a notice being typed and finished, the evaluation, a new crew member from race to deletion, crew assignment with a file, a full ledger, an equipped ship with its buy and sell lists and naming, the trade depot's lists: 29 pictures). Two new scenarios cover the rest: `surfacepictures` (the landed ship, and the terrain vehicle beside a lifeform with Look, Scan, Cargo, Weapon and Maps) and `bridgepictures` (the flight scene's docking bay, Examine, Treat, Hail, Distress, the launch, a hail in an encounter). Without `-Graphics` they take no pictures and pass. The comparison's PROBE rows now record what the pictures show and the differences found, among them the launch's missing COMMENCING LAUNCH SEQUENCE and countdown, an equipped ship's acceleration and the trade depot's analysis list. Nothing under `Assets/` changes.
- **Motion Reports Need a Skilled Science Officer**: the radar reported "Motion detected." with any crew. The original reports motion only when the science officer's science skill exceeds 150 (HYPERMSG-OV TRAK-E and WF28B; the ASSIGN-CREW slot and CREWMEMBER byte are the science officer's science, CONFIRMED against the navigator's and engineer's offsets), and the port now does the same (`Radar.ScienceOfficerReportsMotion`); the radar's blips and sound, the port's own additions, stay for everyone. The original's "X: Y:" figures are each alien group's hourly jump in 8-unit cells, which the port's smooth alien movement lacks (they would always read 0), so they are left out by a design decision of 2026-10-13. Verified by `motiondetected`: its check of the unskilled officer fails before the fix and passes after; `encounters` and `encounterdata` pass.
- **Roadmap: D4 and 0.6 Settled, Phase 7 Updated**: D4 (the Interstel Police, the original's copy protection) is decided: the police are left out (2026-10-12), which also settles the launch's security code and the police's lines. Item 0.6 (the play test of #66 to #72) is closed, the port having since been played in the Editor many times. The Phase 7 entries name the PRs behind the remaining screens and message areas, and the notice wording as decided. The README's completion figure is 58 of 59 items.
- **Analysis Heading and Single-Word Temperatures Match the Original**: the Analysis began "Analysis of Last Sensor Reading" without the original's colon, and a planet with the same temperature word at both ends read "Sub-Arctic to Sub-Arctic" (193 of the 811 planets) where the original prints the word once (ANALYZE-OV, word WF068). Both now match (`AnalysisButton`, `GD_Planet.GetTemperatureText`). The `analysisheading` probe scenario's two checks fail before the fix and pass after; the analysis scenarios pass.
- **Starport Launch Uses the Original Lines**: the text window showed "Opening docking bay doors..." through the countdown, cleared to "Leaving starport...", cleared again to "Starport clear." and a hint, which the bridge buttons then cleared at once, and ended on "Starport clear. / Standing by to maneuver.". The original (LAUNCH-OV &LAUNCH and LAUNCH, HYPERMSG-OV WF0D5 and WF001) shows OPENING DOCKING BAY DOORS, clears the window for COMMENCING LAUNCH SEQUENCE... over the countdown, then keeps LEAVING STARPORT..., ...STARPORT CLEAR and STANDING BY TO MANEUVER together (starflt_136 to 139). The port now matches (`PlayerCamera.BeginCountdown`, `SpaceflightController.SwitchLocation`), and the bridge buttons no longer clear the window after launch. The `launchlines` probe scenario's two checks fail before the fix and pass after; launchconfirm, systemtravel and savepanel pass.
- **Terrain Vehicle Distance Measured as in the Original**: the display read "14 KM." with the ship right beside the vehicle, because it counted the height of the ship's spot (the vehicle comes out 143.6 units below it on planet 92) and used a kilometer of about 9 world units. The original measures along the surface in units of one kilometer, 6.4 to a degree of longitude and 5.3 to a degree of latitude, with an integer square root (MOVE-OV WF05A, SITE-OV .LON and WEFA0). The display and the bearing now use that ground distance (`Tools.SurfaceDistanceInKm`): 4 KM. as the vehicle comes out, 16 units south of the ship's spot so the models do not overlap. Boarding is unchanged. The `tvdistance` probe scenario's four checks fail before the fix and pass after; tvdisplay, terrain, uphill, lava, storms and pushback pass.
- **Input Moves to the Input System Package**: the Editor warned at every start that the project used the Input Manager, which is marked for deprecation. Input now goes through the Input System package, with Active Input Handling set to "Input System Package (New)" instead of "Both" (`activeInputHandler: 1`). `Assets/Input/Starflight Input.inputactions` holds the bindings. Its Ship map has the ten buttons of `ProjectSettings/InputManager.asset` on the same keys (the numpad, arrow, Home, End, Page Up and Page Down keys for the eight directions, Return and numpad Enter for Submit, Escape for Cancel; the old axes had no joystick bindings), read by `InputController` instead of `Input.GetButton`, with `Debounce()` unchanged. Its UI map has what `StandaloneInputModule` read (arrows and numpad 8, 2, 4 and 6 to move, Return and numpad Enter to submit, Escape to cancel) plus mouse, pen and touch pointers, and the Starport and Spaceflight event systems use an `InputSystemUIInputModule` on it (the Intro scene keeps the one it already had on the package's default actions). `SpaceflightController` reads Escape at game over and the Editor's F9 and F10 from the keyboard. New probe scenarios `input` and `starport-input` press every key on a virtual keyboard and check the game's and UI's response; keyboard feel was not tested by hand.
- **Combat Messages Match the Original**: the weapons fired unarmed, a shot that could not fire gave the port's own lines ("No laser cannon installed!", "Insufficient fuel to fire the laser!", "Select a target first!"), every shot said "Missile launched!" or "Hit! Target damaged.", and an alien laser hit said nothing. As in the original's COMBAT-OV, the weapons now fire only once armed, with WEAPONS NOT ARMED, WEAPON NOT AVAILABLE, OUT OF FUEL! and NO TARGET PRESENT (`FireLaserButton`, `FireMissileButton`); the player's shots and hits are silent, and an alien laser hit reports LASER CLASS n, or LASER PARTIALLY DEFLECTED with the Rod Device (WECEA; `CombatController.AlienFiresAtPlayer`). The port's own targeting menu stays. The `combatmessages` probe scenario's six checks fail before the fix and pass after; `h8` now arms the weapons first and expects OUT OF FUEL!; combat, missiles, commlink, combatwounds, roddevice, shimmeringball, systemdamage and flightpictures (with -Graphics) pass.
- **"Galactic Treaty" Spelled Correctly in the Intro**: the Intro's warning page read "As provided by the Galatic Treaty of 4410"; the original (GAME-OV) reads "Galactic", and the scene text is corrected. The `intropictures` probe scenario now checks the warning's wording with or without a graphics device, failing on the old scene and passing on the new; h7 passes.
- **No Before Yes in Starport Questions**: Personnel's DELETE THIS CREWMEMBER? and the Trade Depot's question before an analysis showed Yes left of No, while the original asks with <-N Y->, No first, as the flight console already does. Both button pairs are swapped in `Starport.unity` (No stays selected first). The `starport-noyes` probe scenario checks both (both fail on the old scene).
- **Probe Pictures for the Last Uncovered Screens**: two new probe scenarios photograph the screens `Research/Screen comparison.md` still listed without a picture. `flightpictures` renders the Science and Navigation menus, the save panel with its Switch and Reset questions, combat with Spemin scouts (the Combat menu, a laser shot, a missile), the starmap on the ship and with the cursor moved, Log Planet's question and Log Planet again after No, Land and the descent to the surface, and a Black Egg's countdown and explosion (20 pictures). `intropictures` loads the Intro scene and renders its five pages with the page timer stopped. Without `-Graphics` they take no pictures and pass (the Intro one still checks that the scene is reached). The comparison records what they show and the differences found: Log Planet's extra "Planet logged" lines and no second chance to recommend a planet after No, the save panel's star system coordinates, the original's PLANET OBLITERATED and explosion flash after a Black Egg, the starmap's missing distance, the original's combat flow, and the warning's "Galatic". Nothing under `Assets/` changes.
- **Supplemental Evaluation Fines Booked When Shown**: towing charges came out of the bank at the distress call, and a lost terrain vehicle's 10,000 MU at docking. The original books each fine when the Operations evaluation screen shows its supplemental evaluation (OP-OV WE78B: WF045 and WF12C call WF02B, T+BALANCE and TRANSACT as a FINE). The port now stores the fine with the evaluation (`PD_General.AddSupplementalFine`, saved) and books it when Operations shows it (`PlayerData.BookSupplementalFines`); fines in older save files were already booked. The `starport-supplementalfine` probe scenario and a new check in `distress` fail before the fix (fine taken at the call) and pass after; `distressconfirm` and `lava` now expect the evaluation screen to book the fine; starport-notices and win pass.
- **Trade Depot Buy List in the Original Order**: Starport elements were listed in game data order (Aluminum first). The original's Buy list runs Titanium, Promethium, Molybdenum, Cobalt, Aluminum, Endurium (starflt_078, starflt_097), and so does the port's now (`TradeDepotPanel.GetStarportElementOrder`). The `starport-buyorder` probe scenario's check fails before the fix and passes after; starport and starport-endurium pass.
- **Training Sessions Chosen in Bulk**: the Personnel office trained a skill one session per press of the fire button. The original asks "HOW MANY TRAINING SESSIONS (0-N)?", N being the lesser of the sessions left before the skill's maximum and the sessions the balance covers at 300 M.U. each (counted up to 100,000 M.U.), and trains them all at once (PERSONNEL-OV WEE65 and WF2CD). The port now asks the same, with the stick setting the number (right and left for one more or one fewer, up for the most, down for none) where the original takes typed digits; fire trains that many, cancel none (`PersonnelPanel.SetSessionCount`, `TrainSessions`). The `starport-training` probe scenario's three checks fail before the fix and pass after; `starport` passes.
- **Look Reports the Planet's Fields as in the Original**: Look listed Surface, Weather, Sky, Atmosphere (every gas) and Gravity as a word and a percentage. The original gives SKY, TEMPERATURE and GRAVITY on one line, then ATMO, HYDRO and TERRAIN (MAPS-OV WF4F4), naming atmosphere and hydrosphere by their main compound. Look now does the same: "Sky: It is clear.", "Temperature: Sub-Arctic to Temperate  Gravity: 1.80 G", "Atmo: Methane", "Hydro: Water", "Terrain: Frozen". The temperature is the planet's, as the port has no regions (the original uses the region's), and what lies next to the vehicle follows as before. The `lookfields` probe scenario fails before the fix and passes after; `look` and `storms` pass.
- **Ship Acceleration Uses the Original Formula**: Ship Configuration showed 6 G for an equipped ship the original shows at 7 G (starflt_076, same 356 tons), the result of the port's own power curve, which also went wrong above 500 tons. The original's acceleration is engine class times 500 over the ship's mass, in whole G (SHIPGRPH-OV (.ACC)); the port now computes it that way (`PD_PlayerShip.RecalculateAcceleration`), and the ship flies with it as before. The engine curve values stay in the game data, unused. The `acceleration` probe scenario's check fails before the fix (6 G, and 5 G where the original gives 4) and passes after; systemeffects passes.
- **Colony Recommendation Bonus or Fine Booked Immediately**: the bank paid a recommendation's bonus or took its fine on docking, with Interstel's evaluation. The original books it in the ledger when the planet is recommended, after the interest (CAPTAIN WF37A: WF348, then T+BALANCE and TRANSACT as BONUS or FINE in WF292), and the evaluation is read at the Starport. The port now does the same: Yes to "Recommend this planet for colonization?" books the bonus or fine at once (`PlayerData.BookColonyRecommendation`, kept on the saved recommendation record), and docking writes Interstel's evaluation from it. Recommendations in older save files are still booked at docking. Amounts are unchanged. The `colonies` probe scenario's new check fails before the fix (everything booked at docking) and passes after; its other checks, `starport-interest` and `win` pass.
- **Examine and Treat Ask Who, Like the Original Doctor**: Examine reported on the whole crew at once, and Treat picked the most badly hurt crew member on its own. Both now work like the original's doctor (DOCTOR in the decompiled original, s-macke/starflight-reverse, which matches the original's screenshots). They ask "Who should I examine?" (or "treat?"), and left and right cycle through the crew on board until Enter picks one (`CrewChooser`). Examine answers with the original's sentence, "Science is moderately wounded, and has a vitality of 70 and a durability of 6." (critically up to 20, heavily up to 50, moderately up to 80, slightly below 100, "is dead." for the dead), adding "... is currently being treated." for the patient. During a treatment, Treat first asks "Should I stop treating ... to treat someone else?" with No and Yes on the console (`ButtonSet.StopTreatment`): No continues, Yes stops it and asks who instead. For the chosen crew member the doctor starts treatment, or says "... doesn't need medical treatment." or "... is beyond help.". Treatment still takes time (`PD_CrewAssignment.UpdateTreatment`). Found by the screen comparison (roadmap Phase 7). The `examinetreat` probe scenario's five checks fail before the fix and pass after; `m20` now picks its patients through the console, and its check of a dead crew member reads "beyond help".
- **Project Notes Match the Code**: the old project notes are marked historical, and `Notes.txt` gives the stance values the code uses next to the original ones.
- **Aliens Choose Lines by the Shipped Game's Postures**: the game data gives each comm line one stance, and for 95 of the 540 lines it shares with the shipped game, that stance is not the shipped game's. A line used in several postures is stored as neutral, and many Spemin, Velox, Thrynn and Elowan lines carry a different posture from the shipped one, so a neutral Spemin had no statement at all and a hostile Thrynn answered questions the original never answers. `Starflight Recovered Data.json` now holds each line's shipped fields from the original's STARB.COM (its PHRASECONTRL records; `m_commPostureList`, `GD_CommPosture`): posture mask, context, action and the answers' mood changes. `Encounter.FindComm` picks a line whose posture mask holds the speaker's posture (`GD_Comm.IsSaidIn`), as FIND-PHRASE does; with no such line on the subject, the aliens say one of their subject 5 lines in that posture, as PHRASE-SEARCH does ("This should not concern you."). The neutral fallback remains the last resort. Context, actions and answer mood changes are read in but not yet used. This is a first step toward the shipped mood model (roadmap 2.1, decided 2026-10-12). The `shippedlines` probe scenario's two checks fail before the fix and pass after, its control passing on both; `comms` and `mood` now set the posture their lines need.
- **Alien Moods Use the Shipped Game's Numbers**: the Elowan, Thrynn, Spemin and Veloxi had the port's own mood numbers (#157), taken from the 1984 draft. They now have the shipped game's, from each race's record in STARB.COM (its ORIGINATOR: BASE-EDL, AET, BET, CET, OPE, FPE, HPE; `DevTools/OriginalRecords`), as decided on 2026-10-12. Starting moods are Elowan 44, Thrynn 44, Spemin 10 and Veloxi 44; friendly bands end at 25, 30, 0 and 20, hostile bands begin at 60, 60, 20 and 60, and fight or flight at 60, 80, 80 and 80, so the Elowan have no hostile band and the Spemin are never friendly. Friendly words move the mood by -10, -8, 0 and 0, hostile words by 20, 20, 30 and 20, grovelling by 0, 5, -8 and -8 (`AlienPersonality`). As in the original's COMM-OV, raised shields at the meeting add 5 to the starting mood and armed weapons 10 (`AlienPersonality.GetMeetingMood`), so Spemin meeting an armed, shielded ship start hostile. Races whose shipped mood is below all their thresholds start friendly: the Mysterions, Nomad probes, Veloxi drones, Minstrels, the Enterprise and the Noah 9 derelict (`AlienPersonality.StartsFriendly`), so the Mysterions' shields read lowered on the Analysis, as in the original. Other races start neutral, as before. A crew friendly with the rival race still meets the Elowan and Thrynn in the hostile band, which for the Elowan is fight or flight: they say their line about the Thrynn and depart, since the original uses those lines only for aliens past their neutral posture. The aggression weighing alien strength against the player's is still the port's; line actions, answer mood changes and the homeworld context come later. The `shippedmoods` probe scenario's three checks fail before the fix and pass after; `mood`, `rivals`, `comms` and `shippedlines` now use the shipped bands. Encounter feel still needs play testing.
- **Mineral Deposits Hold 1 to 5 Cubic Meters**: a deposit held 1 to 5 tenths of a cubic meter, so filling the terrain vehicle's 50.0 cubic meter hold took 100 to 500 deposits (183 on the first planet tried). Deposits now hold 1, 2, 3, 4 or 5 cubic meters, ten times as much, filling the hold after 10 to 50. The original design notes show a deposit as "XX.X cubic meters" and mine it a tenth of a cubic meter at a time (`Research/Notes/planet.txt`). Every deposit stays where it was, as the size still takes one random number. The cargo display not yet in any scene (`TerrainVehicleCargoDisplay`) printed tenths as cubic meters and now prints cubic meters, like the other cargo texts.
- **Alien Lines and Player Answers Move the Mood as in the Shipped Game**: the shipped game gives each line an action and each question a mood change for Yes and for No, held in the recovered data since the lines got their shipped postures but unused by the port. An alien line now does what the original's DO-UA does: it ends the comm link or moves the aliens' mood by its number. The Spemin's "Enough jabbering. It is time for a glorious Spemin victory!" adds 100 and they fight. The other mood actions are on home fleet lines (a Veloxi warning 10, a Thrynn warning 20 or 50), which change nothing while a home fleet's mood does not decide its posture. The player's answer to an alien question moves the mood by the question's own number, as YES>EDL and NO-EDL do: No to the Veloxi's "Of course Veloxi are superior all the time. Agreeing?" adds 10, Yes to the Elowan's "Art thou friends or allies of the Thrynn?" 50 (`GD_Comm.m_endsCommLink`, `m_moodAction`, `m_deltaNo`, `m_deltaYes`; `Encounter.ChangeMood`, `ApplyAnswer`). The mood moves only where it decides the posture: for the Elowan, Thrynn, Spemin and Veloxi, outside a home fleet and the other overriding rules. The Gazurtoid, Mechan and Veloxi drone numbers wait for moods of their own. Lines that end the comm link change two transmissions: every Nomad probe planetary report ends it, so a probe sends one report per comm link, and every second verse of the Minstrels' song ends it, so a Minstrel sings two verses. The next meeting continues with the next ones, starting over after the last (`Encounter.FindNextTransmission`, the line used least, by the saved counts of the least-used rule). Previously they sent everything at once, then ended the link. Verified by the `lineactions` probe scenario (three checks fail before the fix and pass after, its control passing on both) and `transmissions`, whose Minstrel and Nomad probe checks now expect the shipped behaviour and fail before the fix. Encounter feel still awaits play testing.
- **Aliens Weigh the Player's Strength by the Shipped Game's Rule**: whether hostile-band aliens threaten or grovel, and whether aliens too agitated to talk fight or flee, rested on the port's own measure: the player ship's armor and shield points against the alien ships', weighted by an invented aggression number. It now follows the shipped game (COMM-OV EDL>POSTURE and IAPWR). The player's strength is the classes of the ship's armor, shields, missile launcher and laser cannon, plus as much again for every 500 points by which the captain's five skills together exceed 550 (`Encounter.GetPlayerStrength`). The aliens' strength is each ship's vessel strength, scaled by the share of armor and shields it has left (`GetAlienStrength`). They grovel when the player is more than 15 stronger and flee when more than 5 stronger. Vessel strengths are byte 5 of the original's VESSEL record, which the data extract calls Energy (Spemin 0, 5 and 10, Thrynn 8, 15 and 22, Veloxi 10, 20 and 25, Elowan 0, 5 and 15, Gazurtoid 80 and 120, Uhlek 255); they live in `Starflight Recovered Data.json` (`m_vesselStrengthList`, `GD_VesselStrength`, `GD_Vessel.m_strength`), as the game data file has no field for them. A fully equipped ship with a skilled captain (48) thus makes three Spemin warships (30) grovel, while an unequipped ship does not, however many armor points it has. `AlienPersonality.m_aggression` is gone. The `shippedstrength` probe scenario's three checks fail before the fix and pass after, its control passing on both; `mood` and `comms` now build their stronger ship with equipment and a skilled captain. Encounter feel still needs play testing.
- **Alien Mood Triggers Follow the Shipped Game**: the aliens' mood moved only with the player's statements and questions, by the 1984 draft's rule (each phrase in the same posture counting less). It now moves with the shipped game's events (COMM-OV <EDL>). Raising shields during an encounter angers the aliens by 5 and lowering them calms them by 5; arming weapons angers them by 10 and disarming calms them by 10 (`Encounter.UpdatePlayerShieldsAndWeapons`). The player's reply to their hail has the posture's full effect, and a player who hails first gets the full effect of each posture the first time it is used in the encounter (`ApplyFullPostureEffect`, `ApplyNewPosture`). Their second unanswered hail angers them by 4, the third by 9. A statement moves them by the posture's effect divided by the player's phrase count in the comm link (a hail reply counting as one), a question by half that (`ApplyPlayerPosture`). The counts are saved with the encounter (`PD_Encounter.m_playerPhrases`, `m_playerHailedFirst`, `m_playerPostures`, `m_alienHails`, replacing `m_playerPhrasesInPosture`). Firing on aliens outside a comm link adds 100 in the original, which the port already covers with attacked aliens' hostility. The `postureeffects` probe scenario's four checks fail before the fix and pass after; `mood` and `rivals` now count the player's hail. Encounter feel is not yet play tested.
- **Aliens Talk as Long as in the Shipped Game**: how many phrases the Elowan, Thrynn, Spemin and Veloxi say in a comm link before tiring came from the 1984 draft: drawn once as the link began, 10 to 20 for grovelling aliens, 5 to 15 friendly, 3 to 8 neutral and 2 to 5 hostile. It now follows the shipped game (COMM-OV GET-PHRASE-LIMIT): 10 to 19 grovelling, 10 to 14 friendly, 5 to 9 neutral and 4 to 6 hostile, redrawn for the aliens' posture whenever their mood changes, with their phrases in the link counted against it (`PD_Encounter.m_alienPhrases`, `m_alienPhraseLimit`, replacing `m_alienPhrasesLeft`; a save without them draws a limit). Friendly aliens now talk longer, and calming or angering them mid-conversation changes how long they go on. The `phraselimit` probe scenario's three checks fail before the fix and pass after. Encounter feel still needs play testing.
- **Aliens Wait for the Player as in the Shipped Game**: aliens whose hail went unanswered hailed again every 10 to 30 seconds for the whole encounter, and aliens whose question went unanswered waited in silence. They now follow the shipped game (COMM-OV HAIL, QUESTION, TIRED-OF-WAITING and ?REPEAT): without a reply they hail again after 20 seconds, three hails in all, then wait (a home fleet still gives all its homeworld warnings); without an answer they say one of their "waiting" lines after 20 seconds, twice at most. These are the game data's subject 6 lines, never used before (the Elowan's "I wait on thy answer.", the Thrynn's "You will pleasse answerrr."). They ask a question instead of making a statement 30 times in 100, the Mechans 70 (?MAKE-STATEMENT), where the port started every race at 50 and raised the Mechans' chance by 25 per phrase. The `alienwaiting` probe scenario's three checks fail before the fix (6 hails, no reminder, 50 and 50) and pass after. Encounter feel still awaits play testing.
- **Mechan Mood, and Heaven Lets a Friend In**: the Mechans counted right answers: five unlocked mechan 9, and one wrong answer made them hostile. They now have the shipped game's mood (their STARB.COM record: EDL 48, friendly below 10, hostile from 75, fight from 99, unaffected by the player's posture), which the answers to their questions move by the shipped numbers: each right answer takes 10 off, a wrong one adds 10, and a wrong answer to "Are you Group 9?", "Do you serve Layton?" or "Are you an enemy of Noah?" adds 100. Four right answers thus make them friendly, and one ordinary slip leaves them talking. Once friendly they stay so for good (COMM-OV +!EDL sets the race's mood to 1: `PD_General.m_mechan9Unlocked`, `Encounter.UnlockMechans`), and the Mechan fleet at Heaven (encounter 77, 145,107 orbit 4, the original's *HEAVEN) goes inactive: a ship in that encounter returns to orbit, and the fleet no longer stops ships entering orbit there (`SpaceflightController.BeginInOrbitEncounter`). This settles roadmap 0.2, marked Ask because the roadmap's named sources did not cover it; the decompiled original does. The `heaven` probe scenario's four checks fail before the fix and pass after; `comms` now answers the Mechans with the button, and `alienshields` and `alienanalysis` give the Mechans the mood for the posture they set.
- **Gazurtoid Now Have a Mood**: the Gazurtoid stayed neutral until fired on. They now have the shipped game's mood (STARB.COM record: EDL 26, never friendly or diplomatic, fight from 40, grovelling at -20, hostility +30): abusive from the start but not attacking, they turn to fight at once when met with shields up and weapons armed (+15), or to flight before a far stronger ship. A grovelling reply or hail calms them by 20. Their lines serve every posture, so only fight or flight changes what the player sees. Verified: the `gazurtoidmood` probe scenario's three checks fail on the old code and pass on the new; the Gazurtoid control in `mood` now ends on their phrase limit, not a timer.
- **Code Review and README Updated After the Decisions of 2026-10-05**: the code review of 2026-10-03 lists #66 to #73 under "Fixed since the review" with how each was verified, including the run of all fifty probe scenarios on the final tree (239 checks pass; the 3 failures were a fault in an older scenario, corrected in #73). It records the five rulings that change no code (crew vitality, the three unused Spemin lines, the planet files, the colours of the experimental planets, the three unwired terrain classes) and carries two game data proposals awaiting a decision: encounters 144 to 146 are hyperspace encounters with the wrong location, and five in-orbit encounters name the wrong orbit. `README.md` describes the new behaviour.
- **Roadmap to Parity With the Original**: `ROADMAP.md` compares the port with the 1986 game using the research sources (STRINFO, the data spreadsheets, the design notes, the screenshots) and the code. It covers where each area stands, the design decisions, and eight phases of about 50 PRs. The critical path runs from the foundations through ruins, artifacts and their effects to the Crystal Planet endgame; lifeforms follow once the game can be won. Under the rule of 2026-10-09, behaviour the sources document for the original game is the design decision.
- **Stardates Use the Original Calendar**: stardates used the real-world calendar from 01-01-4620, via `DateTime`. The original has 10 months of 30 days, a 300-day year: the star data's flare dates use it (234 of 234), the 1984 design notes describe it, the manual speaks of "ten-month", and the Elowan place Arth's flare in "the final week of your Ten-month". Every stardate is now computed from the day count in that calendar (`PD_General.GetStardateYMD` and `GetStardateDHMY`, with no date class, so regional settings play no part): day 40 is 11-02-4620 (was 10-02), day 299 is 30-10-4620 (was 26-10) and day 300 is 01-01-4621 (was 27-10-4620). The day count, clock rate and notice dates are unchanged; the game data's notices are valid in both calendars. Older saves have their bank ledger, ship's log entries for alien messages and logged planets, and current stardate converted on load (`PlayerData.ValidateStardateCalendar`, marked by the new `PD_General.m_stardateCalendar`; save version unchanged), and a game loaded at the Starport now shows the current date there before the first launch. The `smallfixes` probe scenario now expects day 40 as 11-02-4620 instead of 10-02-4620. Verified with the `calendar` probe scenario.
- **Game Clock Runs Wherever the Ship Is in Space**: the stardate advanced only in star systems and hyperspace, freezing in orbit, on planet surfaces, in the terrain vehicle and in encounters, while repairs, treatment and shield recharge carried on; since raised shields burn fuel each star hour, they cost nothing in a fight. The clock now runs in every Spaceflight location except the docking bay, which belongs to the Starport. In the original kernel (`Research/Notes/disys.txt`, `PARALLEL-TASKS`), the clock, repairs and crew treatment share one task list behind one switch, and the manual has the doctor treating the crew on a planet's surface (page 7), so that list runs there. That the switch is also on in orbit and in encounters is inferred: shields are paid for by the star hour and raised for combat (manual page 24). The rate is unchanged; the sources do not give it. Verified with the `gameclock` probe scenario.
- **Builds Start With 12,000 MU**: new games started with 1,000,000 MU, a leftover marked as a hack in `PD_Bank`. Builds now start with the original 12,000 MU, as named in the first starport notice and shown on the original bank screen (`Research/Screenshots/Bank/starflt_017.png`). The Editor keeps 1,000,000 MU so everything can be bought and tried (`PD_Bank.GetStartingBalance`). Saved games keep their balance.
- **Nomad Probe Sensor Picture Matches the Original**: the hand-drawn picture of the Nomad probe (vessel 19) was a thin rod with a small dish, while the original sensor window shows a large bow-shaped dish, a body with two coloured pods and an antenna (`Research/Screenshots/Ships/Nomad Analysis.png`). `DevTools/SensorPictures` now retraces it from that screenshot at tolerance 0.4 into the same files, keeping its import settings and slot; the review sheet is `DevTools/SensorPictures/previews/nomad-probe.png`. The hand-drawn Mysterion picture stays, as it matches the original; an earlier report that it was flattened came from game window screenshots unevenly scaled to 1280 x 720. The Nomad's debris picture, made from the old picture, is unchanged. Verified with the review sheet, not the probe (the file names are unchanged and the probe cannot read pictures).
- **Empty Sensor Window Is Black, as in the Original**: a scan with no picture (an unknown object) left the sensor window magenta, whereas the original shows a black window inside a magenta border (`Research/Screenshots/Encounters/Scanning/starflt_057.png`). `SensorsDisplay` now copies the magenta panel once (same rounded sprite, opaque black, inset 16 units per side, `m_emptyBorderWidth`, about the original's 3 of 90 pixels) and shows it only while a scan has no picture; scans with a picture, and the display before any scan, keep the magenta window. The panel is wired to the new `SensorsDisplay.m_panelImage` in `Spaceflight.unity`. Verified: the four new checks of the `sensorpictures` probe scenario fail on the old code and pass on the new.
- **Every Wreck Shows Its Own Debris**: only 8 of the 23 vessels had a debris picture, so any other wreck (the Elowan, Thrynn, Veloxi, Gazurtoid and Uhlek ships, the Enterprise, the Noah 9) left the sensor window empty. Their 15 debris pictures and masks are now generated from their sensor pictures by `process_texture_debris.py` with its "Light Battle Damage" preset (seed 12345, 25% damage, scatter 2, pieces of at least 150 pixels), which reproduces the eight existing pictures and masks pixel for pixel. The Nomad probe's debris is regenerated from its new sensor picture. `DevTools/SensorPictures/make-debris.ps1` runs the script for one vessel (Python with Pillow required), writes the `.meta` files and wires the entries in `SensorsDisplay.m_debrisBackgroundTextures` and `m_debrisMaskTextures`. Verified: the `sensorpictures` probe scenario now scans every vessel's debris; the 15 new ones and a Thrynn scout destroyed in a real encounter fail on the old scene, and every check passes on the new.
- **Unidentified Objects Show No Readout**: a scan with no picture (an unknown object) still showed mass and bio lines, and the science officer said "Unknown object detected." The original shows an empty readout and "Scanners indicate unidentified object!" (`Research/Screenshots/Encounters/Scanning/starflt_057.png` and `starflt_058.png`). `SensorsDisplay.StartScanning` now shows the two readout lines only for a scan with a picture, and the message uses the original wording. Verified: the two new `sensorpictures` probe checks fail on the old code and pass on the new, and scans with a picture still show their readout.
- **Sensor Pictures Stay Inside the Window**: sensor pictures are 640 units wide and the magenta panel 480, so the colour noise of wide pictures showed beside the window: the Enterprise, the Velox drone (whose hand-drawn picture reaches furthest), and the Enterprise and Thrynn transport debris (found in Editor captures of debris scans, 2026-10-09). `SensorsDisplay` now adds a `RectMask2D` to the window when first shown, inset to where the magenta begins: the panel's inset (16 units left and right) plus its sprite's transparent edge (6 of 128 pixels, 5.76 units, `m_panelSpriteMargin`). Clipping at the panel's rectangle alone left a piece of the Enterprise wreck showing over black in that edge. The noise shader (`Custom/UI/Masked Image`) applies the clip as is; the pictures' black parts use a shader that does not, but they multiply what lies beneath, which beside the panel is already black. Verified in the Editor (the four pictures end at the magenta edge; with the clip moved 200 units in, panel and noise are both cut there) and by the `sensorpictures` probe scenario, whose two new checks (the clip's inset, the clipped noise of an Enterprise scan) fail on the old code and pass on the new.
- **Ship Console in the UI Kit Style**: the Spaceflight console adopts the modern sci-fi UI kit: the grey frame becomes the app's dark background, the panels the kit's outlined navy panels, and the coordinates, display names, ship name, console buttons, status and terrain vehicle readouts, sensor readouts, message box, ship's log and "Commencing System Penetration" popup use the kit's fonts and colour roles. Readout columns keep their 41 pixel rows, and the button bullets are the kit's diamonds (outlined, filled when selected, glowing while running). The sensor window is steel blue instead of magenta (chosen on 2026-10-11, `UIKitTheme.m_sensorWindow`; black vessel silhouettes stay clear on it), while an unidentified object's window stays black, as `SensorsDisplay` strips the style from its panel copy; the kit's second accent is amber instead of magenta. Each element carries a `UIKitStyle` role, applied in the editor and the game alike, so theme changes reach everything. `Starflight Remake > UI Kit > Apply to the Spaceflight Console` (`UIKitSceneTool`) sets the roles from one table; the rebuild makes the bullets from the theme's colours and keeps existing font assets (`Remake Font Assets` regenerates them).
- **Starport Rooms in the UI Kit Style**: Operations, Personnel, Crew Assignment, the Bank, Ship Configuration, the Trade Depot and the Docking Bay match the ship console's new look: outlined navy panels instead of bright blue, light text instead of dark, Orbitron headings, Rajdhani labels and text (name and value columns keep their line heights so they stay aligned), an amber Interstel logo, and navy buttons that turn dim cyan under the cursor (their tints were blue and green). Race silhouettes in Personnel stand on the sensor window's steel blue, like the vessels' sensor pictures. Since the rooms are built alike, `Starflight Remake > UI Kit > Apply to the Starport Rooms` (`UIKitSceneTool.ApplyToStarport`) assigns roles by rule, from the sprite and colour or font and colour each element draws with, and reports anything it leaves untouched (nothing). Saving the scene also upgraded it to Unity 6's format, which is most of its diff.
- **Remaining Spaceflight Screens in the UI Kit Style**: encounter, combat, landing and terrain vehicle messages show in the kit's colours. Their colour tags (`<color=#0a0>` for alien speech, `#0aa`, `white`, `red`, `yellow` and others) are mapped to kit colour roles at display time (`UIKitTheme.KitColors`, called by `Messages.UpdateDisplay`), so saved messages and code keep their tags, green stays green, red stays red, and theme changes apply. The Terrain Map display gets a kit navy background instead of dark grey, kit borders on its map and legend frames instead of grey, and cyan crosshairs with a dark outline; the ship's log arrows are cyan. `Apply to the Spaceflight Console` sets these from its table. The starmap, a 3D model in the viewport, keeps its look.
- **Save Game Panel in the UI Kit Style**: the save game panel (Persistent scene, shown over the Starport and Spaceflight) gets an outlined navy panel, the five slots as raised cards with descriptions in a darker well, slot names in Orbitron with "Active" in cyan, descriptions in Rajdhani with values in kit cyan instead of pure blue (`SaveGamePanel.UpdateDescriptions` passes them through `UIKitTheme.KitColors`), and navy buttons that turn dim cyan when selected, as in the Starport. The Switch and Copy dialogs have a cyan outline; the formerly red Reset dialog is navy with a red outline and its question in the alert colour. `Starflight Remake > UI Kit > Apply to the Save Game Panel` (`UIKitSceneTool.ApplyToSavePanel`) sets the roles from a table; buttons in a row keep their heights (a `LayoutElement`), since the layout sized them from their old, larger sprite. Button tinting is now one helper (`TintButton`), shared with the Starport rooms.
- **Officer Icons on the Console**: the icon beside the name of whoever has the console now shows who it is. The captain, science officer, navigator, engineer, communications officer and doctor each get the UI kit's line icon (drawn in Figma), the terrain vehicle a new one in the same style, and the bridge keeps Interstel's logo. The icons are cyan instead of the logo's muted grey. `ButtonController.ChangeOfficerText` takes the icon (`UIKitTheme.OfficerIcon`) with the name, the theme holds the seven sprites (`UIKitTheme.GetOfficerIcon`, imported from `Assets/UI Kit/Sprites` by `Starflight Remake > UI Kit > Rebuild`), and `Apply to the Spaceflight Console` wires the image to the button controller and preserves its aspect, as the icons are square and the logo wide.
- **Selected Console Button Is Highlighted**: following the UI kit's Console Button, the selected row is raised in navy with a cyan bar on its left edge and its label in the primary text colour, other labels use the secondary text colour, and a running button (after the fire button, or one that keeps running like Maneuver) gets a cyan row with dark text. Bullets keep their outline, filled and glowing states. `ButtonController.ShowButtonStates` sets bullets, label colours and highlight in one place (called by `UpdateButtonSprites`, the fire button and `SetSelectedButton`) and moves the highlight behind the selected label; `Apply to the Spaceflight Console` creates and wires the highlight (`UI Kit Selection` with its `Bar`, the first child of the labels' panel) and leaves label colours to the controller.
- **Starport Concourse in the UI Kit Style**: the Starport's 3D hall (and the station seen from outside at launch) swaps the original EGA colours for the kit's, keeping its geometry (chosen on 2026-10-12). The teal floor becomes navy with its star in darker navy and a glowing cyan outline, the blue wall the sensor window's steel blue, the red doors a softly glowing dim cyan, the transporter pad navy with a glowing amber edge, and the grey hull the kit's navy, border blue and blue grey. `Starflight Remake > UI Kit > Apply to the Starport Concourse` (`UIKitConcourseTool`) sets the materials from the theme and builds the floor textures (`Starport Floor - Kit Albedo` and `- Kit Emissive`) from the original, which it keeps; a flood fill from the corner separates the floor's teal from the teal band around the star, which becomes the glowing outline. The door frames are part of the hull mesh, so the door panels glow instead.
- **Docking Bay in the UI Kit Style**: the hangar where the ship waits before launch takes the kit's colours with a soft glow, as chosen for the concourse on 2026-10-12. The grey metal of doors and walls becomes a light blue grey, keeping its shading and stencils; Interstel's yellow logo on the doors becomes softly glowing kit amber, and the sirens are amber. `Starflight Remake > UI Kit > Apply to the Docking Bay` (`UIKitDockingBayTool`) sets the materials from the theme and builds the door textures (`Docking Bay Doors - Kit Albedo` and `- Kit Emissive`) from the original, which it keeps.
- **Launch View in the UI Kit Style**: the launch completes the docking bay's new colours. The countdown over the opening doors is kit amber instead of the original yellow (`Apply to the Spaceflight Console`; its animation only changes size and opacity), and the sirens, lit orange by the launch and landing animations in the original, now light in amber along with their eight lights (`Apply to the Docking Bay` derives the green and blue keys of the domes' emissive colour from how lit the red key says they are; `Apply to the Spaceflight Console` sets the lights' colour). The station seen after launch is the concourse model and already has its colours. Saving the two animations also upgraded them to Unity 6's format, which is most of their diff.
- **Terrain Vehicle View in the UI Kit Style**: scan labels over objects around the terrain vehicle use the kit's label style (Rajdhani in spaced capitals, with a dark outline to read on any ground) instead of plain white Arial, and the kit's colours: green for elements and vegetation, the secondary text colour for rocks, amber for dropped cargo, the primary text colour for ruins and artifacts, and the alert colour instead of magenta for lifeforms. `TerrainObjectLabel` now creates TextMesh Pro text instead of the legacy `TextMesh` and destroys its copy of the font material; `ScanButton` takes the colours from `UIKitTheme` (the original ones without a theme). The probe now counts labels by their TextMesh Pro text. The rest of the view (console, Look and Scan reports, cargo and weapon menus) already had the kit's look.
- **Target Bracket in Encounters**: the alien ship selected with the Target button is now marked in the viewport, as in the UI kit's Encounter and Combat mockups: four corners around it and a label beneath in the kit's small label style, amber with the vessel's range while the encounter is not hostile, red with its shields when it is (fired on, or a hostile alien stance). Previously only a message named the target. `TargetBracket` fits the bracket to the on-screen outline of the target's meshes each frame, hides it with no target, a dead or off-screen target, or outside an encounter, and updates the label's number without allocating strings; it refreshes the target's renderers when an encounter puts another vessel's meshes in the same model slot. `Apply to the Spaceflight Console` creates it in the viewport (`UI Kit Target`, masked to the viewport, under its fade). The console, comm buttons and encounter and combat messages already had the kit's look.
- **Hyperspace View in the UI Kit Style**: following the kit's Hyperspace mockup, star systems in view show their coordinates beside them ("123 · 101", small label style), and the viewport gets the kit's frame: a cyan mark at each corner and the current location in the top left ("HYPERSPACE", "STAR SYSTEM", "IN ORBIT", "ENCOUNTER", "TERRAIN VEHICLE" and others). `StarLabels` positions a pool of 32 labels by the stars in view each frame, with texts built once at startup so flying allocates no strings; `ViewportFrame` sets the name only on a location change. `Apply to the Spaceflight Console` creates both in the viewport (`UI Kit Star Labels`, masked to the viewport under the target bracket, and `UI Kit Frame` on top). The mockup's course line is omitted: the ship is flown with the stick, so there is no course to draw.
- **Star System View in the UI Kit Style**: following the kit's Star System mockup, a planet in view shows its orbit and surface just right of its edge ("III · Rock", small label style). `PlanetLabels` keeps one label for each of the eight orbits, positions it each frame while the planet's centre is in view (in the star system and just after launch), and rebuilds the text only when a different planet occupies the orbit, so flying allocates no strings. `Apply to the Spaceflight Console` creates it in the viewport (`UI Kit Planet Labels`, beside the star labels; one builder now makes both). The frame reads STAR SYSTEM there. The mockup's orbit rings are left to the star system view's own series (look B, with orbit paths planned), which renders that 3D view.
- **In Orbit View in the UI Kit Style**: the viewport frame now shows a status in its top right corner, opposite the location name, as in the kit's mockups: in orbit the planet below ("ORBIT I · ROCK"), in a star system the orbit the ship can enter once close enough ("APPROACHING ORBIT III", with the original "Standing by to orbit"), and in an encounter an open comm link ("COMM LINK OPEN") or else armed weapons ("WEAPONS ARMED"). `ViewportFrame` computes a status key each frame and rebuilds the text only when it changes; `Apply to the Spaceflight Console` adds the status to the frame. The kit has no In Orbit mockup, and the planet, the view's own 3D, is left as is.
- **Intro in the UI Kit Style**: the whole intro, logos included, adopts the kit's look (chosen on 2026-10-12): the Binary Systems logo's red line and the white Starflight logo turn cyan, the original team's names become white Orbitron headings instead of green Space Age, the copyright line sits on a navy strip, the remake's credits are on an outlined navy panel (roles in kit cyan, names in the primary text colour instead of green), and Interstel's warning is a navy panel with the alert outline and an alert-coloured heading instead of a red panel. The Electronic Arts logo is unchanged. `Starflight Remake > UI Kit > Apply to the Intro` (`UIKitSceneTool.ApplyToIntro`) sets the roles from a table and builds kit-coloured Binary Systems logo images (`Intro Binary Systems ... Kit.png`) from the originals, which it keeps.

### Removed

- **Stale Crash-Recovery Scene Copy**: `Assets/_Recovery/0.unity`, its `.meta` and the folder's `.meta`, Unity's crash-recovery copy of `Assets/Scenes/Spaceflight.unity` committed with the combat work in b9727c5. Unreferenced and not a build scene, it differed from the real scene in two lines: the `ScanType.Unknown` slot of the `SensorsDisplay` background and mask texture arrays pointed at the Spemin Scout textures instead of the Spemin Warship debris textures. `.gitignore` now lists `Assets/_Recovery/` and `Assets/_Recovery.meta`.
- **Debris Script That Could Not Run**: `process_multiple_ships_debris.ps1` at the repository root called a nonexistent `process_ship.py` with paths fixed to one checkout, and nothing referred to it. `process_ship_debris.py` does its job with `--batch` (see `DevTools/ShipModels/README.md`).
- **Dead Encounter Method**: `Encounter.LeaveEncounterAfterVictory`, never called from code, a scene, a prefab or an animation; the player leaves a won encounter by flying out of it.
- **Dead Debug Drawing and an Unused Field**: the switched-off `if ( false && ... )` debug drawing block in `TerrainVehicle.OnDrawGizmos`, and `PlanetGenerator.m_legendTexture`, which nothing ever assigned or read.

### Fixed

- **Nebula Skybox Finally Appears**: each territory has its own nebula sky (blue for human space, red for the Uhlek, violet for the Gazurtoid and so on, cross-faded at the borders by `StarflightSkybox`), yet space was plain black behind the stars. `StarflightSkybox.LateUpdate` rotates the sky by the ship's speed over its maximum, computing speed as distance over `Time.deltaTime`. A frame that takes no time (one of the scene's first does, as seen headless) made that zero over zero, as did a stationary ship with no maximum speed (engines broken beyond use). The rotation became NaN, stayed NaN because each frame's rotation multiplies into the last, and the skybox shader drew nothing. The sky now rotates only on frames that take time with a maximum speed above zero, the rotation is kept at unit length, and a non-finite one is reset. The `skyboxpictures` probe scenario checks the rotation after scene start, with no maximum speed and in each territory, and with `-Graphics` that no territory's sky is black (the old code fails all 16 checks).
- **Game Clock Runs at the Original Rate**: the clock ran 175.2 game hours per hour of play (365 days in 50 hours), a placeholder from before the calendar matched the original and no source gave the rate. The decompiled kernel settles it: `?NEW-HO` starts a new star hour each time REAL-MS elapses, and REAL-MS is a fixed 10,000 ms. A star hour now passes every 10 seconds of play (`PD_General.c_gameSecondsPerSecond`, 360 game hours per hour), so a calendar year takes 20 hours of play instead of 41, and everything hourly (shield fuel, storms, alien movement, repairs, treatment, Arth's flare) runs twice as fast in real time, as in the original. Found while comparing flight with the decompiled original. The `clockrate` probe scenario checks for 60 game minutes per 10 seconds (the old code gave 29.2).
- **Playthrough Test No Longer Circles the Ship**: since the terrain vehicle only re-boards from the ship's own map cell (#350), the playthrough must drive within 2.5 units of the ship. Steering at the ship every frame made the slowly turning vehicle orbit it at about 7 units (stage 10, the Black Egg's planet: 564 units driven, never closer). The final approach is now driven in short pushes with stops between (`PtCreep`). Test tooling only. Verified: stages 10 to 21 pass 50 of 50 on a rerun, the full playthrough 122 of 122 on master after #372.
- **Fleeing Ships Get a Minute's Head Start**: a ship leaving an encounter came out 160 units from the aliens, who were back inside the 128-unit encounter range in about a second, so fleeing restarted the encounter. The original allows a minute: leaving sets ENC-TIME to the current time plus 60000 ms (HYPERMSG-OV `WF022`), and HYPER-OV `>ENCOUNTER` starts one only after that; an encounter begun in orbit gets no grace. The port now matches (`SpaceflightController.HoldOffEncounters`, called by `Encounter.LeaveEncounter`), while the aliens keep moving. Found while comparing flight with the decompiled original. The `encountergrace` probe scenario checks it (the old code fails: the encounter restarted before the ship was seen outside it).
- **Starport Repair and Resale Prices Match the Original**: Repair charged only for armor, at the armor class's price (3 to 17 M.U. a point, decided on 2026-10-10 when no source gave a price), fixed damaged systems for free, and parts resold at full price whatever their damage. The decompiled CONFIG-OV gives both. Repair (`WF226`) costs 4 M.U. per missing armor point; for the engines, shields, missile launcher and laser cannon, the installed class's price in hundreds of M.U. times the damage percentage plus 5% (a class 3 laser cannon at 40%: 34,020 M.U.); and 100 M.U. per damage point to the sensors and comm (`PD_PlayerShip.GetStarportRepairCost`). A part sells (`WECB0`) for its price in hundreds times its condition over 105, in hundreds again, minimum 5: the game data's sell price when whole, half of it at 50% (`GetPartResaleValue`). A newly bought part is now whole instead of inheriting the replaced part's damage. Found while comparing trade with the decompiled original. The `starport-repair` probe scenario checks armor, systems and resale (four checks fail on the old code).
- **Landing and Launching Burn Fuel, and Heavy Gravity Is Fatal**: the ship landed and launched anywhere for free. In the original, LAND-OV burns the planet's gravity over 40 in tenths of a cubic meter of Endurium per landing and per launch (gravity in hundredths of a G, rising only by the square root above 8 G: 0.2 m³ at 1 G, 2.7 at the heaviest), refuses a launch without it ("Insufficient fuel for re-launching."), crashes a landing without it ("Emergency locator transmission: starship ISS ... crashed on planet ... No survivors.", ending the game), and crushes the hull on a planet above 7.99 G (194 of the 811). Once the science officer's analysis has shown the gravity, Land warns of a landing the ship would not survive or could not launch from; a second Land goes down anyway. The port now does all of it (`PD_PlayerShip`, `LandButton`, `DescendButton`, `LaunchYesButton`, `PlayerCamera.PlayerHasLanded`). Found while comparing flight with the decompiled original. The `landinggravity` and `landingwarning` probe scenarios check it (all four and one of two checks fail on the old code).
- **Low Fuel Warnings in Hyperspace**: the ship said nothing as its Endurium ran down in hyperspace and simply stopped when it ran out. The original warns at 150 steps' worth ("FUEL SUPPLY LOW", yellow), 75 ("FUEL SUPPLY CRITICAL", orange) and none ("OUT OF FUEL", pink), each with a beep (HYPER-OV `(-ENDURIUM)`, HYPERMSG-OV `WF149`, `WF163`, `WF182`); a step is an eighth of a coordinate, so with class 1 engines the warnings come at 9.0 and 4.5 cubic meters. The port now matches (`PlayerShip.WarnOfFuel`, counting the Tesseract's halved use). Found while comparing flight with the decompiled original. The `fuelwarnings` probe scenario checks all three (all fail on the old code).
- **Trade Depot Trims Oversized Orders**: typing more than the money or hold allowed gave "Insufficient funds", "Insufficient cargo space" or "Not enough in cargo hold" and traded nothing. The original cuts the volume to the most that can be traded, the figure its prompt shows (TDEPOT-OV `WEACC`: `ABS ELEM-AM @ MIN`), and trades that; so does the port now (`TradeDepotPanel.OnEndEdit`), keeping "Insufficient funds" for a player who cannot pay for any of it. Found while comparing trade with the decompiled original. The `starport-tradeclamp` probe scenario checks a purchase and a sale of 5000 cubic meters (both fail on the old code); the `starport` scenario's H4 check now expects an oversized sale to sell exactly what the hold has, and `starport-tradetexts` reaches "Insufficient funds" with no money.
- **Alien Missiles Work as in the Original**: an alien ship fired one weapon, its laser if it had one, so the Spemin, Thrynn and Veloxi warships, the Veloxi drones and the Enterprise never launched their vessels' missiles, and alien missiles chased the player's ship until they hit. In the original, COMBAT-OV rolls the laser (`WECEA`) and the missile (`WEC95`) independently, launches only while fewer than 10 alien missiles are in the air (`WDFD2`), and re-aims only the plasma bolt (`WE929`): other missiles fly to where the ship was at launch. The port now matches (`CombatController.AlienFiresAtPlayer`); an arriving missile hits if the ship is still within 100 units of that point (the port's map cell, the laser's range of 800 being the original's 7 cells). Found while comparing combat with the decompiled original. The `alienmissiles` probe scenario checks a Spemin Warship's two weapons and a miss on a ship that moved (both fail on the old code), with a hit on a ship that stayed as the control.
- **Alien Shields and the Rod Device Match the Original**: alien shields absorbed what they could of every hit and passed the rest to the armor, even when not raised (the Spemin never raise them; friendly or surrendering aliens lower them) and inside a nebula. In the original, DAMAGE-OV (`WF162`) gives a hit wholly to the shields if they have points left, are up and the fight is not in a nebula, never below 0 and with no overflow to the armor; otherwise wholly to the armor. The port now matches (`CombatController.ApplyDamageToAlien`), so a shielded ship takes two hits to destroy however big the first. The Rod Device halved alien laser hits, a choice made for the port; the original's `WED36` (`?ROD @ IF 4 /`) quarters them. Found while comparing combat with the decompiled original. The `alienshieldhits` probe scenario checks an Uhlek and a Spemin ship, and `roddevice` the quarter (all three fail on the old code); the probe's `Kill` helper hits until the ship is destroyed.
- **Towing Fine Calculated as in the Original**: a distress call's fine scaled from 15,000 to 80,000 M.U. with the distance from Arth relative to the farthest star (the port's reading of STRINFO's "range from 15000 to 80000 MU"). The original's COMM-OV (`WEF2D`, `WEF6D`) multiplies the distance from Arth (the larger axis, in its coordinates, eight per starmap coordinate) by the engines' fuel per step (7 minus their class, halved with the Tesseract) over 5 and by the in-flight value of a tenth of Endurium (twice its Starport price: SHIPBUTTONS `>FLT` doubles it at launch), with a 15,000 minimum and no maximum: better engines make a tow cheaper, dearer Endurium dearer. The port now matches (`PlayerData.GetTowingFine`); from the star farthest from Arth with class 1 engines it is 236,000 M.U. Found while comparing trade with the decompiled original. The `distress` probe scenario checks 15,000 and 236,000 (the latter fails on the old code).
- **Two Playthrough Checks No Longer Depend on Chance**: stages 4 and 5 expected exactly 13.0 cubic meters of minerals, stage 3's yield from deposits of 1 to 5 cubic meters; with the original 1.0 to 9.9 (#354) it was 20.4. They now check that minerals are aboard. Stage 13 expected an Uhlek ship destroyed before the playthrough breaks off the fight at 40% armor, a matter of luck (one run dealt 940 points of damage without a kill); it now checks that the weapons hit: a ship destroyed, or the aliens' armor and shields down. Test tooling only. Verified: the full playthrough on master after #357 failed only these three (119 of 122); stages 4, 5 and 13 pass 16 of 16 on a rerun.
- **Mineral Deposits Hold 1.0 to 9.9 Cubic Meters, as in the Original**: deposits held 1 to 5 whole cubic meters (a decision of 2026-10-05 had changed tenths to cubic meters without the original figure). The original's SEED-OV seeds a deposit with an INST-QTY of RRND 10 to 100 (`WF163`), in tenths like its holds (the terrain vehicle's 500 is 50.0 cubic meters): 1.0 to 9.9. Deposits now draw the same, still with one random number, so every deposit, rock, tree and ruin stays put (`TerrainElement.Initialize`). The terrain vehicle's hold now fills after about nine deposits instead of twenty. The `deposits` probe scenario checks the sizes on planet 90 (fails on the old code) and that the placement checksum is unchanged.
- **Aliens Under Fire No Longer Talk**: aliens the player had fired on still answered hails, and since aliens hold fire during a comm link, firing then hailing stopped their fire. In the original, fighting aliens never talk (COMM-OV `<TALK>`: `?FIGHT` leaves no chance to talk, and the player's fire outside a comm link turns them to fight). Fired-on aliens now neither hail nor answer, and an open comm link ends when the player fires, leaving the console on the menu fired from (`Encounter.AliensAreFighting`, `PlayerAttacked`); cowardly aliens still talk. Found while checking the code review's open items against the decompiled original. The `firedonsilent` probe scenario checks all three (the first two fail on the old code).
- **Radar No Longer Throws Without a Science Officer**: the radar asks the science officer whether to report motion (`Radar.ScienceOfficerReportsMotion`), and with none assigned, as in a crewless ship started in the Editor's Spaceflight scene, the personnel lookup threw an ArgumentException whenever something moved (found by the full probe run, in `debrispictures`). Now nobody reports it, as every other crew lookup already checks `IsAssigned`. The `motiondetected` probe scenario has a check for it (fails on the old code).
- **Code Review Low Items Rechecked**: the Low list of the code review of 2026-10-03 was last updated at #80. Rechecked against master and the decompiled original on 2026-10-14, nine entries, fixed or no longer applicable, are marked Closed; the original settled two open ones, fixed in their own PRs (deposit sizes, fighting aliens not talking).
