This PR closes two more: a game loaded on the surface of a planet whose maps could not be generated now tells the player why, as one loaded in orbit does (`StarSystem`; the `unmapped` scenario's new check fails on the old code), and `TerrainArtifact`, groundwork no scene, prefab or code used (artifacts live in `TerrainRuins`), is deleted.
- **Terrain Vehicle Out of Fuel No Longer Strands the Crew**: with an empty tank Move only beeped, and with no Distress on the vehicle's console the crew were stranded until a save was loaded (found by the playthrough test on planet 90, where a vehicle below the liquid line burns fuel seven times as fast). In the original, MOVE-OV says "OUT OF FUEL" and "RETURNING TO SHIP ON FOOT", leaves the cargo on the ground where the vehicle stopped (>DEBRIS) and returns the crew to the ship, where the vehicle is refuelled. The port now matches (`TVCargoButton.DropAllCargo`), and the cargo can be picked up again. The `outoffuel` probe scenario checks the messages, the crew aboard, the cargo left behind and the full tank (all four fail on the old code).
- **Terrain Vehicle Re-Boards Only Beside the Ship**: stopping Move within 2 km of the ship put the vehicle aboard, so after landing on an artifact site the crew could not stop at the ruin beside the ship (found by the playthrough test once distance was measured along the ground). The original boards only on the ship's own map cell, where the console shows 0 KM. (BEHAV-OV `?EMBARK`: the vehicle's grid position equals the ship's; a move is 1 km), so the port now boards within half a move, 0.5 km (`MoveButton.c_boardingDistanceKm`). The `reboard` probe scenario checks 0.3 km (in), 1 km (out, fails on the old code) and 5 km (out).
- **Terrain Vehicle Can Re-Board a Ship on Raised Ground**: Move measured the distance to the ship in 3D, from the vehicle's saved position, which is flat (ground height is only drawn), to the ship standing on the ground. On raised ground the ship was hundreds of units above that, so Move and Enter beside the ship never took the crew in, leaving only a save to load (found by the playthrough test on every planet tried). The distance is now measured along the ground, as the vehicle's display already did. The `reboard` probe scenario checks it on planet 94, with a control 5 km away (the 1 km check fails on the old code).
- **Veloxi Orbit Permission Survives a Load**: the drones' permission to orbit, given after three right answers, lived only in the Spaceflight scene, so loading a game in orbit, or launching from Sphexi after a load, had them ask again (found by the playthrough test). It is now saved (`PD_General.m_orbitPermissionList`, read through `GetOrbitPermissions`, since older saves lack it) and still ends in hyperspace. The `orbitpermission` probe scenario checks both (the saved permission fails on the old code).
- **No More "Outside Orbital Range." Mid-Orbit**: Enter over a planet stopped the engines, but the ship coasted out of range during the two-second fade into orbit, and the star system said "Outside orbital range." between "Initiating orbital maneuver..." and "...Orbit established." (found by the playthrough test on every orbit). The ship now holds still once the maneuver begins. The `orbitentry` probe scenario checks it with a ship leaving the range at full speed (fails on the old code).
- **Tow Messages Come Before the Victory Bonus**: a distress call after the win put Interstel's bonus in the text window before "Initiating cryogenic stasis.", "Retrieval complete, crew revived." and the towing charges, since the tow's lines followed the docking bay's own (found by the playthrough test). They now come first. The `towbonus` probe scenario checks the order (fails on the old code).
- **Drone Encounter Ends Before "...Orbit Established."**: after the Veloxi drones granted permission to orbit, the text window said "...Orbit established." before "Leaving encounter..." and "...Encounter terminated.", since returning to orbit prints its line on arrival (found by the playthrough test). The encounter now announces its end first (`Encounter.SayLeavingEncounter`). The `drones` probe scenario checks the order (fails on the old code).
- **Two Flaky Probe Scenarios Fixed**: the full run on c9a071ee failed `systemdamage` and `lifebehaviour`, though nothing they test had changed. Since the combat wounds, about one hit in seven through the shields falls on the crew rather than a system (DAMAGE-OV picks one of the fields), so the single hit in `systemdamage` sometimes damaged no system; it is now repeated on an undamaged ship until it hits one. In `lifebehaviour` a dangerous lifeform attacks every half second, and over the 4 s check a few unlucky blows could kill the whole crew (only the captain's vitality was set); now the whole crew starts at full vitality and the attack is read as soon as it comes. Test tooling only. Verified: both pass in three runs each.
- **Five New Probe Scenarios No Longer Time Out**: `encounterviewscale`, `commpicture`, `tvdistance`, `starport-training` and `starport-noticetyping` (#296, #297, #299, #300 and #301) ran all their checks but never called `Finish`, so the full run on 9b7e91c8 reported "RESULT timeout" from the probe's 120 s watchdog. Each now ends with `Finish( "scenario=... checks=...", 0 )` (`commpicture` with code 2 if the comm screen does not come on), and `add_scenario.py` rejects a scenario without a `Finish` call. Verified: all five pass their checks and exit 0.
- **Crystal Cone Stays Quiet During the Black Egg's Countdown**: in orbit around the Crystal Planet with the Crystal Cone aboard and an armed Black Egg below, the text window showed "Captain, we are receiving a countdown transmission from the Black Egg...", then the Cone's "located the control nexus", then 5, 4 and so on (found by the playthrough test). The Cone now skips the nexus report while an egg counts down in the star system (`BlackEgg.IsArmedEggInStarSystem`): the planet is about to go. The `nexusegg` probe scenario checks the orbit's lines (fails on the old code).
- **Developer Button Removed From the Viewport**: a white "Regen Starfield" button for regenerating the star field (`InfiniteStarfield.InitFromUnityButton`) sat in the bottom left of every Spaceflight view (space, landing, docking bay), builds included. It is now switched off in the scene; a developer can re-enable it in the Inspector (`UI/Starfield Regen Button`). Found while bringing the landing view into the UI kit: the landing's console and the docking bay's landing (doors and sirens) already have the kit's look.
- **Black Egg Can Now Be Dropped**: the egg is armed by dropping it from the terrain vehicle's cargo list, but a bought or found egg sits in the ship's hold, which the terrain vehicle never drew from, so the game could not be won. In the original every disembark moves the Black Egg and the Flat Device from the ship's hold into the terrain vehicle's (`WEE39` in the decompiled terrain vehicle overlay `TV-OV`, called on disembark; in DOSBox the vehicle's cargo list shows nothing else from the ship's hold, and the ship's Cargo can only jettison). The port now matches (`DisembarkButton`); what is left in the terrain vehicle comes back aboard with its cargo, as before. Verified with the `tvtakesegg` probe scenario: on the old code the egg and the Flat Device stay in the ship and the list has nothing to drop (1 of 4 checks); with the fix, 4 of 4.
- **Save Panel Shows Stardates Day First**: save slots showed the stored year-month-day stardate (4620-03-15), while the rest of the game (the Operations panel, the bank, the ship's log) shows day-month-year as the original does (15-03-4620). `SaveGamePanel.UpdateDescriptions` now uses `PD_General.GetDisplayStardate`; the stored form is unchanged. The `savedata` probe scenario's new date line check fails on the old code and passes on the new.
- **Ruin Sign No Longer Shows "[NUMBER]"**: the Mardan 4 sign that STRINFO puts at "several locations" was logged as "SIGN - MARDAN 4 MINING STATION NO. [NUMBER]": the recovered data kept STRINFO's field for the station number, and nothing filled it in. It now reads NO. 17, the number in the shipped game's own message record (the decompiled original's `instance.txt` has the sign twice, NO. 82 and NO. 17; the first is message 28). Found in the STRINFO message audit (`Research/STRINFO message audit.md`, roadmap Phase 7). The `recovereddata` probe scenario's new check (no planet message keeps a bracketed field) fails on the old data and passes on the new; `ruins` still passes.
- **Latitude and Longitude No Longer Swapped**: the landing map's LAT field showed the east-west position and LONG the north-south one ("LAT 180W  LONG 90S" where the original reads "LAT 15N  LONG 30W"), and the coordinates above the main view put east-west first ("30 W   15 N" for the original's "15N x 30W"). The port's `m_selectedLatitude` holds east-west and `m_selectedLongitude` north-south; both displays now show each where the original does (`TerrainMapDisplay.MoveCrosshairs`, `Viewport.UpdateCoordinates`). Found in the screen comparison (roadmap Phase 7). Both checks of the `coordinates` probe scenario fail before the fix and pass after.
- **Blank Line Before GAME OVER and CANCELLED**: the game-ending flare read "...AND CREW WERE INCINERATED." with "GAME OVER" on the next line, and a distress call after the Starport is gone read "THERE'S NO RESPONSE!" with "CANCELLED" on the next. STRINFO 2.14 prints a blank line before each, and the shipped game two newlines before "GAME OVER" (HYPERMSG-OV in the decompiled original). The two story texts in `Starflight Recovered Data.json` now have the blank line; nothing else changes. Found in the STRINFO message audit (`Research/STRINFO message audit.md`, section 6). The `arthflare` probe scenario's two new checks fail on the old data and pass on the new; `gameover`, `distress` and `recovereddata` pass.
- **Ship Wrecks Keep Their Hulls**: the debris models under `Assets/Game Objects/Ships Debris/` are the ships' own meshes with faces knocked out (`process_ship_debris.py`), but were imported with blank white `Standard` materials that nothing replaced: a Spemin scout's wreck was stark white facets where its textured hull had been, and the player ship's wreck (F10, or any loss) was invisible in hyperspace, white `Standard` with no light. A wreck now takes its ship's materials (`ShipWreck`, from `Encounter.SpawnDebrisForShip` and `CombatController.SpawnPlayerDebris`, matched by renderer name; the debris meshes keep the ships' material slots and texture coordinates), scorched dark with the ship's lights off, and glows with embers that cool over six seconds to a smoulder that keeps it visible. Colours are set with material property blocks, so a wreck makes no material copies. The `debrispictures` probe scenario checks both wrecks' materials (both fail on the old code) and renders them with `-Graphics`.
- **Analysis Typo "Senor" Fixed**: Analysis with no sensor reading said "I need a current senor reading.", where the original says "I NEED A CURRENT SENSOR READING." (ANALYZE-OV in the decompiled original, s-macke/starflight-reverse). Found in the probe's new encounter pictures (roadmap Phase 7). The `noreading` probe scenario fails before the fix and passes after.
- **Two Notes in the Recovered Data Corrected**: the Black Egg site's note in `Starflight Recovered Data.json` put the microfilm message pointing to it on Arth, while STRINFO 3.1 puts it at random places on another planet, as the port's own microfilm record has it. And the file's `m_about`, like `ROADMAP.md`, said the planet-from-sun reading holds in 15 of the 16 STRINFO entries that also print an orbit number: there are 18, and 17 agree with the game data's orbits (counted by script; the 18th is Koann, decision D5). Found in the audit of the recovered texts against STRINFO (roadmap Phase 7). Neither text appears in the game; the `recovereddata` probe scenario still passes.
- **Roadmap Phase 7 Records Where the Comparison Stands**: `ROADMAP.md` now records the screen comparison of 2026-10-11 and the audit of the recovered texts against STRINFO of 2026-10-12: which differences the PRs since #215 fixed, which two await a hand test, and what is left (the UI kit's screens, the landing crosshair, the security code and the Interstel Police under D4, and the game data's own wording).
- **Screen Pictures Cover the Missing Screens**: the probe's `screenpictures` scenario took the sensors in orbit without a scan, hyperspace at Arth's own position (where the view was its sun) and an encounter with no reading or comm link, too little for the screen comparison to judge. It now takes 12 pictures: a real planet scan and its analysis, hyperspace away from any star, and a real scan of a Spemin scout (the encounter's own, waited on until the reading is in), its analysis and the comm link. It clears the folder's older pictures first. The scout's reading matches the original's screenshot: mass 4x10^2, bio 100, energy 100. Test tooling only.
- **Supplemental Evaluations Use the Shipped Game's Wording**: the Operations room's three supplemental evaluations kept three slips of STRINFO's transcription: "CONSEQUENLY, YOU HAVE BEEN FINED 10,000 MU" for the lost terrain vehicle, and "LIABLE FOR ANY COSTS" and "ON BEHALF SAID CREW" in the towing charges. They now read CONSEQUENTLY, LIABLE FOR ALL COSTS and ON BEHALF OF SAID CREW, the shipped game's words (OP-OV in the decompiled original), and each, like the mission completion, has STRINFO's blank line after its SUBJECT line. Only `Starflight Recovered Data.json` changes. Found in the STRINFO message audit (`Research/STRINFO message audit.md`, section 3, roadmap Phase 7). The `recovereddata` probe scenario's new check fails on the old data and passes on the new; `distress` still passes.
- **Status Display Uses the Original Wording**: the Status display showed "None" for missing shields and weapons (the original: SHIELDS: DOWN, WEAP: UNARMED), the energy as "20.0M3" (the original: "20.00", four figures, no unit), the cargo as "40.0% Full" ("40 %") and the damage as "10% Hull Damage" where the original gives a word. The words and their bands are the shipped game's own table (STARB.COM, the status display's code), by the hull left: NONE at all of it, MINIMAL from 95%, SLIGHT from 80%, MODERATE from 50%, HEAVY from 20% and CRITICAL below (`StatusDisplay.GetDamageText`, `GetEnergyText`). Found in the screen comparison (roadmap Phase 7). The `statusdisplay` probe scenario's three checks fail before the fix and pass after; the `m11` and `perframe` checks of the damage line now read the words.
- **Disembark Messages Match the Original**: disembarking said "Stand by, scanning planet..." instead of the original's "STANDBY...SCANNING PLANET." (SHIPBUTTONS in the decompiled original, s-macke/starflight-reverse) and skipped "HULL CHECK COMPLETE" (HP-OV), between "INITIATING HULL INTEGRITY CHECK" and "ACTIVATING TERRAIN VEHICLE". `DisembarkButton` now says both, 0.4 seconds apart instead of 0.65, so all four fit in the map's 2-second fade (the third already came at 1.95). Found in the screen comparison (roadmap Phase 7). The `disembarklines` probe scenario (a real disembark on planet 94) fails before the fix and passes after.
- **Probe Line Matching Handles Name Placeholders**: the probe helpers `SaysOneOf`, `SaysALineOfAnyStance` and `SaysALineOf` matched game data lines with `Contains`, but `Encounter.AddComm` first fills in the player's ship (`&`) and captain (`*`) and the aliens' captain (`%`) and ship (`+`). So when the Thrynn chose a greeting with a name (380, 381, 383, "Thiss isss captainn % of the Thrynn vesssel +"), the `rivals` check of a Thrynn crew member's translation failed despite a correct translation, and the Veloxi surrender line 486 ("I captain %") could fail `cowardice` the same way. The helpers now use `SaysLine`, which splits a line at its placeholders and finds each piece in order.
- **Perframe Probe Check No Longer Fails in Storms**: the `perframe` scenario checks that the terrain vehicle's display changes as the vehicle moves, but a storm makes the bearing read LOST! everywhere, so the check failed whenever the hourly weather rolled one (seen in a full run on 2026-10-12, after #210 added storms). The scenario now stops the weather first, like `tvdisplay`. Test tooling only.
- **Intermittent Commlink Missile Check Fixed**: the `commlink` scenario lets Spemin scouts and the Uhlek fire on the ship for about 30 s before it launches two missiles. Since systems became damageable (#145, roadmap 1.2), each hit that gets through also takes a share of the bare hull's 250 points from one random installed system. 100,000 armor points kept the hull alive, but occasionally the hits destroyed the missile launcher, so `CombatController.CanFireMissile` refused both launches and both player missile checks failed with `playerMissile=hit0/timeout0/explosions0` (two full runs, 2026-10-11 and 2026-10-12; passing runs show the same spread, a first missile doing 40, 34, 23, 8 or 2 damage). The scenario now repairs the systems and readies the launcher before each launch, logging the launcher health it found. With the launcher destroyed by hand first, the old scenario fails both checks and the new one passes; five plain runs pass 8 of 8, hitting for 40 each time. Test tooling only.
- **Thrynn Artifact Offer Worded as in the Original**: the Thrynn offer for an artifact read "...aboard your sship a Tesseract thiss artifact iss of interesst to uss. We will buy itt from you for 29 cubic Arth unitxx of energy crysstalsss": the template field "unitxx" was printed raw, the name ran into the next sentence, and the full price was quoted even when the hold had room for less, though only what fitted was paid. The shipped game (COMMSPEC-OV in the decompiled original) fills in "a" or "an", the name with a full stop, "UNITSS", or "UNIT" for one, and the smaller of the price and the hold's free room, which is also what it pays. The port now matches (`Encounter.GetThrynnArtifactPayment`, `FindThrynnOffer`). Found in the STRINFO message audit (`Research/STRINFO message audit.md`, roadmap Phase 7). In the `thrynntrades` probe scenario, the two new checks (the wording, and a hold with room for 10) fail before the fix and pass after; its five other checks, `rivals`, `aliennames` and `homeworlds` pass.
- **Terrain Vehicle Date and Bearing Match the Original**: the terrain vehicle's display gave the date with the hour ("01.00-01-4620", the ship's format), not the original's "DATE : 01-01-4620", and shortened the bearing to the ship ("14 KM. N") that the original spells out ("1KM. NORTH", "NORTHWEST"). It now shows a day-month-year date (`PD_General.GetDisplayStardate`) and the full bearing (`TerrainVehicleDisplay.c_cardinalDirections`). Found in the screen comparison (roadmap Phase 7). Both `tvdisplay` probe checks fail before the fix and pass after.
- **Comm Link and Encounter Endings Announced**: the port was silent when a comm link ended or the ship left an encounter. The original says "COMMUNICATIONS HAVE BEEN TERMINATED" (COMM-OV in the decompiled original, s-macke/starflight-reverse), then "LEAVING ENCOUNTER..." and "...ENCOUNTER TERMINATED" (built in STARB.COM from the words of "LEAVING STARPORT..." and "...STARPORT CLEAR"), as its Motion Detected screenshot shows. `Encounter.Disconnect` and `LeaveEncounter` now say them. Found in the screen comparison (roadmap Phase 7). Both `encounterend` probe checks fail before the fix and pass after.
- **Colony Fines Follow the Shipped Game**: an unsuitable recommended planet cost 100 MU the first time, then 5000 MU per reason, a rule of the port's own, as STRINFO 1.2 only says later fines "vary with how unsuitable the planet is". The shipped game counts the unsuitable planets recommended so far (CAPTAIN overlay WF348 and WF292, OP-OV WED0C, in the decompiled original): 10,000 MU for each earlier one, up to 8, never under 100 MU. So the fines run 100 MU with the warning, then 10,000, 20,000 and on to 80,000 MU from the ninth on, whatever the reasons. `PlayerData.EvaluateColonyRecommendations` now does this (`c_unsuitableFineStep`, `c_unsuitableFineSteps`). Found in the STRINFO message audit (`Research/STRINFO message audit.md`). In the `colonies` probe scenario, the changed check (the second planet fined 10,000 MU) and the new one (20,000 after 2, 80,000 after 8 and after 12) fail before the fix and pass after; the seven other checks pass.
- **Planet Atmospheres Glow Instead of Showing a Hard Ring**: an atmosphere (in the star system and in orbit) is a flat quad (`Square.fbx`) only 5.5% larger than the planet, drawn with `Atmosphere - Opacity.png`, a texture dark in the middle, brightest at the planet's edge and sharply cut off just beyond it: a one-colour band with a hard outer edge, the same by day and night. The quad also sat at 85% of the planet's depth, inside it (0.8 radii below its top), hiding the texture's haze over the disk, and most game data atmosphere colours are dark (planet 90's is 0.13, 0, 0.2), which an additive band all but loses. A dedicated shader (`Assets/Resources/Shaders/Planet Atmosphere.shader`) now works out per pixel how close the line of sight passes to the planet's centre: a halo fading from the edge into space and a haze thickening towards the edge of the disk, both lit by the planet's sun, with a faint fringe at the terminator. `Planet.FitAtmosphere` puts the quad just above the planet, sizes it for the halo, raises the colour to full brightness and lets density set the strength, measuring the planet from its mesh (a rotated sphere's renderer bounds are up to 41% too big). The `atmospherepictures` probe scenario checks the halo room, the quad's place in front of the planet and the colour (all three fail on the old code), and renders the planets with `-Graphics`.
- **Evaluation Screen Shows One Evaluation per Page**: the Operations evaluation screen poured every evaluation into one text that grew with each docking and ran off the screen, and repeated the mission completion on every visit after the win. The original pages them (OP-OV in the decompiled original): first Interstel's supplemental evaluations (towing charges, the lost terrain vehicle, mission completion), one per page and shown once, then the recommended planets' evaluations, kept and shown one per page on every visit, the last followed by "END OF EVALUATIONS". The port now does the same, using the notices screen's More button (hidden on the last page) and Quit for the original's "PRESS SPACEBAR FOR NEXT EVALUATION" and "[ QUIT ]". Colony evaluations get their own saved list (`PD_General.m_colonyEvaluationList`; `GetColonyEvaluationList` moves an older save's out of the supplemental ones), and `m_missionCompleteShown` shows the win's evaluation once. Found in the STRINFO message audit (`Research/STRINFO message audit.md`). The three new `starport-win` probe checks (the pages of a first visit, the next one, and an older save) fail before the fix and pass after; `colonies`, `starport-input`, `starport-savedata`, `savedata`, `distress` and `lava` pass.
- **Transporter Effect Shimmers Instead of Drawing Opaque Squares**: `TransporterEffect` (the terrain vehicle picking up a deposit or artifact) loaded `Resources/Starport Transporter Light.prefab` for particles, but that prefab is just a point light, so every pickup fell back to sparkles with an untextured `Particles/Standard Unlit` material: tiny opaque squares. It also moved the object's materials to a transparent queue with depth writes off "so alpha changes work", though nothing changed their alpha; this only lifted the object out of the terrain fog, so a fogged deposit flashed bright gold when picked up. The effect now shimmers with rising, twinkling blue sparkles sized to the object, a pad of light on the ground and a blue point light, drawn with the shared `EffectMaterials` on a separate object so the last sparkles outlive the object, which keeps its materials and still shrinks away in 1.5 s. The `transporterpictures` probe scenario picks up a deposit, checks the shaders and the object's rendering (both fail on the old code) and renders the pickup with `-Graphics`.
- **Notices and Two Analyses Use the Shipped Game's Wording**: the game data's Starport notices and artifact analyses, typed from STRINFO, carried six of its slips or typos: "penalized recommendations of planets" (02-01-4620), "lost several ships in this vicinity" (05-01-4620), "Extensive experience on board the ISS Intrepid" (23-01-4620, Ihsss T'larra), "minimial or no life" (06-03-4620), the Hypercube's "atarxia" and the Rod Device's "laer shield". They now read as in the shipped game (notices from the decompiled original's `instance.txt`, analyses from `data.h`): "penalized for recommendations", "in its vicinity", "experience as communications officer on board", "minimal", "ataraxia" and "laser shield". A decision of 2026-10-12 makes these six words the only change to `Starflight Game Data.json`. The Humming Gizzy's price and the Shimmering Ball's volume, where STRINFO also differs, stay: the original's own data (`Research/Data/Starflight 1 Data.xls`) has the game data's 500 MU and 0.3 m3. Found in the STRINFO message audit (`Research/STRINFO message audit.md`, sections 2 and 4, which now record the decision). The new `recovereddata` probe check fails on the old game data and passes on the new.
- **Alien Lines Rotate, Least Used First**: the aliens (and the player's crew) picked a fitting line at random, without repeats within a meeting. The shipped game never does: it says the line used least so far, the first in data order on a tie (FIND-PHRASE in COMM-OV, the decompiled original), keeping the counts in its phrase records, so each race's lines take turns across all its meetings. `Encounter.FindComm` now does the same, saving the counts in `PD_General.m_commUseList` (an older save starts every line at 0); the Noah 9 derelict's order from #264 is now just this rule, carried across meetings. Answers to questions keep their own order. Adopted after the STRINFO message audit (`Research/STRINFO message audit.md`). The new `comms` probe check (a Thrynn patrol's statements in two meetings: 316 to 322, then 327 and 328) fails on the old code (random order) and passes on the new; the `transmissions` check of the Noah 9 beacon now tests the rule across meetings, and the 28 other encounter scenarios, `savedata` and `h7` pass.
- **Noah 9 Beacon Lines in the Shipped Order**: in orbit of the Noah 9 wreck, the derelict's two lines, the officer's "Captain, we're picking up a distress beacon signal" and "SOS... SOS", came in random order, so the SOS could come first. The shipped game picks nothing at random: it says the subject's least-used line, the first in the data on a tie (FIND-PHRASE in COMM-OV, the decompiled original), so the report comes first, then the SOS, then they alternate, as STRINFO 2.11 prints them. `Encounter.FindComm` now takes the derelict's lines in game data order, the first not yet said in the meeting; other races keep the random pick among unsaid lines. Found in the STRINFO message audit (`Research/STRINFO message audit.md`, section 6). The new `transmissions` probe check (the first two lines in three meetings) fails on the old code (the third meeting opened with the SOS) and passes on the new; the other three checks pass on both.
- **Star System Travel Messages Match the Original**: the port said "Ship is within orbital range." over every planet, nothing on Maneuver after launch, only "Leaving orbit..." on leaving orbit, the stellar parameters again on leaving a planet's range, and nothing about the docking bay doors on arrival. It now follows the original (screenshots starflt_140 to 142 and 075, with STARB.COM's wording): "Leaving orbit..." and "...Commencing system travel." on leaving the Starport or an orbit (`ManeuverButton`), "Outside orbital range." on leaving a planet's range, "Standing by to dock with Starport." over Arth and "Standing by to orbit." with "Press Enter to orbit." over other planets (`StarSystem.Update`; the original uses the spacebar), and "Closing docking bay doors." first on arrival at the Starport (`DockingBay.StartLandingAnimation`, `Messages.InsertText`). Found in the screen comparison (roadmap Phase 7). The six `systemtravel` probe checks fail before the fix and pass after; the `unmapped` check now expects the new orbital range wording.
- **Terrain Vehicle No Longer Shakes, Creeps or Jumps Near Obstacles**: deposits and trees push back an overlapping terrain vehicle (`TerrainVehicleCollider`, `TerrainVehicle.AddPushBack`), but the push was only drawn: `Update` drew the vehicle at its saved position plus the push, then forgot it, so a vehicle against a deposit flickered in and out of it every other frame. The push also built up over all physics steps before the next frame (a frame of a third of a second threw the drawn vehicle about 130 units), and a merely touching contact (positive separation) raised the speed, so a standing vehicle crept. Now only an overlap pushes, along the ground, at most 0.5 per contact per physics step (a design decision of 2026-10-11), applied to the saved position and transform at once so the next step does not push again for the same overlap; the vehicle moves from its saved position, and nothing moves it while paused. Driving into a deposit still stops the vehicle. The `pushback` probe scenario covers it (9 of 16 checks fail on the old code).
- **New Missile and Laser Effects**: `MissileProjectile` drew a grey capsule with a lit `Standard` material and a trail of untextured `Particles/Standard Unlit` squares that kept piling up behind a missile held still by the pause. `LaserBeam` called `Resources.Load( "LaserRed_Mat" )`, but that material is in `Assets/_MyCombatAssets/`, not a Resources folder, so every beam fell back to an untextured `Particles/Standard Unlit` material (its `_Mode` float alone does not change the blending) that the camera's bloom turned into a soft red line. A missile is now a glowing warhead with a continuous flame streak (a `TrailRenderer` with the laser beam texture, unbroken at any speed) and a smoke trail emitted by distance, its particles pausing with the game. A laser is a coloured glow around a white-hot core (two lines with a beam texture made in code, `EffectMaterials.m_beamMaterial`), with a muzzle flash, a sparking flare where it strikes and a flicker as it fades; a plasma bolt is the same in green. Timings are unchanged (a beam lasts 0.3 s, a missile flies at 500 a second). The `weaponpictures` probe scenario checks the shaders and the paused exhaust (2 of 3 checks fail on the old code) and renders them with `-Graphics`.
- **Home Fleets Speak Their Shipped Homeworld Lines**: a home fleet stepped one counter through hails, responses and refusals together, so the Veloxi fleet skipped its first refusal ("Us are not answer the questions", STRINFO 2.2), answered a hail with "Of course us is Veloxi. Now preparing to dying you..." and attacked at once, and the Thrynn fleet ended its warnings on "You arre nnot wellcome in thiss ssspace", not STRINFO's "You must pay the consequences". The shipped game's phrase records say where each line is used (the PHRASECONTRL context, read in by #259): those two lines belong to space, and the Gazurtoid's "the holy land, the planet of the Gazurtoid" only to their homeworld, where a Gazurtoid fleet waits (encounter 65). `GD_Comm.m_homeworld` now comes from that context; a home fleet is an orbit encounter at its race's homeworld (STRINFO 5.2, `GameData.IsHomeworldOf`), excluding the Gazurtoid at the Shimmering Ball's planet; each list has its own saved count (`PD_Encounter.m_homeworldWarningCounts`); and a subject with no homeworld line gets a refusal, where the shipped game says a subject 5 line (PHRASE-SEARCH). Nor does a home fleet tire of talking any more (the 1984 notes' phrase limit could end a Veloxi fleet's comm link with "Enough the talking" before its last warning): its talk ends with that warning. Found in the STRINFO message audit (`Research/STRINFO message audit.md`, section 1.4). The changed and new `homeworlds` probe checks (the Thrynn attack after their third hail, the Veloxi answer 473 and holding fire, then their four hails, the Gazurtoid fleet's homeworld hail) fail before the fix and pass after, with two controls; `shippedlines`, `commlink`, `cowardice`, `rivals`, `comms`, `mood`, `encounterdata`, `encounterend`, `drones`, `transmissions`, `alienshields`, `savedata` and `h7` pass.
- **Landing Messages in the Original Order**: the landing said "Topography net locked on." 6 seconds in, still in orbit, then "Retro rockets firing...", which the original lacks. The original's DESCEND (ORBIT-OV in the decompiled original, s-macke/starflight-reverse) says "COMPUTING DESCENT PROFILE...", "AUTOPILOT ENGAGED. DESCENDING...", "TOPOGRAPHY NET LOCKED ON" as the terrain grid appears, then "SAFE LANDING, CAPTAIN.". The topography line now comes with the terrain (the landing animation's event, `PlayerCamera.TopographyNetLockedOn`, replacing `RetroRocketsFiring`), not on a timer in `DescendButton`. Found in the screen comparison (roadmap Phase 7). Both checks of the `landinglines` probe scenario, a real landing on planet 94, fail before the fix and pass after; `m23`'s landing check now expects the original's four lines.
- **New Hull and Shield Hit Effects**: like the old explosions, `HullHitEffect` and `ShieldHitEffect` used untextured `Particles/Standard Unlit` and lit `Standard` materials. The hull hit threw square dots and grey quads along the shot's direction (`LookRotation( hitDirection )`, into the ship) from the ship's centre, with debris that lived 1 s but was switched off in mid-air at 0.6 s; the shield hit was a pale lit disk, growing to twice its size and covered in square blue dots. All three effects now share the explosion's textures, materials and shard meshes (`EffectMaterials`). A hull hit strikes the side the shot came from, with a flash, hot spark streaks, a few glowing shards with short trails thrown back towards the shooter and a puff of smoke, all gone by 0.55 s. A shield hit lights the bubble with its own shader (`Assets/Resources/Shaders/Shield Bubble.shader`): a faint rim, a hot spot where the shot struck (on the shooter's side, slightly above the flight plane so the ripple reads as a ring), a ripple spreading across the bubble and blue sparks, for 0.45 s (was 0.4 s). `CombatController` gives both effects the shot's direction, so an alien ship hit by the player throws sparks back towards the player. The `hitpictures` probe scenario checks the shaders, particle lifetimes and the side the hits land on (4 of 5 checks fail on the old code) and renders them with `-Graphics`.
- **Alien Ship Analysis Worded as in the Original**: Analysis always gave an alien ship's shields and weapons as "Not certain" and its size as "Size: 2.0 times the size of our ship". It now follows the original's `.SHIELDS`, `.WEAP` and `.SIZE` (ANALYZE-OV in the decompiled original, s-macke/starflight-reverse, matching its 24 analysis screenshots in `Research/Screenshots/Ships/`): shields "None apparent" for a vessel without any, "Unknown" for the Gazurtoids, else "Class n equivalent" (vessel shields over 200) "raised" or "lowered"; weapons "Armed" or "Not armed"; and an unlabelled size, the whole number of times our ship's mass, or "0." and the decimals to the first non-zero digit ("2 times the size of our ship.", "0.04"). Raised and armed follow the original's encounter rule (COMM-OV, `<AUX>`): shields are up if a ship in the encounter has shields and the posture is not friendly, never for the Spemin; weapons are armed if a ship has weapons and the posture is hostile; both are down while the aliens surrender (`Encounter.AliensHaveShieldsUp`, `AliensAreArmed`). The type, shields and weapon lines each roll the science officer's skill and say "Not certain" on a failure, like the original's `?.CERTAIN`. Not matched: the original shows Mysterion and Enterprise shields lowered, but the port treats races without a mood as neutral, so they show raised. Found in the screen comparison (roadmap Phase 7). The six `alienanalysis` probe checks fail before the fix and pass after; the `cargo` size check now expects the original's format.
- **Explosions Rebuilt With a Dedicated Particle Shader**: `ExplosionEffect` made its materials with `new Material( Shader.Find( "Particles/Standard Unlit" ) )` and no texture, drawing opaque untextured quads: a hard yellow square for the flash, ten opaque near-black smoke squares filling the view within two seconds, square spark dots and lit grey debris cubes (`Standard`), with the player ship's wreck hidden under the smoke. Seen in the Editor after F10. The explosion now has its own particle shader, `Assets/Resources/Shaders/Explosion Particle.shader`, in a Resources folder so every build includes it (`Shader.Find` only finds shaders the build refers to; a Windows build lists it among the Resources assets). It blends premultiplied with a per-material occlusion value: flash, sparks and the faint shockwave ring only add light, fire also hides some of what is behind it so it stays orange over a blue planet, and smoke and debris cover it. Its textures are made once in code from hashed noise (not `Random`, which the planets depend on): a white-hot flash, ragged fire puffs cooling from yellow to deep red, a faint shockwave ring in the flight plane, spark streaks, tumbling hull shards that glow and cool to dark metal with short glowing trails, and brown-grey smoke, plus an orange point light for half a second. Timing is unchanged (the caller is told at 1.5 s, the explosion switched off at 2.2 s). The particles now follow `SetScale`, so a missile hit gets the half-size explosion `CombatController` requests (each particle system used to scale only with its own transform). The `explosionpictures` probe scenario checks materials, meshes and scale (3 of 4 checks fail on the old code), and the new `probe.ps1 -Graphics` renders the camera at fixed game times for before and after pictures.
- **Coordinate Bar Updates After Returning From the Surface**: back from the surface, the bar above the main view kept the terrain vehicle's position ("1S x 0E") in orbit and in the star system until the ship moved; the original's orbit shows the ship's coordinates ("125 100", starflt_143). `SpaceflightController.SwitchLocation` now writes the bar on every location change. Found in the screen comparison (roadmap Phase 7). Both `coordinatebar` probe checks (orbit and star system) fail before the fix and pass after.
- **Obsolete API Warnings Removed From Standard Assets Image Effects**: a full recompile listed 7 `CS0618` warnings from `Assets/3rd Party/Standard Assets/Effects/ImageEffects/Scripts`. Removed: the three `RenderTexture.MarkRestoreExpected()` calls (MotionBlur, Tonemapping), which Unity says do nothing, and the `SystemInfo.supportsImageEffects` checks (Blur, NoiseAndScratches, PostEffectsBase, ScreenSpaceAmbientOcclusion), which never disabled anything, since Unity says it always returns true. The effects behave as before.
- **Distress Call Asks for Confirmation First**: Distress towed the ship home at once, refused in the docking bay ("You're safely docked. No need for a distress signal.") and in an encounter ("We're already in contact with another vessel!"), and the docking bay's own lines wiped its tow message. It now asks first, like the original's TOW-US (SHIPCON in the decompiled original, s-macke/starflight-reverse): "Transmit emergency distress call?" with No and Yes on the console (`ButtonSet.Distress`, `DistressNoButton`, `DistressYesButton`; No first, as in the original's `[N Y]`). No restores the previous buttons. Yes answers "We're already home!" in the docking bay and "Transmission is being jammed." in an encounter; otherwise it says "Initiating cryogenic stasis." and "Retrieval complete, crew revived." with the towing charges once the ship is back in the docking bay. The original allows a tow once an encounter's alien ships are all gone; the port says jammed in every encounter, since a tow would bypass `Encounter.LeaveEncounter`. Found in the screen comparison (roadmap Phase 7). The five `distressconfirm` probe checks fail before the fix and pass after; the `distress` and `arthflare` scenarios now answer Yes.
- **Encounter Start Survives an Empty Ship Model Slot**: `Encounter.Start` turned off every alien ship model template without a null check, so one empty slot in `m_alienShipModelTemplate` threw a `NullReferenceException` and stopped `Start` before the debris templates were turned off. It now skips empty slots, like the debris loop below it; `ResetAlienShipModels` still reports any vessel needing the missing model. No slot in `Spaceflight.unity` is empty today; this guards the slots while the missing ship models are added. Verified with the new `shipmodels` probe scenario, which also lists the vessels still on the placeholder model and measures every vessel's model and debris in a real encounter.
- **Launch Confirmation Uses the Original Wording**: Launch asked "Confirm launch?" with Yes before No. It now asks "Ready to launch. Confirm?" with No first, like the original's "READY TO LAUNCH / CONFIRM: [N Y]" (CAPTAIN in the decompiled original, s-macke/starflight-reverse) and the port's other confirmations. Found in the screen comparison (roadmap Phase 7). Verified with the `launchconfirm` probe scenario: its two checks of wording and order fail on the old code and pass on the new, and its control (No keeps the ship docked) passes on both. The `orbit` scenario now presses Yes by its label.
- **Alien Ship Debris Matches Its Ship**: `Encounter.SpawnDebrisForShip` gave debris its template's local scale and no rotation, so a Spemin wreck was 1.97 times the ship's size (debris models are imported with `globalScale` 19.685), a Mysterion wreck 2.41 times (`globalScale` 20), and the Spemin Transport and Warship wrecks lay pitched 90 degrees off. Debris now keeps its template's rotation and is scaled to the longest side of the destroyed ship's model, so it takes the ship's place whatever scale its model was imported at. Verified with the `shipmodels` probe scenario, which now checks the size and orientation of every vessel's debris on the frame it appears.
- **Flaky Debris Orientation Check in the `shipmodels` Probe Scenario**: the check that a wreck is oriented like its ship sometimes failed for vessel 20 ("debris=(90.6, 90.6, 90.7) ship=(90.6, 80.0, 90.6)") and passed on a rerun of the same tree. There were two causes. The Mysterion is a cloud of orbs that `Mysterion.Update` moves with game time, so its size at destruction depends on how long the encounter's first frames took (84.1 to 96.3 units wide), while its debris is a cube scaled to its longest side: from about 89 units wide, the debris height alone was off by more than the allowed tenth. And the scenario measured the debris one frame after the kill, after `DebrisTumble` had turned it by that frame's time (up to 17 degrees a second, over as much as a third of a second in a slow frame). The scenario now measures the debris right after the kill (`SpawnDebrisForShip` runs inside `ApplyDamageToAlien`) and compares its proportions with the ship template's at rest, each within a tenth of the longest side as before. With every encounter frame forced to a third of a second, the old check failed in both of two runs (vessel 20 both times, vessel 16 once) and the new one passed three times; with the debris turned 90 degrees after the kill, the new check fails for every vessel whose debris is not a cube.
- **Cyan Messages Showed Their Colour Tag**: four messages used `<color=cyan>`, a name TextMesh Pro does not know (it accepts red, lightblue, blue, grey, black, green, white, orange, purple and yellow), so the tag was printed as text: "<color=cyan>Shields absorb 100 damage!" (seen in an Editor capture of an encounter, 2026-10-09), the continuum flux warning in hyperspace, artifacts found by the terrain vehicle's scan in ruins, and an artifact's name in the cargo list. They now use `#00FFFF`. Verified with the new `colournames` probe scenario, which checks every `<color=name>` in `Assets/Scripts` against TextMesh Pro's names and renders the shield message as the message box does: both checks fail on the old code and pass on the new.
- **Colour Names Scenario Joins the Full Run**: `colournames` (from the cyan colour tag fix) is now in `run-all-scenarios.ps1` next to `sensorpictures` and counted in the probe README. It was held back from that PR because other PRs kept changing the same one-line scenario list while it was open.
- **Sensor Planet Scenario Joins the Full Run**: `sensorplanet` (the planet's own surface in the sensor window, #171) is now in `run-all-scenarios.ps1` next to `sensorpictures` and counted in the probe README (110 scenarios). It was held back from #171 because other open PRs changed the same one-line scenario list and count.
- **System Map and Sun Scenarios Join the Full Run**: `systemmap` (the realistic system map and sun colours, #183 and #186), `maplife` (the map's animations, #190) and `sunlife` (the sun's living surface, #194) are now in `run-all-scenarios.ps1` next to `m16` and `flaredata`, and counted in the probe README (127 scenarios). They were held back from those PRs because other open PRs changed the same one-line scenario list and count.
- **Black Egg Terrain Vehicle Scenario Joins the Full Run**: `tvtakesegg` (the terrain vehicle takes the Black Egg and the Flat Device out of the ship's hold, #217) now closes `run-all-scenarios.ps1` and is counted in the probe README (148 scenarios).
- **Star System View Scenario Joins the Full Run**: `systemviewpictures` (planets lit by their star, the Milky Way, orbit paths and engine glow, #245, #252 and #256) now closes `run-all-scenarios.ps1` and is counted in the probe README (152 scenarios).
- **No Percent Signs in the Sensor Readout**: the readout showed Bio and Min (or Energy, for a vessel) with a percent sign, "Bio: 100%   Energy: 100%". The original sensor screen shows plain numbers, and `SensorsDisplay` now does too, for planets, vessels and debris alike. Verified with the `sensorpictures` probe scenario, whose readout checks now expect plain numbers: 5 of its 64 checks fail on the old code (the planet, Spemin Scout, Mechan Scout, Minstrel and Spemin Scout wreck readouts) and all 64 pass on the new.
- **Planet Size in the Sensor Window Follows the Original**: a planet's disc filled 47 to 56% of the sensor window's height, a gas giant's 48 to 76%, on a smooth curve over mass. Scans of nine planets in the original (2026-10-10, 2x10^20 to 5x10^23 tons) show a different rule: below 10^22 tons the disc is 2 + 4 x the mass's leading digit pixels across (2x10^20 tons: 10 pixels, 3x10^21: 14, 4x10^21: 18, 5x10^21: 22, 6x10^21: 26), and from 10^22 tons up it is 62 pixels, in a window 57 pixels high. `SensorsDisplay.GetPlanetPictureScale` now applies that rule to the mass passed to the scan; masses under 10^20 tons and from 7x10^21 to 9x10^21 tons, not observed in the original, follow it too. The orbit and system views keep their sizes (`GD_Planet.GetScale`). Verified with a new `sensorpictures` check that measures the disc from the mask file and the window's clip: it fails on the old code (every disc 0.468 of the window's height) and passes on the new for 200, 3000, 6000, 10000 and 400000 tons (0.146, 0.205, 0.380, 0.906 and 0.906, as in the original), and all 65 checks of the scenario pass.
- **Small Planets Sized Correctly in the Sensor Window**: the rule from "Planet Size in the Sensor Window Follows the Original" sized planets under 2x10^21 tons by their mass's leading digit, so a 6x10^19 or 6x10^20 ton planet got a 26 pixel disc and a 1x10^21 ton planet a 6 pixel one. Six more planets scanned in the original (2026-10-11) show that every planet under 10^21 tons is 10 pixels across (6x10^19, 2x10^20 and 6x10^20 tons), as is 1x10^21 tons, while 7 and 9x10^21 tons give 30 and 38 pixels, matching 2 + 4 x the leading digit. `SensorsDisplay.GetPlanetPictureScale` now draws a 10 pixel disc under 10^21 tons and nothing smaller from 10^21 to 10^22 tons. Verified with the `sensorpictures` probe scenario, whose disc check now covers 60, 200, 600, 1000, 3000, 6000, 7000, 9000, 10000 and 400000 tons: the old code fails for 60, 600 and 1000 tons (0.380, 0.380 and 0.088 of the window's height, against 0.146 in the original); with the new code all ten match and all 66 checks pass.
- **Corrected Mass of the Gas Giant in Orbit 3 of 123,101**: the game data gave planet 99 a mass of 400000 (4x10^23 tons), from the survey `Research/Data/Starflight_1_Survey.xls`, the source of the planet masses. The original's sensors read 5x10^23 tons (observed 2026-10-10 in the GOG release; atmosphere, hydrosphere, lithosphere and mineral density match the survey, so it is the same planet), while the other eight planets scanned there match the survey. Its mass is now 500000. The sensor picture keeps its size (both masses are of the order of 10^23 tons); its model in orbit and in the star system grows slightly (`GD_Planet.GetScale` scales with mass). Verified with a new `sensorpictures` check that scans the planet's game data through to the end of the readout: the old data reads 4x10^23 and fails, the new reads 5x10^23 and all 66 checks pass.
- **Corrected Mass of the Frozen Planet in Orbit 7 of Arth's System**: the game data gave planet 90 a mass of 40000 (4x10^22 tons); the survey `Research/Data/Starflight_1_Survey.xls`, the source of the planet masses, says 3.6x10^22, its only mass that is not a single digit. The original's sensors read 6x10^21 tons (observed 2026-10-11 in the GOG release; its orbit, gravity of 1.08 g and mineral density of 43 match, and its 26 pixel disc fits 6x10^21 tons). Following the same decision as for planet 99, its mass is now 6000, which shrinks its sensor picture (26 pixels in the original's window instead of the full 62) and its model in orbit and in the star system. Verified with a new `sensorpictures` check that scans the planet's game data through to the end of the readout: the old data reads 4x10^22 and fails, the new reads 6x10^21 and all 67 checks pass.
- **Mechan Scout Model Shown at Full Size**: slot 4 of `Encounter.m_alienShipModelTemplate` in `Spaceflight.unity` pointed at the Mechan Scout FBX asset itself, so encounters cloned it at scale 1: 3.5 units long against the player ship's 51, a speck on screen. The scene already held an unused Mechan Scout template among the other model templates (scale 20, right way up, Mechan hull material); slot 4 now uses it, and the Mechan Scout is 69 units long. Verified with the `shipmodels` probe scenario, which now checks that every vessel's model is at least a quarter of the player ship's length.
- **Prototype Define Set Without Editor Focus**: `SemanticKernelDefineSync` queued its check on `EditorApplication.delayCall`, which runs on the Editor's next update, and a background Editor gets no update until it regains focus: after NuGetForUnity restored the packages in an Editor driven from the Unity command line, `STARFLIGHT_SEMANTIC_KERNEL` was only added once the window was clicked. The check now runs immediately when the Editor loads and whenever the Semantic Kernel packages are imported or removed, and a check that falls in play mode runs again once the Editor is back in edit mode. Verified: with the Windows Editor in the background, the define was removed through the Unity command line; Unity recompiled, and during the following reload `SemanticKernelDefineSync` added the define back and requested the second compile, all without the window being clicked (`Editor.log`). That covers the reload path; the package import path runs the same check but was not tested. The Console does not keep the line, because the second compile clears it.
- **Picked-Up Deposits No Longer Reappear**: each time the terrain vehicle went out, the planet's objects were placed again from its seed, so a picked-up mineral deposit was back, full, the next time (and after loading a game); nothing recorded what had been taken. A deposit is now identified by its planet and placement order, which the seed keeps stable, and the new `PD_PlanetSurfaces` save section records what remains of every deposit something was taken from: one taken whole is gone, one taken in part keeps the remainder. The saved amount is applied after the deposit's random draw, so nothing on any planet moves. Older saves load with an empty record (the save version is unchanged). The artifacts and messages of roadmap phase 3 will be tracked the same way. Verified with the `pickups` probe scenario.
- **Correct Flare Days for Stars That Flared Before the Start**: the original stored a star's flare day as a signed 16 bit number, negative for a star that flared before the game began. The game data holds it unsigned for the 36 stars that had: Earth's sun, at 65476, flared 60 days before the start, and its `m_daysSincePreviousFlare` holds the -60, yet the game read 65476 as a flare 179 years away. `GD_Star.Initialize` now restores the sign, so those suns shine as stable suns (their glow was slightly too large), and the flare check and the analysis of a star yet to flare (roadmap 1.8) get the right day. Also found: for all 234 stars still to flare, the year, month and day fields give the flare day in a calendar of 10 months of 30 days, day 0 = 01-01-4620 (only 18 fit the real-world calendar the port shows). Arth's sun flares on day 300, 01-01-4621 in that calendar; the original checks for a flare as each day ends, so it comes as 30-10-4620, the last day of the year, ends: the Elowan's "final week of your Ten-month". Verified with the `flaredata` probe scenario.
- **Encounter Locations and Orbits Match the Original**: four of the twelve orbit encounters in the game data named an empty orbit, so they could never begin: the Spemin home fleet (139), the Thrynn home fleet (165), and Veloxi drones 302 and 305. A fifth, drone 301, began at the wrong planet. Their orbits are now 6 (139), 4 (165), 5 (302), 1 (305) and 1 (301), as in the original planet records (`Research/Data/Starflight 1 Data.xls`). STRINFO's planet list agrees for all but 301, which it omits: the Spemin and Thrynn homeworlds, Sphexi, and one of the Black Egg's planets. Thrynn scouts 144 to 146 were star system encounters at coordinates with no star, so all three sat at star 0; they are now hyperspace encounters, as in their original records. For saves written before this change, `DataController.LoadPlayerData` moves the scouts from star 0 to hyperspace, keeping their ships and what was said (`PD_Encounter.ValidateLocation`). Orbits need no repair, since they are read from the game data each time. Verified with the `encounterdata` probe scenario.
- **Sensor Pictures for Planets and for Ships Without a Picture**: a planet scan showed a rectangle of static, because the planet slot of `SensorsDisplay.m_maskTextures` in `Spaceflight.unity` held an alien robe texture (`Velox Robe - Albedo.png`) and `Sensors - Planet Mask.png` went unused: the ends of both texture arrays were miswired when `ScanType.Debris` was inserted before `Planet` and `Unknown`. The same mistake put a Spemin warship wreck in the `Unknown` slot, the stand-in for every vessel without its own picture, so 15 of the 23 vessel types (the Elowan, Thrynn, Veloxi, Gazurtoid and Uhlek ships, the Enterprise and the Noah transport) appeared as that wreck, and the `Debris` slot pointed at two assets missing from the repository. The planet slot has its mask again. The `Unknown` slot is now empty, and a scan with no picture leaves the sensor window blank, as the original does for an unidentified object (`Research/Screenshots/Encounters/Scanning/starflt_057.png`); before, a scan type with no picture at all would have kept the last scan's picture on screen. The `Debris` slot names the wreck picture it already showed through the fallback, until wrecks get per-vessel pictures. Readout, analysis and picture scale are unchanged. Verified with Editor screenshots of every scan and with the new `sensorpictures` probe scenario: 3 of its 8 checks fail on the old code, and all pass on the new.
- **Each Wreck Shows Its Own Debris**: scanning any destroyed ship's debris showed a Spemin warship wreck, because the sensor display had a single `Debris` slot. `Assets/Game Objects/UI/Sensors Debris/` holds the debris of eight vessels (the three Spemin ships, the Mechan scout, the Velox drone, the Nomad probe, the Mysterion and the Minstrel), and `SensorsDisplay` now has two arrays indexed by vessel id for them, `m_debrisBackgroundTextures` and `m_debrisMaskTextures`. `Encounter.StartScanning` passes the wreck's vessel id, which `SensorsDisplay.StartScanning` takes as an optional last parameter. A vessel without a debris picture leaves the sensor window empty, like a ship without a picture, and the `Debris` slot of the scan type arrays is empty. Readout and analysis are unchanged. The folder's two other images, `Sensors - Planet_debris` and `Sensors - Spemin Warship_debris_debris`, are unused leftovers from the script that made the debris pictures. Verified with the `sensorpictures` probe scenario, which now also destroys a ship in a real encounter and scans its wreck: 4 of its 12 checks fail on the old code, and all pass on the new.
- **Ship Scans Read Each Vessel's Own Values**: a sensor scan of a ship passed fixed values (a mass of 20 at one power of ten, bio 0, minerals 100), so every ship read 2x10² tons, bio 0, min 100. The original reads the vessel's mass in tons from the game data, plus its bio density and energy (`Research/Screenshots/Ships/`: the Spemin scout reads 4x10² for its mass of 400, the Enterprise 1x10⁴ for 19000, the Nomad probe 2x10¹ for 20, the Minstrel a plain 2 for 2; the Mechan scout reads bio 0 and energy 100, the Minstrel bio 100 and energy 0, as in the game data). `Encounter.StartScanning` now passes the vessel's mass with no extra power of ten and its bio and mineral density, a vessel's second value is labelled Energy, and a mass under ten tons is shown without a power of ten. A wreck now reads its vessel's mass, which had been one power of ten too high (4x10³ for a Spemin scout of 400). Planets read as before, with bio and minerals; the percent signs remain (the original shows none). Verified with the `sensorpictures` probe scenario, which now waits for four scans to finish and reads them: 4 of its 17 checks fail on the old code, and all pass on the new.
- **Alien Comm Progress Kept Across Loads**: `PD_General.m_lastCommIds` was an `int[,]`, which `JsonUtility` does not serialize, so per-race question and answer progress never reached the save file and reset on every load. It is now a flat `int[]` (20 races x 16 subjects) accessed through `GetLastCommId()` and `SetLastCommId()`. Older saves without the field still load (the table is reallocated on first use), and the save version is unchanged.
- **Compiler Errors From the 0.9.0 Bug-Fix Pass**: bounds checks in `AnalysisButton` used `.Count` on the `GD_Vessel[]` vessel list (now `.Length`), and the iteration cap added to the `TradeDepotPanel` selection-box loop left `selectionBoxOffset` not definitely assigned (CS0165; now initialized, behavior unchanged). Found by the new compile check.
- **Playing in the Editor No Longer Modifies `Black.mat`**: the viewport fade wrote its opacity into the shared material asset, so each Play in the Editor left `Assets/Shared/Colors/Black.mat` modified in git. `Viewport` now fades its own copy of the material.
- **Floating Objects No Longer Jump When Their Timer Wraps**: bobbing objects take their height from the sine of a timer, which wrapped at 360 as if it counted degrees. The sine works in radians, where 360 is not a whole number of turns, so the object jumped by most of its range at every wrap (every 360 seconds at a speed of 1). The timer now wraps after a whole turn.
- **Explosions No Longer Cut Off**: an explosion was switched off after 1.5 seconds, but its smoke and debris last two, so they vanished in mid-air. An explosion now stays active until they are gone. Anything waiting on an explosion (the game over screen) is still notified after 1.5 seconds.
- **Probe Scenario `h7` Planted a Ship That Cannot Exist**: the scenario gave a bare hull 400 armor points and expected them back after reloading its save. Since this release's load repair cuts armor back to the ship's maximum (250 for a bare hull), three of its six checks failed. The scenario's ship now has class 1 armor plating, whose 500 points can hold the 400. Found by a run of all fifty scenarios on the final tree; the game was behaving correctly.
- **Probe Scenario `vesselstats` Failed When the Watched Elowan Ship Was a Warship**: the scenario compares the speeds of the first ship in a Spemin and an Elowan encounter and expected 2200 / 1500 (1.47). Each ship's vessel is drawn at random from its encounter's vessels, and the Elowan warship's move delay is 2700, not the 2200 of the transport and scout, so about one run in three measured 1.80 (2700 / 1500), which is correct game behaviour, and failed. Frame timing was not the cause: the Spemin speed was identical in every run. The scenario now expects the ratio of the two watched ships' move delays and measures speed per second of game time (the sum of `Time.deltaTime` over the watched frames) rather than real time, so a slow frame cannot skew it either. The game is unchanged.
- **Probe Scenario `cargo` Failed Intermittently Because Its Deposits Pushed the Terrain Vehicle Away**: the scenario placed each deposit 2 units in front of the terrain vehicle, inside it. Every deposit pushes a terrain vehicle it touches (`TerrainVehicleCollider`), so the vehicle jumped back and forth every other frame, and in some runs (2 of the 7 measured, 2 of about 8 earlier) it kept a push and moved 2 to 8 units, putting the next deposit out of the Cargo button's reach (10) and failing the check "nothing is taken from it with a full hold". The lifeforms were not involved: they have no colliders. The scenarios that place a deposit beside the vehicle (`m10`, `cargo`, `terrain`, `leaks`, `deposits`, `pickups`) now share `BesideTheVehicle`, which puts it 8 units to the right of the vehicle's heading: in reach, yet clear of the vehicle's capsule even for planet 90's largest deposit (Lead, a sphere of radius 3.8), which would still touch it 8 units in front. `cargo` also checks that the vehicle has not moved.
- **Probe Scenario `mates` Failed Intermittently Because Its Prey Decided Before the Hunter Approached**: the scenario's last check puts a small herbivore 25 units from a large carnivore and expects it to flee. All lifeforms decide on the same frame, every half second. In a run where the herbivore decided before the carnivore was coming at it (1 of 13 runs measured for this fix, 1 of 6 before; caused by the order in which Unity updates the two, or by the carnivore unpairing at random, 2% per decision, on the decision that paired it), the herbivore saw nothing coming and wandered off into the air (the test lifeforms fly). The carnivore never approaches a lifeform in the air, so it waited until the herbivore wandered into it, then attacked, and the check failed with the herbivore 11.8 units nearer instead of fleeing. Forcing the herbivore to decide first fails this way every time. The scenario now holds the herbivore's decisions until the carnivore is coming at it, then lets it decide on the next frame. The game is unchanged; that a flying lifeform never lands again, though the 1984 notes have it land, is reported separately.
- **Probe Scenario `flyers` Failed Intermittently Because Its Hunter Wandered Off While the Flyer Was Airborne**: the check that a hunter comes at a flyer once it lands failed in the full run of 2026-10-12 ("Other Wandering | landed 1 times") and passed on two reruns of the same tree. While its prey is airborne a hunter does not approach it (`?MATE-ON-GROUND` is false) but wanders, as in the 1984 notes, and the scenario's carnivore moves 12 units a second: starting 70 units from the terrain vehicle, in one run it was 129 away when the flyer landed 5.5 seconds in. Beyond 150 units it is outside the encounter area and does nothing, so a flyer that landed late (a 20% chance per decision, over dry ground only) found no hunter. Holding the flyer in the air for its first 10 seconds made the old scenario fail the same way (the hunter stopped 152.3 units from the vehicle, then "Other Wandering | landed 4 times"). The scenario now unpairs both lifeforms from any earlier mate and lets the hunter decide once with the flyer airborne (a new control check: paired with it, the hunter does not approach). It then holds the hunter's decisions and movement until the flyer lands, places it 20 units from the flyer towards the vehicle and lets it decide on the next frame, repeating this at the next landing if it unpaired at random on that decision (2% per decision). With the flyer held aloft for 10 seconds the new scenario passed twice (the hunter approached after 11.6 and 15.5 seconds), and it passed all six ordinary runs (the hunter approached 0.5 to 5.5 seconds in). The game is unchanged. The probe README's note on lifeform timing says to set `m_moving` to false when holding a lifeform, and why a hunter must be held while its prey is airborne.
- **Four Probe Scenarios Entered Encounters With No Crew**: the full probe run of 2026-10-12 on `master` (151 scenarios, 906 checks, none failed) logged an exception in `debrispictures` absent from the previous run: an `ArgumentException` in `PD_Personnel.GetPersonnelId`, from `Encounter.AddComm` when the Spemin scouts hailed. Alien speech names the player's captain, and the scenario had never assigned a crew, so the captain's role pointed at no personnel file. Unlike the rest, `h6`, `m15`, `debrispictures` and `shipmodels` entered an encounter without calling `EnsureCrew()`, and threw whenever the aliens happened to hail. A real game cannot get here: the docking bay will not launch until every role is assigned, a dead captain's command passes down the chain of command, and losing the whole crew ends the game. The four scenarios now fill the crew first, and the probe README says any scenario entering an encounter must. With a hail forced in `debrispictures`, the old setup threw every time (no captain assigned) and the new one does not; the four scenarios match their full-run results (in `shipmodels` only the sizes of vessels 20 and 22 differ, which vary between runs), with no failed checks and only the Editor's own startup exception.
- **Flying Lifeforms Land Again**: a flying lifeform took to the air the first time it moved and never came down: `TerrainLifeform.Move` set it flying on every move and nothing cleared the flag, although the comment said it landed when it stopped. A hunter only approaches a mate on the ground (the notes' `?MATE-ON-GROUND`), so a flyer that had moved once was never hunted again. By design decision of 2026-10-12, lifeforms now follow the Air-Ground Transitions of the 1984 notes (`lf-behav.txt`, defined in `lf-words.txt`) after each step of their behaviour. FLY: a moving flyer takes to the air at 75% per step ("a fairly high %"). LAND: an airborne flyer comes down when it wanders, at 20% per step ("low to moderate %"), and when it attacks its mate or the terrain vehicle, if `?OK-TO-LAND` holds (dry ground, with neither another grounded lifeform nor the vehicle within half a square). A dead or stunned flyer drops at once, onto a liquid's surface if it is over one. A grounded flyer also keeps to dry ground, as in the notes ("Aliens in the air do not need to make any of these checks"), and over a liquid a flyer flies above the surface rather than the bottom (`TerrainLifeforms.GetGround` returns the surface height there). FLOAT is not implemented. Verified with the new `flyers` probe scenario, which fails on the old code (5 of 7 checks: no landing, stunned and dead flyers stay airborne, the attacker stays up, no hunter after a flight) and passes on the new; `mates` still passes.
- **Message Box Slide Only Worked for a One-Second Slide**: the slide took its position from the elapsed seconds where it needed the fraction of the slide completed. The two agree only when the slide lasts one second, its current setting; with any other duration the box reached the end of its path early. It now follows the duration.
- **Starfield Wrap Tested the Wrong Edge**: in the starfield's second orientation, which the game does not use, a star leaving through the top was tested against the right edge and was not brought back in at the bottom. It is now tested against the top edge.
- **Experimental Planet Mesh Fixes (Test Scene Only)**: from a resolution of 105 up, the mesh has more than 65535 vertices, and its triangles silently pointed at the wrong ones. A planet with no parent object could not be generated, the mesh stayed in memory after its planet was destroyed, and the adapter failed when switched off without a planet controller. All four are fixed. Only `Test.unity` uses this code.
- **Two Latent Index and Lookup Errors**: the system display placed a planet in its picture by orbit number without a bounds check (every planet in the game data is in orbit 1 to 8; any other is now skipped, as the display's update already did). The planet generator tool's unused `PG_ContractResolver` checked its ignore list when adding an include, so a second include for a type discarded the first, and a type with ignores threw an exception.
- **Editor Tool Fixes**: the planet generator's save functions leaked a texture per saved picture. Cancelling the file dialog in the High Resolution Screenshot and Build Atmosphere Normal Map windows left an empty file name that made the next click throw. Save Texture to File tried to encode unreadable textures and leaked its copy. Mass Create GameObjects threw without a source or parent object and divided by zero when the source had no children; it now reports what is missing. The Starflight shader inspector threw on a cubemap or render texture in a normal map slot and, with several materials selected, updated only the first one's keywords.
- **Landing Site Crosshair Speed Depended on Frame Rate**: the crosshair moved a fixed step per frame, so it crossed the map faster at higher frame rates and accelerated without limit. It now moves by time, at its old 60 frames per second speed, and stops accelerating at the configured but unused top speed (60 degrees per second, reached after two seconds).
- **Wrong Encounter Loaded**: `Radar` keeps `PlayerData.m_encounterList` sorted by distance, but `Encounter.Show` (since b9727c5) picked the encounter by array index, so most encounters loaded another's ships, stance and position and saved kills to the wrong record. Lookup is by `m_encounterId` again, through `PlayerData.FindEncounter()`.
- **Destroyed Encounters Kept Coming Back**: an encounter with every ship destroyed still chased the player, stayed on the radar and restarted empty. `SpaceflightController.UpdateEncounters` now skips encounters with no living ships.
- **Hyperspace Encounters Chased a Freshly Launched Ship**: between launch and Maneuver the ship counted as being in hyperspace, so hyperspace encounters measured their distance to its position inside the Arth system. Those whose coordinates happened to lie nearby left their posts to intercept (nine at the start of a new game, the nearest about 50 seconds away), and one that arrived began an encounter above Starport. A freshly launched ship is no longer pursued.
- **Aliens Appeared on the Wrong Side in Star Systems**: when an encounter begins, the alien ships are placed on the side they approached from. In a star system that side was computed from the ship's last hyperspace position, which is unrelated, so the aliens appeared elsewhere. It now comes from the ship's position in the system.
- **Two Encounters Could Begin in the Same Frame**: two alien groups reaching the ship in the same frame both started encounters, one on top of the other, sounding the alarm and "Scanners indicate unidentified object!" twice and abandoning the first encounter's freshly set up ships. Only one encounter, the nearer, begins per frame now.
- **Radar Was Blind Dead Astern**: the radar compared an encounter's bearing with its sweep by plain subtraction, which breaks where angles wrap from 180 to -180 degrees. An encounter directly behind was never detected, and within 15 degrees to one side of it the sweep had less and less time to catch it. Bearings are now compared the short way around the circle.
- **Alien Comms Missing from Ship's Log After Load**: `PD_ShipsLog.m_alienComms` was a `List<Entry>[]`, which `JsonUtility` cannot serialize, so entries were never saved and the Alien Comms buttons threw a NullReferenceException after a load. It is now an array of serializable `EntryList` wrappers read through `GetAlienComms()`.
- **Save Locked After Deleting Assigned Crew**: deleting a personnel file left crew roles pointing at it, so Crew Assignment and the Docking Bay threw on every open and the ship could never launch. Deletion now unassigns the crew member, and loading repairs affected saves.
- **Starport Panels Stayed Live While Closing**: a second Exit click closed the panel again, so the Trade Depot and Ship Configuration logged their bank transaction twice, and the buttons still worked as the panel slid away. A closing panel now ignores Exit clicks and input, with its controls disabled until it reopens.
- **Shield Outline Surrounds the Whole Ship**: the red shield outline in Ship Configuration and on the status display was drawn for the old model, and the new textured model's weapon mounts (#344) crossed it at top and bottom, badly so in Ship Configuration, where the model is larger. `Shields Outline.png` is redrawn around the fully equipped ship (16 cargo pods, missile launchers and laser) for both views: a box around the rear from engines to mounts, tapering to a rounded nose like the old one, 14 pixels clear of the ship, in the old line's width and red. `DevTools/ShieldOutline/make_outline.py` draws it from ship silhouettes written by the probe. Verified with the new `shieldoutline` and `starport-shieldoutline` probe scenarios (with `-Graphics`): every ship pixel lies inside the outline and clear of its line (36 and 3,203 pixels failed with the old outline).
- **Console Stayed Live During Landing**: Descend did not lock the console, so throughout the 35 second landing animation the stick still moved the selection and the fire button still activated it, and Abort restored the bridge buttons while the ship kept landing. The console now stays locked until touchdown, as during a launch.
- **Save Panel Opened During Launches and Landings**: Escape opened the save panel mid-launch or mid-landing. The game counted as paused, but the camera animation and its events carried on behind the panel, so the player returned to a ship that had already landed. Escape is now ignored during launches and landings, like the other controls.
- **Closing the Save Panel Could Restart a Lost Game**: Escape also opened the save panel during the second and a half of the ship's explosion. The game over screen appeared behind it, and closing the panel resumed the game with the game over text on screen. Escape is now ignored while the ship explodes, and a finished game stays paused.
- **Landing Reported "Safe landing, captain." 23 Seconds Early**: Descend printed its own messages on a timer from the original game's 12 second landing, on top of the 35 second landing animation's, so "Autopilot engaged. Descending..." appeared twice and "Safe landing, captain." at 12 seconds and again at touchdown. The button now prints only "Topography net locked on."; the animation reports the start of the descent and the landing.
- **Trade Depot Sell Exploit**: selling never checked the amount against the hold, so any amount could be sold and the element volume went negative. Overselling now shows an error, and `PD_ElementStorage.Remove` refuses to go below zero.
- **Selling Armor Kept Its Armor Points**: selling armor reset the class but not the points, so buying class 5 armor and selling it straight back left an unarmored ship with 1,500 armor points for a net 1,200 MU. Selling armor now drops the ship to the bare hull's 250 points, or fewer if damaged.
- **Old Saves Could Exceed Their Armor's Maximum**: a save made before the armor sale fix could hold, say, 1,500 armor points on an unarmored ship, and the Damage report read "Hull: 600% (1500/250)". Loading now trims armor points to the ship's maximum (`PD_PlayerShip.ValidateArmorPoints`, alongside the other load repairs in `DataController.LoadPlayerData`). It only ever removes points, so a save written with a destroyed ship still loads with 1 point.
- **New Ship Showed "83% Hull Damage"**: the status display measured armor against 1,500 points and shields against 2,500, the best equipment available, so an undamaged new ship read "83% Hull Damage" and its gauges filled only with class 5 equipment. The Damage report and Repair button instead took the maximum from the armor plating, 0 without any, so the bare hull (250 points) showed as "None installed" and could not be repaired. A single source now gives the maximum armor and shield points (`PD_PlayerShip.GetMaximumArmorPoints()` and `GetMaximumShieldPoints()`): the installed armor's points, or the bare hull's 250. The damage text, both gauges, the Damage report and the Repair button all use it, the engineer repairs a bare hull ("Beginning repairs on the hull, Captain."), and "Hull breach imminent" triggers below a quarter of that maximum instead of a fixed 250 points.
- **Trade Depot Amount Parsing**: the decimal part was added as a raw integer (`5.25` became 7.5) and a lone `-` threw `FormatException`. Amounts now parse as tenths with the invariant culture, accept a decimal comma, and reject signs.
- **Cargo Pods Could Be Sold Out From Under Their Cargo**: a pod could be sold while the hold needed its space, leaving negative free volume that slipped past the Trade Depot's "hold is full" check. A pod now sells only if the cargo fits without it ("Unload cargo first"), and negative free volume counts as full.
- **Mineral Deposits Could Be Picked Up Twice**: a deposit stays on the surface during its 1.5 second transporter effect and could be picked up again then, so pressing Cargo twice put it in the hold twice. It can now be picked up only once.
- **Random Events Repeated After Every Terrain Vehicle Trip**: a planet's rocks, deposits and trees are placed with random numbers seeded from the planet, so they always land in the same places. This left the game's random numbers at the same point after every disembarkation on a given planet, and everything random afterwards (alien speech and actions, where their ships appear) repeated identically. The random state is now restored after placement.
- **Scans Counted Deposits Being Picked Up**: during a deposit's 1.5 second transporter effect, a scan still listed it and restored its label. Scans now leave it out.
- **Scan Labels Lingered After Leaving the Terrain Vehicle**: scan labels on rocks and deposits are not part of those objects, so they hung in the air after the player returned to the ship, their 30 second countdown frozen. They are now hidden with their objects.
