- **Crater Maps Reloaded on Every Spaceflight Scene Start**: reading the three textures (about six million pixels) cost 0.45 seconds whenever the scene started (returning from Starport, after a game over). They are now read once per session.
- **Displays Allocated Memory Every Frame**: the status and terrain vehicle displays rebuilt their text piece by piece every frame, changed or not, leaving about 900 and 500 bytes of garbage per frame. The docking bay fetched the astronaut's materials nine times a frame while he faded out (about 570 bytes a frame). Texts are now rebuilt only when what they show changes, and the materials are fetched once.
- **Stardate Texts Rebuilt Every Frame**: the game time rebuilt both stardate strings every frame of flight (112 bytes of garbage per frame), though they show only the day and the hour. They are now rebuilt when either changes, and once after a load.
- **Spaceflight Scene Stayed in Memory After Leaving It**: three references kept it alive: the spaceflight controller's never-cleared static instance, the save panel's memory of who last opened it, and the placement code's memory of the terrain vehicle's last planet. Through them every scene object and its planets' height maps stayed in memory at Starport and on the title screen (about 40 MB in a test). All three are released now.
- **Collected Deposits Leaked Their Materials**: a collected deposit's transporter effect copied the deposit's materials plus one for its sparkles, and none were destroyed with it: three leaked materials per pickup until the next scene change. They are now destroyed with the effect.
- **Log Opened Scrolled Down After an Empty Log**: the ship's log kept the previous log's scroll position. That was normally corrected at once, but not after an empty log, so the next log opened scrolled down, its selected first entry out of sight, until the stick moved. A log now always opens at its first entry.
- **Ship's Log Scroll Loops Have a Limit**: the two loops that scroll a log's list to its selected entry had no pass limit. Each pass scrolls one row, so they are now capped at one pass more than the log's entry count. The hang the code review suspected does not occur in play (shown on 2026-10-04), but in a hand-made state (list scrolled down, zero-height rows) the old loops never ended and the new ones exit at once.
- **Objects on the Exact Edge of the Map Had No List**: rock, deposit and tree placement tracks positions in 32 lists across the map, and a position exactly on the right edge mapped to a nonexistent 33rd list, so placing it failed. It takes the random number generator returning its largest value, so it is rare, and no planet is known to be affected. Such positions now go into the last list.
- **Cargo Volumes Shown Ten Times Too Large**: cargo holds count in tenths of a cubic meter, and several texts printed that as cubic meters: the ship's cargo list showed 200 for 20.0 cubic meters of Endurium, the terrain vehicle said "Picked up 3 cubic meters" for a 0.3 deposit, and its cargo list read "Capacity: 3/500 m³" for 0.3 of 50.0. All now show cubic meters; amounts picked up are unchanged.
- **Deposits Too Large for the Hold Vanished**: with room for only part of a deposit, the terrain vehicle took that part and the whole deposit disappeared. The rest now stays on the ground, and the message says how much is left.
- **Bank Ledger Showed Income as "-1400+"**: leaving the Trade Depot or Ship Configuration richer than before logged the amount with its minus sign and a trailing plus. It now reads "1400+", matching spending's "500-".
- **Buy Maximum Overflowed Above 214 Million MU**: the Trade Depot computed the most the player could afford by multiplying the balance by ten in 32 bits, which went negative at 300,000,000 MU. It now uses 64 bits.
- **Sensor Analysis Said "0 times the size of our ship"**: a scanned vessel's size is its mass divided by ours, and the division truncated the fraction, so a vessel half our size read 0 times and one 8.9 times read 8. It now shows one decimal.
- **Garbled Alien Words Dropped**: 70445da left the capitalization `if` without a body, so it guarded adding the word and most garbled words vanished from alien messages. The capitalization block is restored.
- **Alien Messages Showed a `*` Instead of the Captain's Name**: the game data marks the captain's name with `*` and the ship's with `&`. These were filled in for player transmissions, but alien speech was rebuilt from the raw text, so the Spemin asked about "the necessities of self defense captain *". The eight alien lines with the captain's name and the one with the ship's now show them. Alien captain and ship names (`%` and `+`, in 15 alien greetings) remain unfilled, as the game has no names for them.
- **Mechan 9 Could Be Unlocked After Firing on the Mechans**: five right answers to the Mechans' questions permanently unlock Mechan 9, even in an encounter where the player had fired on them. That encounter stayed hostile, as usual until the player leaves, but every later Mechan encounter began friendly. Right answers no longer unlock Mechan 9 in an encounter where the player has fired on the Mechans.
- **Button Presses Could Land on a Different Button**: the fire button activates the selected button 0.35 seconds after the press. If the buttons changed in between, for example when the aliens asked a question, the press hit the first button of the new set, so a press on Statement could answer Yes to a question not yet read. A press is now dropped if its button is no longer selected.
- **Statement Button Could Transmit "ERROR"**: the game data has statements for the friendly, hostile and obsequious postures but none for neutral, so a neutral statement transmitted the word ERROR. It now transmits nothing. The console cannot currently get there (the comm buttons follow a hail, which sets a posture); this is a guard.
- **Missile Immunity Never Loaded**: the game data key was spelled `m_immuneToMissles`, so `GD_Vessel.m_immuneToMissiles` was always false. The key is renamed (no values changed), making Gazurtoid ships and the Mysterion immune again.
- **Missile Launchers Could Never Fire**: the missile count was only ever decremented, never stocked, so a purchased launcher always answered "Out of missiles!". Missiles are no longer counted: each launch uses 0.02 cubic meters of Endurium and each laser shot 0.01, and neither weapon fires without Endurium.
- **Most Alien Ships Scanned as "Unknown"**: the sensor display lacks pictures for 15 of the 23 vessel types, and a missing picture also turned the scan type into Unknown, so scanning an Elowan, Thrynn, Veloxi, Gazurtoid or Uhlek ship (or debris) showed "Unknown object detected" and an analysis of "Insufficient data". Now only the picture falls back; the readout and analysis describe the real ship.
- **Training Blocked Once Medicine Was Maxed**: the Train check compared only the last skill; it now compares totals across all skills.
- **Repair and Treat Were Instant and Free**: one press of Repair restored five armor points per point of engineering skill on the spot, and Treat healed every hired person at once, even those left at Starport. Both now take time: the engineer repairs armor at 0.02 points per second per skill point (minimum 0.5), the doctor treats the most injured living crew member aboard at the same rate, one patient per command, and both report when done. Pressing the button again shows progress and time remaining.
- **Planet Gravity Lost a Zero**: gravity is stored in hundredths of a G, and the text dropped the fraction's leading zero, so 105 read "1.5 G" instead of "1.05 G" and 5 read "0.5 G" instead of "0.05 G" (105 of the 811 planets). The fraction now always has two digits.
- **Entering a Star System Froze the Game**: planet maps are computed on a background thread, but the main thread waited for it, freezing the game for as long as each planet took and moving the "Commencing System Penetration" bar only once per planet. The main thread now checks each frame and carries on once the planet is done, so the game keeps running and the bar advances while each planet is processed.
- **Planet Colours Blurred to One Side Only**: an index slip in the colour map filter's small blur made every pixel take a quarter from its right neighbour and nothing from its left; it now takes a quarter from each side. A second, row-to-row blur built a 32 MB map per planet and then discarded it; it is removed, and none of its work ever reached the screen. On planet 90 the fix changes 7% of the pixels, by 1.4 of 255 on average and at most 64, at the edges between colours.
- **Planet Generator Tool Padded the South Pole With North Pole Heights**: the tool pads each planet's 24 rows of source data above and below, blending to one height per pole, but for the south pole it read the bottom row's colours with the top row's heights. It now reads the bottom row. Only regenerated planets are affected: the project's 811 planet files were made with the old code and are untouched. For 273 of them the south pole would come out at a different height, by a fifth of the height range at the median.
- **Planet Generator Tool Could Hang or Fill Planets With NaN**: any exception left the tool's progress bar up, so the Editor looked hung (a wrong game data file name was enough). With Evaporation Constant at 0 an erosion rain drop took 25 times as long to end, and with Friction Constant also at 0 it never ended. Mountain Scale or Lacunarity at 0, or a Final Blur Radius of 0, divided by zero and filled the height map with NaN. The tool now validates its settings first and says what is wrong, always clears its progress bar, caps each drop at twice the steps its water can last, and treats a blur radius of 0 as no blur. Texture Map Height must be 1024, the only size the game can read.
- **Planet Generator Tool Gave a Different Planet Every Run**: the erosion pass rained on eight parts of the map at once, on eight threads writing to one map without a lock. A drop can run out of its own part, so updates were occasionally lost: six runs on one map gave six results. The parts are now rained on in turn on one thread, so the same map and settings always give the same result, at about five times the cost (5.2 s versus 1.0 s for a 256 by 128 map on a 16 processor machine). **No planet file was regenerated**: the project's 811 files are unchanged.
- **Damaged Planet File Locked the Game**: an unreadable planet data file (damaged, or from another version of the planet generator) raised its error on the main thread every frame, leaving the game paused in "Commencing System Penetration" for good, and since entering the system had already saved, loading led straight back there. A file cut off inside its last part raised no error and left part of the surface data empty. Unreadable files are now skipped with an error in the log while the system's other planets generate as usual. The reader checks the version, the map sizes, that all data is present with nothing extra, and the checksum; all 811 planet files in the project pass.
- **Landing on a Planet That Could Not Be Generated**: a planet whose maps cannot be generated (data file missing or damaged) has no elevation data, and Descend hit an exception after "Computing descent profile...". Land now refuses such a planet ("We can't land here. The surface of this planet could not be mapped."), as does Disembark for a saved game already on its surface.
- **Ungenerated Planets Wore Another Planet's Maps**: a planet whose maps cannot be generated (data file missing or damaged) kept whatever its material held: the maps of the planet in the same orbit of the previous system. It now appears as a plain grey ball (four plain 4 by 4 pixel maps, no surface detail), and the player is told "The surface of this planet could not be mapped." on reaching orbital range and on entering orbit. Clouds and atmosphere still show, as they come from the game data, not the file. **Not seen**: the probe has no graphics, so the look itself was not inspected.
- **Planet Maps Were Never Freed**: each planet's texture maps are built when the ship enters a system, and the previous system's maps stayed in memory with the planet files they came from. Loading the next scene (docking at Starport) cleared most of them, but not the last system's. Four systems took runtime-created textures from 22 MB to 69 MB in a headless run, which has no graphics memory to count. A planet's maps are now destroyed once its replacement's maps are on its model, or at once if its orbit is empty in the new system or the scene is left, and a planet file is unloaded once its data is copied.
- **Shields Could Be Raised With No Fuel**: shields could go up without Endurium, and the hourly fuel use then threw a NullReferenceException. Raising shields now requires Endurium.
- **Lowering and Raising Shields Refilled Them**: lowering the shields discarded their charge and raising them refilled it, so two presses mid-fight fully restored them. Shields now keep their charge when lowered, absorb damage only while up, and regain 1% of full charge every 5 seconds (the rate in the original design notes). Starport fully recharges them while docked, and a save made with shields down gets a one-time full charge.
- **Progress Lost on Quit**: the game saved only on location changes and panel closes; it now also saves on quit, unless the ship has been destroyed.
- **Games Saved in the Terrain Vehicle Loaded Into Starport**: the game saves on entering the terrain vehicle, but the list that maps saved locations to scenes lacked that location, so continuing opened the Starport scene; it now opens Spaceflight. The save panel lacked it too and ran the ship line into the location line; it now says "Terrain Vehicle".
- **Terrain Vehicle Saves Stuck When Their Planet File Broke**: a game saved in the terrain vehicle and loaded after its planet's data file was damaged had no ground, no way back to the ship, and threw an exception every frame (61 in the first second). Such a game now returns to orbit on load: the vehicle comes aboard with its cargo, the ship is put in orbit, the game saves there, and the player is told why.
- **Stardate Depended on the Computer's Calendar**: the stardate used the regional calendar, so the Thai calendar gave the year 5163 instead of 4620, and with the Saudi Arabian calendar, which has no year 4620, computing the game time failed on every frame of spaceflight. The stardate now always uses the same calendar.
- **Bank and Notice Dates Depended on the Computer's Region**: the bank's date column, "Today is" and notice dates in Operations, and a notice's date and heading in the ship's log used the regional date format, so the Thai calendar showed 5163 and the order of day and month varied by region. These are stardates and now appear as the game shows stardates: day-month-year, `26-03-4620`, as on the original game's bank and notice screens (`PD_General.GetDisplayStardate`). Since the ship's log lives in the save, notices dated by an older build are redated on load.
- **New Game Terrain Vehicle Was Never Initialized**: a new game created its terrain vehicle without resetting it, so it started with no fuel or cargo holds, working only because disembarking refuels it and the first pickup creates the hold. New games now reset it like everything else.
- **Game Over Put the Player Back in the Lost Fight**: after the ship was destroyed, the title screen kept the lost game in memory, so continuing dropped the player back into the encounter with no armor and the aliens still hostile, and closing the save panel during the explosion wrote the destroyed ship to the slot. The title screen now reloads the slot's last save (made when the encounter began), and a destroyed ship is never saved, copied to another slot or carried through a slot switch. Older saves holding a destroyed ship load with 1 armor point so they can be saved again.
- **Crash While Saving Could Wipe the Slot**: saves overwrote the old file directly, and an unreadable save was silently replaced by a new game and then overwritten. Saves are now written to a temporary file and swapped in, the replaced save is kept as `.bak`, loading falls back to it, and an unreadable save is moved to `.corrupt` so nothing overwrites it.
- **Debris and Missiles Used the Wrong Alien Ship Model**: combat used a ship's index in the encounter's ship list as its model slot, but slots are packed for living ships only. After re-entering an encounter with ships already destroyed, a kill spawned debris in another ship's slot or an empty one, and an empty slot made `Encounter.Update` throw every frame, trapping the player. `Encounter` now maps ships to slots behind `GetAlienShipModel()`, used for debris and missile homing.
- **Aliens Never Fought Back**: only the Mechans ever turned hostile, firing on a ship changed nothing, and the Uhlek had no update at all, so their 67 encounters never acted. A laser shot or missile launch at any alien now makes that encounter hostile until the player leaves, the Uhlek attack on sight and never talk, and a race without its own update (the Enterprise) shoots back once fired upon.
- **Aliens Kept Firing During a Comm Link**: hostile aliens kept shooting while the two sides talked on the comm screen. They now hold fire while the link is up, with the timer to their next shot paused, and the fight resumes when it ends. This is the original design: every alien fire rule in the design notes requires `?IN-COMM FALSE` (`Research/Notes/communic.txt`). The check is in `Encounter.UpdateAlienCombat`, the single path for all alien fire, so it covers every race.
- **Destroyed Ships Kept Flying**: destroyed ships were still moved every frame, so wreckage chased the player. Only vessels 1 to 4 and 20 have a debris model, so destroyed Elowan, Thrynn, Veloxi, Gazurtoid and Uhlek ships also kept their normal model and looked intact. Wrecks now stay where they died, ships without a debris model vanish with their explosion, and the encounter camera frames only living ships.
- **Combat Target Carried Over Between Encounters**: the selected target was never cleared and firing only range-checked the index, so a stale target from the previous encounter could be dead or not yet present, letting the player destroy a ship that was not on screen. The target is now cleared on entering an encounter, and firing at anything but a living ship in the encounter clears it and says so.
- **Unity Object Checks Missed Destroyed Objects**: four places (the Target button, the F10 debug key, the mineral and artifact pickups) tested Unity objects with `??` or `?.`, which miss destroyed objects, and the terrain vehicle's collider compared tags with `==` and read a collision's first contact point without checking one existed. They now use `== null`, `CompareTag` and a contact count check, as the project rules require. Nothing changes for the player.
- **Missiles Carried Over Between Encounters**: a missile in flight when the player left an encounter kept going and still did damage on arrival: an alien missile could hit the player in the next encounter, and a player missile damaged the ship with the same number there. All missiles are now cleared when an encounter ends and when one begins.
- **Ship Could Be Destroyed More Than Once**: a hit on an already exploding ship destroyed it again, with another wreck, another explosion and another game over call; the aliens' own fire did this during the 1.5 seconds of the explosion. The ship is now destroyed once: missiles in flight are cleared, the aliens stop firing at the wreck, and its weapons do not fire.
- **Missiles Kept Flying While the Game Was Paused**: missiles kept flying behind the save panel, the starmap and the ship's log, and did their damage there. They now stand still while the game is paused and fly on afterwards.
- **Expired Missiles Just Vanished**: a missile that had not reached its target after five seconds was silently switched off. It now reports a miss to its launcher, as the handling code always expected: a small explosion where it was, its sound, and no damage, for player and alien missiles alike.
- **Explosions Stayed at Half Size**: a missile hit plays its explosion at half size, and the explosion kept that size when reused, so the next destroyed ship could go up in a half-size blast. Every explosion now starts at full size.
- **Launch With Every Missile Airborne Cost Fuel and Launched Nothing**: only eight missiles, the aliens' included, can be in the air at once. With all eight airborne, a launch still spent its Endurium, made the aliens hostile and said "Missile launched!" but launched nothing. Such a launch now does nothing and costs nothing.
- **Alien Ships Had No Hit Points**: damage to alien ships was computed from scratch per hit and never stored, so a hit either destroyed a ship outright or did nothing (a class 1 laser could never destroy a Spemin Warship, yet one-shot an Elowan). Alien ships now have armor and shield points, 100 per vessel class, saved with the game; shields absorb damage first. Ships in older saves get their points on the first hit.
- **Spemin Slime Drips No Longer Jump**: the rest of the Spemin's new comm portrait moves smoothly with the frame rate, but its drips changed shape in a single frame twice per fall: a drip letting go of the ceiling halved in length between frames, and a landing drip went from 4.8 to 0.3 tall at once (its full-width splash also vanished in one frame when the cycle restarted). A hanging drip now stretches to exactly its falling shape, a landing drip's bottom stops on the goo while its top keeps descending at falling speed until flat, and the splash spreads to almost twice the drip's size, then sinks away, slowing to nothing, to a new drip's starting size (`SpeminCommAnimation.UpdateDrips`). The `commspemin` probe scenario now also watches the drips for 5 s at a steady 30 frames per second: no frame's change in a drip's height or width may differ from the changes in the frames on either side by more than 0.3 of the drip's size. The old drips fail at 2.8 sizes; the new ones pass at 0.11.
- **Removed References to Assets Never in the Repository**: `Spaceflight.unity` referenced seven assets missing from the project (their GUIDs resolve to nothing): five material overrides on the Mechan and Endurium model instances, the material of the terrain vehicle's Water Ripples particles, and the emitter mesh of the hyperspace flux template's particles. None was ever visible: the overrides targeted renderers the models no longer have, or a second material slot the Endurium crystal lacks, so every part already drew with its own material; Water Ripples is disabled and nothing enables it; and the flux emitter's circle shape never reads the mesh. The overrides are removed and the two references cleared, so the Inspector no longer flags them as missing. An editor report of the scene (every unresolvable object reference, plus each Mechan and Endurium part's final material) shows seven before and none after, with unchanged materials on every part.
- **Planet Pages Admin Now Requires a Login**: `Planets/index.php`, the original author's page collecting the community's planet screenshots, deleted a screenshot whenever its admin opened a plain link (`index.php?remove=N`), checking only that the request came from a fixed address, so any page the admin visited, or anyone else at that address, could delete the whole collection; `convert.php` ran for anyone. Found in a security review of the repository (cross-site request forgery, CWE-352, low severity). The admin now logs in with a password checked against a hash in the git-ignored `Planets/admin-config.php` (`admin-config.example.php` explains how to create it; without it nobody can log in), in a session with an HttpOnly, SameSite=Lax cookie and a new id at login (`admin-session.php`). Removing a screenshot is a POST carrying the session token, and `convert.php` runs only for the logged-in admin, on a POST with the token. Uploader names in the list are now escaped, since the token appears on that page. The public upload is unchanged. Verified with PHP 8.3's built-in server and a router making every request appear to come from the old admin address: the old pages fail the attack checks (a link deletes a screenshot, `convert.php` runs without a login), and the new pages pass all 20 checks covering the attacks, login, removal, conversion, upload, logout and an unconfigured login.

## [0.9.0] - 2026-01-04

### Added

#### Combat
- **Player Ship Destruction and Game Over**: the full death sequence for the player's ship, with explosion effects, debris and a game over screen.
- **Debris System**: a new `DebrisTumble.cs` component gives the wreckage of destroyed ships tumbling physics.
- **Victory Detection**: `AllEnemyShipsDestroyed()` reports when every enemy ship in an encounter has been destroyed.
- **Debris Scanning**: debris fields left after a ship is destroyed can be scanned for salvage.
- **Leaving After Victory**: players can now leave an encounter once every enemy ship is destroyed.

#### Encounters
- **Alien Stance Tracking**: a hostile stance now persists throughout a combat encounter.
- **Ship Model Validation**: ship model templates are bounds-checked, with errors logged.
- **Debris Spawning**: `SpawnDebrisForShip()` creates debris where a ship was destroyed.

#### Sensors
- **Debris Scan Type**: the new `ScanType.Debris` (index 24) scans the wreckage of destroyed vessels.
- **Fallback Textures**: stricter bounds checks on the texture arrays, falling back to the Unknown type.
- **Debris Analysis**: the Analysis button now describes debris when wreckage has been scanned.

#### Terrain and Exploration
- **TerrainArtifact.cs**: a new component that places artifacts on planet surfaces.
- **TerrainRuins.cs**: a new component for ancient ruin sites on planets.

#### Debug Features
- **F9 Test Encounter**: spawns a hostile Spemin encounter for combat testing.
- **F10 Instant Death**: destroys the player's ship to test the game over flow.
- **Extended Logging**: debug output for stance changes, combat events and debris spawning.

### Fixed

#### Critical
- **Escape on Game Over**: now returns to the Intro scene instead of loading the saved game.
- **NullReferenceException in PD_ShipsLog**: added null checks for the `m_alienComms` array.
- **Stuck in an Encounter**: the player could not leave an encounter after destroying every enemy.
- **Hostile Stance Reset**: the alien stance no longer resets in the middle of combat.

#### Combat
- **Enemy Ships Not Firing**: `UpdateHostileFire()` now iterates over the alien ships correctly.
- **Weapon Arming**: the weapon arm and disarm state is now managed correctly during encounters.
- **Damage Application**: corrected the damage calculation for shields and armor.

#### Displays
- **Sensor Display Bounds**: added bounds checks for the background and mask texture arrays.
- **Status Display Updates**: the status display now updates in real time during combat.

### Changed

- **RestartGame()**: now loads the Intro scene instead of Persistent, for a clean restart.
- **SpaceflightController**: refactored the game over and restart logic.
- **CombatController**: improved object pooling for combat effects.
- **Encounter.cs**: a major refactoring of combat behaviour and stance management (about 277 lines added).

### Technical Notes

- All changes tested with Unity 6 (6000.3.2f1).
- No breaking changes to the save file format.
- Older saves without the `m_alienComms` array still load.

---

### Added
- The first combat system.
- The alien encounter framework for all 10 races.
- The planet generation system.
- Starport operations.
